## XXVIII A Chapter on Unicorn-Hunting / 二十八 论猎独角兽之一章

===Original===
None in Erl was so busy but that he came that morning to see the magic that was newly come out of Elfland, and to compare the trolls with all that the neighbours said of them. And the folk of Erl gazed much at the trolls and the trolls at the folk of Erl, and there was great merriment; for, as often happens with minds of unequal weight, each laughed at the other. And the villagers found the impudent ways of the bare brown nimble trolls no funnier, no more meet for derision, than the trolls found the grave high hats, the curious clothes, and the solemn air of the villagers.

And Orion soon came too, and the folk of the village doffed their long thin hats; and, though the trolls would have laughed at him also, Lurulu had found his whip, and by means of it made the mob of his impudent brethren give that salutation that is given in Elfland to those of its royal line.

===Chinese===
厄尔这地方，再忙的人那天早上也赶来看刚从精灵国出来的那桩魔法，并将那些巨怪与邻里间所传的种种两相比较。于是厄尔之民大大地端详那些巨怪，巨怪也大大地端详厄尔之民，一片欢腾；因为，正如常发生在轻重不均的心智之间的情形，彼此都笑对方。村民们觉得那些光着身子、棕褐肤色、身手敏捷的巨怪那副放肆作派并不更滑稽、也并不更该受嗤笑，正如巨怪们看村民那些高得古怪的高帽、稀奇的衣裳和庄重的神气，也觉得不过如此。

不久奥利安（Orion）也来了，村人纷纷摘下他们又长又薄的帽子；巨怪们本也想笑他，但卢鲁鲁已找回了他的鞭子，借它使他那一群放肆的弟兄行了在精灵国中向王室血脉所行之礼。

===Original===
When noonday came, which was the hour of dinner, and the folk turned from the kennels, they went back to their houses all praising the magic that was come at last to Erl.

During the days that followed Orion's hounds learned that it was vain to chase a troll and unwise to snarl at one; for, apart from their elvish speed, the trolls were able to leap into the air far over the heads of the hounds, and when each had been given a whip they could repay snarling with an aim that none on Earth was able to equal, except those whose sires had carried a whip with hounds for generations.

===Chinese===
到了正午是用饭的时辰，众人离开犬舍回去，一路上无不称颂那终于降临厄尔的魔法。

其后数日间，奥利安的群犬渐渐明白：追赶一个巨怪是白费气力，朝一个巨怪龇牙是不智之举；因为撇开它们精灵般的速度不谈，巨怪们能纵身跃起，远远高过群犬的头顶；而一旦每只巨怪都配上了鞭子，它们回敬龇牙时的准头，除了那些世代执鞭率犬之人的后裔，尘世间无人能及。

===Original===
And one morning Orion came to the pigeon-loft and called to Lurulu early, and he brought out the trolls and they went to the kennels and Orion opened the doors, and he led them all away eastwards over the downs. The hounds moved all together and the trolls with their whips ran beside them, like a flock of sheep surrounded by numbers of collies. They were away to the border of Elfland to wait for the unicorns where they come through the twilight to eat the earthly grasses at evening. And as our evening began to mellow the fields we know, they were come to the opal border that shut those fields from Elfland. And there they lurked as Earth's darkness grew, and waited for the great unicorns. Each hound had its troll beside it with the troll's right hand along its shoulder or neck, soothing it, calming it, and holding it still, while the left hand held the whip: the strange group lingered there motionless, and darkened there with the evening. And when Earth was as dim and quiet as the unicorns desired the great creatures came softly through, and were far into Earth before any troll would allow his hound to move. Thus when Orion gave the signal they easily cut one off from its elfin home and hunted it snorting over those fields that are the portion of men. And night came down on the proud beast's magical gallop, and the hounds intoxicate with that marvellous scent, and the leaping soaring trolls.

===Chinese===
一天早晨，奥利安来到鸽笼前早早唤了卢鲁鲁，他领着众巨怪出来，他们走到犬舍，奥利安打开舍门，领着他们全体东行，越过丘陵。群犬齐齐地跑动，执鞭的巨怪们跑在它们身旁，犹如一群被许多牧羊犬团团围住的羊。他们是往精灵国的边境去，在那里等候独角兽于薄暮时分穿过暮霭来吃人间的青草。当我们所知的原野上的傍晚开始变得柔和，他们已抵达那道将原野与精灵国隔开的蛋白石色边境。他们在那里潜伏着，随着人间的黑暗渐浓，等待着那些巨大的独角兽。每一只猎犬身旁都伴着一只巨怪，巨怪的右手搭在它的肩上或颈上，抚慰它、安抚它、使它静止，左手则握着鞭子：这一群古怪的组合一动不动地滞留在那里，随傍晚一同暗下去。当人间变得如独角兽所愿的那般昏暗而寂静时，那些巨大的生灵轻轻地穿过来了，并且已深入人间腹地，才有巨怪允许他的猎犬动弹。于是当奥利安发出信号，他们轻而易举地切断了一只独角兽回归精灵故乡的退路，将它追逐得喷着响鼻，奔过那些属于人类所分得的田地。夜色降临在那头高傲之兽的神奇飞奔之上，降临在为那奇异的气味所醉的群犬之上，降临在那些腾跃纵跳的巨怪之上。

===Original===
And, when jackdaws on the highest towers of Erl saw the rim of the sun all red above frosted fields, Orion came back from the downs with his hounds and his trolls, carrying as fine a head as a unicorn-hunter could wish. The hounds weary but glad were soon curled up in their kennels, and Orion in his bed; while the trolls in their pigeon-loft began to feel, as none but Lurulu had felt ever before, the weight and the weariness of the passing of time.

All day Orion slept and all his hounds, none of them caring how it slept or why; while the trolls slept anxiously, falling asleep as fast as ever they could, in the hope of escaping some of the fury of time, which they feared had begun to attack them. And that evening while still they slept, hounds, trolls and Orion, there met again in the forge of Narl the parliament of Erl.

===Chinese===
当厄尔最高塔楼上的寒鸦看见落日如一轮红边浮于凝霜的田野之上时，奥利安带着他的群犬与巨怪从丘陵归来，带回了一颗独角兽猎人所敢奢望的最漂亮的首级。疲惫却快乐的群犬很快便蜷进了犬舍，奥利安则蜷进了自己的床；而鸽笼里的巨怪们则头一回开始感觉到——除了卢鲁鲁谁也不曾有过的那种感觉——时光流逝之重与倦。

整整一天奥利安都在睡，他所有的猎犬也在睡，它们全不在乎自己是如何睡、又为何而睡；巨怪们却睡得忧心忡忡，竭尽全力尽快入睡，指望借此逃脱一部分他们唯恐已开始袭击他们的时光之怒。那天傍晚，群犬、巨怪与奥利安尚在酣睡之际，厄尔议会的人又在纳尔（Narl）的铁匠铺里聚首了。

===Original===
From the forge to the inner room came the twelve old men, rubbing their hands and smiling, ruddy with health and the keen north wind and the cheerfulness of their forebodings; for they were well content at last that their lord was surely magic, and foresaw great doings in Erl.

"Folklings," said Narl to them all, naming them thus after an ancient wont, "is it not well with us and our valley at last? See how it is as we planned so long ago. For our lord is a magic lord as we all desired, and magical things have sought him from over there, and they all obey his hests."

"It is so," said all but Gazic, a vendor of beeves.

===Chinese===
从铁匠铺进入内室来了十二位老人，搓着手、含着笑，健康、凛冽的北风与他们对前景的乐观使他们面色红润；因为他们终于心满意足：他们的领主确实是通魔法的，而且他们预见厄尔将有大作为。

「乡亲们，」纳尔对他们众人说，这是按一种古老的旧习这样称呼他们，「如今我们与我们的山谷终于好起来了吧？看看，事情正如我们许久以前所谋划的那样。因为我们的领主正如我们所愿是一位通魔法的领主，而神奇之物已从那边来寻他，并且统统听他号令。」

「正是如此。」除卖牛肉的加齐克（Gazic）外，众人皆如是说。

===Original===
Little and old and out-of-the-way was Erl, secluded in its deep valley, unnoticed in history; and the twelve men loved the place and would have it famous. And now they rejoiced as they heard the words of Narl, "What other village," he said, "has traffic with over there?"

And Gazic, though he rejoiced with the rest, rose up in a pause of their gladness. "Many strange things," he said, "have entered our village, coming from over there. And it may be that human folk are best, and the ways of the fields we know."

Oth scorned him, and Threl. "Magic is best," said all.

And Gazic was silent again, and raised his voice no more against the many; and the mead went round, and all spoke of the fame of Erl; and Gazic forgot his mood and the fear that was in it.

===Chinese===
厄尔又小又古老又偏僻，深藏于它那幽谷之中，在历史上默默无闻；这十二个人爱这地方，巴不得它名扬四海。如今他们听见纳尔的话便欢喜起来：「哪个村子，」他说，「能与那边有往来？」

加齐克虽与其余的人一同欢喜，却在他们欢欣的一个间歇站了起来。「许多奇怪的东西，」他说，「从那边进入了我们的村子。可也许人之本身才是最好的，以及我们所知的那些原野之道。」

奥斯（Oth）嗤笑他，斯瑞尔（Threl）也嗤笑他。「魔法才是最好的。」众人皆说。

加齐克便又沉默了，不再向多数人提高嗓门；蜂蜜酒传了一圈又一圈，众人皆谈论起厄尔的名声；加齐克便忘了他的那番心绪，以及其中的恐惧。

===Original===
Far into the night they rejoiced, quaffing the mead, and by its homely aid gazing into the years of the future, so far as that may be done by the eyes of men. Yet all their rejoicing was hushed and their voices low, lest the ears of the Freer should hear them; for their gladness came to them from lands that lay beyond thought of salvation, and they had set their trust in magic, against which, as well they knew, boomed every note that rang from the bell of the Freer whenever it tolled at evening. And they parted late, praising magic in no loud tones, and went secretly back to their houses, for they feared the curse that the Freer had called down upon unicorns, and knew not if their own names might become involved in one of the curses called upon magical things.

===Chinese==
===Chinese===
他们痛饮蜂蜜酒，欢腾直至深夜，借这朴实的酒力凝望未来之年月——但凡人力之目所能及的那样远。然而他们一切欢腾都是压低了声音的，唯恐那听忏悔的神父（the Freer）的耳朵听见；因为他们的欢喜来自那些远超救赎之念的土地，他们已将信任寄托于魔法——而他们深知，每当黄昏那神父的钟敲响，钟里发出的每一个鸣响，都是针对魔法的轰鸣。他们散得很晚，并不高声地赞美魔法，偷偷地各自回去，因为他们畏惧那神父对独角兽所降的诅咒，也不知道自己的名字会不会卷入某一道针对魔法之物的诅咒中去。

===Original===
All the next day Orion rested his hounds, and the trolls and the people of Erl gazed at each other. But on the day that followed Orion took his sword and gathered his band of trolls and his pack of hounds, and all were away once more far over the downs, to come again to the border of nebulous opal and to lurk for the unicorns coming through in the evening.

They came to a part of the border far from the spot which they had disturbed only three evenings before; and Orion was guided by the chattering trolls, for well they knew the haunts of the lonely unicorns. And Earth's evening came huge and hushed, till all was dim as the twilight; and never a footfall did they hear of the unicorns, never a glimpse of their whiteness. And yet the trolls had guided Orion well, for just as he would have despaired of a hunt that night, just when the evening seemed wholly and utterly empty, a unicorn stood on the earthward edge of the twilight where nothing had stood only a moment before: soon he moved slowly across the terrestrial grasses a few yards forward into the fields of men.

===Chinese===
次日一整天奥利安让他的群犬歇息，巨怪们与厄尔之民彼此相视。但再过一天，奥利安佩上剑，召集他那队巨怪与那群猎犬，全体再次远赴丘陵之外，来到那片朦胧的蛋白石色边境，潜伏等候薄暮时分穿过的独角兽。

他们来到边境上离三天前才搅扰过的那处甚远的一个地方；奥利安由喋喋不休的巨怪们引路，因为他们深知那些孤独的独角兽出没之处。人间的傍晚浩大而寂静地降临，直到一切都如暮霭般昏暗；他们听不见独角兽的一丝足音，也看不见它们白色身躯的一瞥。然而巨怪们确实把奥利安引得不错，因为正当他即将对那夜的狩猎绝望之际，正当那傍晚似乎全然彻底地空无一物之时，一只独角兽站在了暮霭趋向人间的那一边缘——片刻之前那里还一无所有：不久它缓缓越过人间的青草，向前几步走进了人类的田野。

===Original===
Another followed, moving a few yards also; and then they stood for fifteen of our earthly minutes moving nothing at all except their ears. And all that while the trolls hushed every hound, motionless under a hedge of the fields we know. Darkness had all but hidden them when at last the unicorns moved. And, as soon as the largest was far enough from the frontier, the trolls let loose every hound, and ran with them after the unicorn with shrill yells of derision, all sure of his haughty head.

But the quick small minds of the trolls, though they had learned much of Earth, had not yet understood the irregularity of the moon. Darkness was new to them, and they soon lost hounds. Orion in his eagerness to hunt had made no choice of a suitable night: there was no moon at all, and would be none till near morning. Soon he also fell behind.

===Chinese===
又一只跟上，也走了几步；然后它们站定了足足一刻钟——以我们人间的钟点计——除了耳朵之外什么也不动。那整个工夫，巨怪们使每只猎犬都噤了声，一动不动地躲在我们所知原野的一道树篱之下。黑暗几乎将它们尽数遮蔽之时，独角兽们终于动了。而当最大的那只离边境足够远时，巨怪们放出了所有的猎犬，随它们一起追赶那只独角兽，发出尖利的嗤笑之声，全都志在必得那颗高傲的头颅。

然而巨怪们那敏捷而细小的心智，虽已从人间学到了许多，却尚未明了月亮的不规则。黑暗对它们而言是件新鲜事物，它们很快便与群犬失散。奥利安急于狩猎，并未挑选一个合适的夜晚：那一夜根本没有月亮，而且直到将近天亮也不会有。不久他也落在了后面。

===Original===
Orion easily collected the trolls, the night was full of their frivolous noises, and the trolls came to his horn, but not a hound would leave that pungent magical scent for any horn of man. They straggled back next day, tired, having lost their unicorn.

And while each troll cleaned and fed his hound on the evening after the hunt, and laid a little bunch of straw for it on which to lie down, and smoothed its hair and looked for thorns in its feet, and unravelled burrs from its ears, Lurulu sat alone fastening his small sharp intelligence, like the little white light of a burning glass, for hours upon one question. The question that Lurulu pondered far into the night was how to hunt unicorns with dogs in the darkness. And by midnight a plan was clear in his elvish mind.

===Chinese===
奥利安轻而易举地召拢了巨怪们——夜里到处是它们轻浮的吵闹声，巨怪们应他的号角而来，但没有一只猎犬肯为了任何人间的号角离开那股刺鼻而神奇的气味。第二天它们才三三两两地疲惫归来，丢了它们的独角兽。

当每一只巨怪在狩猎之后的傍晚为它的猎犬清洗、喂食，为它铺一小捆稻草好让它躺下，为它抚平毛发、在它的脚掌间寻找刺、解开它耳朵里的苍耳时，卢鲁鲁独自坐着，将他那细小而锋利的智慧，如同聚光玻璃的那一点白光，一连数小时集中于一个问题之上。卢鲁鲁苦苦思索直至深夜的那个问题是：如何在黑暗中用犬猎独角兽。到了午夜，他那精灵的心中已有了清晰的计划。

## XX A Historical Fact / 二十 一桩史实

===Original===
Amongst the weary hounds refreshed with fury and triumph, Orion stepped with his whip and drove them away from the monstrous dead body, and sent the lash quivering round in a wide circle, while in his other hand he took his sword and cut off the unicorn’s head. He also took the skin of the long white neck and brought it away dangling empty from the head. All the while the hounds bayed and made eager rushes one by one at that magical carcase whenever one saw a chance of eluding the whip; so that it was long before Orion got his trophy, for he had to work as hard with his whip as with his sword. But at last he had it slung by a leather thong over his shoulders, the great horn pointing upwards past the right side of his head, and the smeared skin hanging down along his back. And while he arranged it thus he allowed his hounds to worry the body again and taste that wonderful blood. Then he called to them and blew a note on his horn and turned slowly home towards Erl, and they all followed behind him. And the two foxes stole up to taste the curious blood, for they had sat and waited for this.

While the unicorn was climbing his last hill Orion felt such fatigue that he could have gone little further, but now that the heavy head hung from his shoulders all his fatigue was gone and he trod with a lightness such as he had in the mornings, for it was his first unicorn. And his hounds seemed refreshed as though the blood they had lapped had some strange power in it, and they came home riotously, gambolling and rushing ahead as when newly loosed from their kennels.

Thus Orion came home over the downs in the night, till he saw the valley before him full of the smoke of Erl, where one late light was burning in a window of one of his towers. And, coming down the slopes by familiar ways, he brought his hounds to their kennels; and just before dawn had touched the heights of the downs he blew his horn before his postern door. And the aged guardian of the door when he opened it to Orion saw the great horn of the unicorn bobbing over his head.

===Chinese===
在那些因狂怒与胜利而重新振作起来的疲惫群犬之间，奥利安执鞭上前，将它们从那具庞大可怖的尸骸旁驱开，挥鞭使鞭梢颤动着在空中划出一个大圈；与此同时，他另一只手抽出剑，斩下了那独角兽的首级。他也将那条长长的白色颈皮一并剥下，连着头颅一同带走，颈皮空荡荡地垂在头颅之下。群犬却始终吠叫不止，只要有一只瞧见能避开鞭子的空当，便一只接一只地急扑向那具充满魔法的尸骸；因此奥利安费了好些工夫才取得他的战利品，因为他挥鞭之劳并不下于挥剑之苦。但终于他用一根皮绳将它挎上双肩，那支大角自他头部右侧斜斜指向上方，沾满血污的皮则沿着他的背垂下。就在他这般安顿战利品之际，他任由群犬再去撕咬那尸体，尝一尝那奇异的血。随后他唤了它们，吹了一声号角，缓缓地转身向厄尔回程而去，它们统统跟在他身后。那两只狐狸也偷偷上前，去尝一尝那奇异的血——因为它们一直蹲在那里候着这一刻。

当那独角兽爬上它最后一座山丘时，奥利安只觉疲乏得几乎再也挪不动步；可如今那沉重的头颅悬在他肩上，他一切的倦意竟都消散了，脚步轻捷得如同清晨一般——因为这是他亲手猎得的第一只独角兽。他的群犬也似乎精神焕发，仿佛它们所舔过的那血里含着某种奇异的力量；它们一路闹哄哄地归来，嬉耍前冲，一如刚从犬舍里被放出之时。

奥利安就这样在夜里越过丘陵归家，直到他看见前方的山谷里满是厄尔的炊烟，其中他的一座塔楼的一扇窗里，还亮着一盏迟熄的灯。他沿着熟识的小路走下山坡，把群犬带回犬舍；就在黎明即将触及丘陵高处之前，他在他的后门前吹响了号角。那年迈的守门人为他开门时，只见那独角兽的大角正在他头顶上一颠一颠的。

===Original===
This was the horn that was sent in later years as a gift from the Pope to King Francis. Benvenuto Cellini tells of it in his memoirs. He tells how Pope Clement sent for him and a certain Tobbia, and ordered them to make designs for the setting of a unicorn’s horn, the finest ever seen. Judge then of Orion’s delight when the horn of the first unicorn he ever took was such as to be esteemed generations later the finest ever seen, and in no less a city than Rome, with all her opportunities to acquire and compare such things. For a number of these curious horns must have been available for the Pope to have selected for the gift the finest ever seen; but in the simpler days of my story the rarity of the horn was so great that unicorns were still considered fabulous. The year of the gift to King Francis would be about 1530, the horn being mounted in gold; and the contract went to Tobbia and not to Benvenuto Cellini. I mention the date because there are those who care little for a tale if it be not here and there supported by history, and who even in history care more for fact than philosophy. If any such reader have followed the fortunes of Orion so far he will be hungry by now for a date or a historical fact. As for the date, I give him 1530. While for the historical fact I select that generous gift recorded by Benvenuto Cellini, because it may well be that just where he came to unicorns such a reader may have felt furthest away from history and have felt loneliest just at this point for want of historical things. How the unicorn’s horn found its way from the Castle of Erl, and in what hands it wandered, and how it came at last to the City of Rome, would of course make another book.

===Chinese===
这就是多年以后教皇当作礼物赠予弗朗西斯王的那支角。本韦努托·切利尼（Benvenuto Cellini）在他的回忆录里曾提及它。他讲到教皇克雷芒（Pope Clement）如何把他和一个名叫托比亚（Tobbia）的人一并召去，命他们为一只独角兽的角——旷世所见最精的一只——设计镶座。请想见奥利安该有多欣喜罢：他所猎得的第一只独角兽，其角竟能在数代之后仍被推崇为世间仅见之最精者，而评判之地非他，正是罗马——那样一座大有途径搜求、比照此等珍奇之城。因为教皇既能从中拣出最精的一只作礼物，则当时可供挑选的此类奇角想必不在少数；然而在我这故事所处的较为古朴的年代，此角之珍稀，到了独角兽尚被视作传说之物的地步。赠角于弗朗西斯王一事，其年约当1530，角以黄金镶嵌；而这桩活计终究落到了托比亚手中，并未交给本韦努托·切利尼。我之所以提及此年份，是因为世间有那么一种人：若非这里那里有史实为之佐证，他们便对一桩故事兴致缺缺；而即便在史书之中，他们看重的亦是事实，而非义理。这样的读者若一路追随奥利安的际遇读到此处，此刻必已渴盼着一个日期、一桩史实了。至于日期，我给他1530。至于史实，我则选取本韦努托·切利尼所记载的那桩慷慨的馈赠——因为很可能恰恰在读到独角兽这一节时，这样的读者最觉自己远离了历史，也正是在此处，因缺乏史实之物而最感孤寂。至于那独角兽的角是怎样从厄尔城堡辗转流出，又曾流落过何人之手，最终怎样到了罗马城——那自然会是另外一本书的事了。

===Original===
But all that I need say now about that horn is that Orion took the whole head to Threl, who took off the skin and washed it and boiled the skull for hours, and replaced the skin and stuffed the neck with straw; and Orion set it in the midmost place among all the heads that hung in the high hall. And the rumour went all through Erl, as swift as unicorns gallop, telling of this fine horn that Orion had won. So that the parliament of Erl met again in the forge of Narl. They sat at the table there debating the rumour; and others besides Threl had seen the head. And at first, for the sake of old divisions, some held to their opinion that there had been no unicorn. They drank Narl’s goodly mead and argued against the monster. But after a while, whether Threl’s argument convinced them, or whether as is more likely, they yielded from generosity, which arose like a beautiful flower out of the mellow mead, whatever it was the debate of those that opposed the unicorn languished, and when the vote was put it was declared that Orion had killed a unicorn, which he had hunted hither from beyond the fields we know.

===Chinese===
但我现在关于那支角所须说的，只是：奥利安把整颗头颅送到了斯瑞尔那里，斯瑞尔剥下皮、洗净，将头骨煮上好几个时辰，再把皮覆回，用稻草填塞颈项；奥利安便将它安在高大厅堂里所有悬挂的首级之中最居中的位置。消息于是传遍整个厄尔，迅疾一如独角兽的飞奔，传说着奥利安所赢得的这支精美之角。于是厄尔议会的人又在纳尔的铁匠铺里聚首了。他们在那儿的桌旁坐下，就这桩传闻辩论；除斯瑞尔外，旁人也曾见过那颗首级。起初，为了旧日分歧之故，有些人仍固守己见，认为根本没有什么独角兽。他们饮着纳尔上好的蜂蜜酒，与那怪兽力争。但过了一阵，究竟是斯瑞尔的论据说服了他们，还是——更有可能——他们出于慷慨而退让了（这慷慨如同一朵美丽的花，从那醇厚的蜂蜜酒中绽放而出），无论如何，反对独角兽一方的辩论渐渐气衰，到表决之时，众人宣告：奥利安确曾杀了一只独角兽，是他从我们所知的原野之外一路追猎至此的。

===Original===
And at this they all rejoiced; for they saw at last the magic for which they had longed, and for which they had planned so many years ago, when all were younger and had had more hope in their plans. And as soon as the vote was taken Narl brought out more mead, and they drank again to mark the happy occasion: for magic at last, said they, had come on Orion, and a glorious future surely awaited Erl. And the long room and the candles and the friendly men and the deep comfort of mead made it easy to look a little way forward into time and to see a year or so that had not yet come, and to see coming glories glowing a little way off. And they told again of the days, but nearer now, when the distant lands should hear of the vale they loved: they told again of the fame of the fields of Erl going from city to city. One praised its castle, another its huge high downs, another the vale itself all hidden from every land, another the dear quaint houses built by an olden folk, another the deep of the woods that lay over the skyline; and all spoke of the time when the wide world should hear of it all, because of the magic that there was in Orion; for they knew that the world has a quick ear for magic, and always turns toward the wonderful even though it be nearly asleep. Their voices were high, praising magic, telling again of the unicorn, glorying in the future of Erl, when suddenly in the doorway stood the Freer. He was there in his long white robe with its trimming of mauve, in the door with the night behind him. As they looked, in the light of their candles, they could see he was wearing an emblem, on a chain of gold round his neck. Narl bade him welcome, some moved a chair to the table; but he had heard them speak of the unicorn. He lifted his voice from where he stood, and addressed them. “Cursed be unicorns,” he said, “and all their ways, and all things that be magic.”

===Chinese===
众人于是皆大欢喜；因为他们终于亲眼看见了自己曾久久渴望、许多年前便已为之谋划的那桩魔法——那时众人都还年轻，对自己的谋划还怀有更多的希冀。表决一毕，纳尔便又搬出更多的蜂蜜酒，他们再次痛饮以志此喜庆之时：因为，他们说，魔法终于降临到了奥利安身上，光辉的未来一定正等着厄尔。长长的屋子、烛光、友善的人们，以及蜂蜜酒所带来的深深慰藉，使人很容易向前眺望一小段时光，看见尚未到来的那么一两年，看见正在不远处隐隐发光的即将来临的荣光。他们再次说起那些日子——如今是更近了——那时远方的土地将听闻他们所爱的这个山谷：他们再次说起厄尔原野的声名将如何从一座城传到另一座城。一人称赞它的城堡，一人称赞它那辽阔高耸的丘陵，又一人称赞山谷本身——它藏得对诸邦之地全然不见，再一人称赞那些由古老之民所建的可爱而古趣的屋舍，还有一人称赞那越过天际线延展的幽深树林；众人都在谈论着那个时辰：有朝一日广阔的世界将这一切尽皆听闻，全因奥利安身上的那桩魔法；因为他们知道，世人对魔法的耳朵向来灵敏，纵然几近酣眠，也总会转向那神奇之物。他们的嗓音高亢，赞美着魔法，再次说起那独角兽，为厄尔的未来而得意洋洋——就在这时，教士（the Freer）突然出现在了门口。他就在那里，穿着他那缀有淡紫色饰边的白色长袍，立在门口，身后便是黑夜。借着烛光望去，他们能看见他颈间一条金链上挂着一枚徽记。纳尔向他道了声欢迎，有人搬了把椅子到桌前；可他已经听见了他们说起独角兽。他从他所立之处提高了嗓音，向众人开口。「愿独角兽受诅咒，」他说，「以及它们的一切行径，以及一切属魔法之物。」

===Original===
In the awe that suddenly changed the mellow room one cried: “Master! Curse not us!”

“Good Freer,” said Narl, “we hunted no unicorn.”

===Chinese===
一阵突来的敬畏使这间醇和的屋子变了气氛，有人叫道：「师父！别诅咒我们！」

「善良的教士，」纳尔说，「我们并未猎过什么独角兽。」

===Original===
But the Freer raised up his hand against unicorns and cursed them yet. “Curst be their horn,” he cried, “and the place where they dwell, and the lilies whereon they feed, curst be all songs that tell of them. Curst be they utterly with everything that dwelleth beyond salvation.”

He paused to allow them to renounce the unicorns, standing still in the doorway, looking sternly into the room.

And they thought of the sleekness of the unicorn’s hide, his swiftness, the grace of his neck, and his dim beauty cantering by when he came past Erl in the evening. They thought of his stalwart and redoubtable horn; they remembered old songs that told of him. They sat in uneasy silence and would not renounce the unicorn.

===Chinese===
但教士却仍举手向独角兽，再次诅咒它们。「愿它们的角受诅咒，」他喊道，「以及它们栖居之所，以及它们赖以为食的百合花，愿一切歌咏它们的歌谣受诅咒。愿它们与一切居于救赎之外的事物，统统彻底受诅咒。」

他停了一下，好让他们弃绝独角兽，自己则一动不动地立在门口，严厉地凝视着屋内。

他们却想起那独角兽皮毛的光润、它的迅捷、它颈项的优雅，以及它在傍晚经过厄尔身旁时缓步跑过时那朦胧的美。他们想起它那雄壮而可畏的角；他们记起那些讲述它的古老歌谣。他们不安地沉默而坐，不肯弃绝那独角兽。

===Original===
And the Freer knew what they thought and he raised his hand again, clear in the candlelight with the night behind him. “Curst be their speed,” he said, “and their sleek white hide; curst be their beauty and all that they have of magic, and everything that walks by enchanted streams.”

And still he saw in their eyes a lingering love for those things that he forbade, and therefore he ceased not yet. He lifted his voice yet louder and continued, with his eye sternly upon those troubled faces: “And curst be trolls, elves, goblins and fairies upon the Earth, and hypogriffs and Pegasus in the air, and all the tribes of the mer-folk under the sea. Our holy rites forbid them. And curst be all doubts, all singular dreams, all fancies. And from magic may all true folk be turned away. Amen.”

===Chinese===
教士知道他们心中所想，便再次举起手来——烛光之中分外清晰，身后便是黑夜。「愿它们的迅捷受诅咒，」他说，「以及它们光润的白皮；愿它们的美丽受诅咒，以及它们一切属魔法之物，以及一切漫步于施过魔法的溪畔之物。」

他仍能从他们眼中看见对那些他所禁绝之物的恋恋之情，于是他仍未停止。他把嗓音提得更高，继续说道，目光严厉地盯着那些不安的面孔：「愿大地上的巨怪、精灵、哥布林与众精灵受诅咒，愿空中的骏鹰与珀伽索斯受诅咒，愿海底一切人鱼之族受诅咒。我们的圣礼禁绝它们。愿一切疑虑、一切奇异的梦、一切幻想受诅咒。愿一切纯真之民都能远离魔法。阿门。」

===Original===
He turned round suddenly and was into the night. A wind loitered about the door, then flapped it to. And the large room in the forge of Narl was as it had been but a few moments before, yet the mellow mood of it seemed dulled and dim. And then Narl spoke, rising up at the table’s end and breaking the gloom of the silence. “Did we plan our plans,” he said, “so long ago, and put our faith in magic, that we should now renounce magical things and curse our neighbours, the harmless folk beyond the fields we know, and the beautiful things of the air, and dead mariners’ lovers dwelling beneath the sea?”

===Chinese===
他猛地一转身，便隐入了黑夜。一阵风在门口徘徊了片刻，随后把门砰地关上。纳尔铁匠铺里这间大屋子，与片刻之前并无两样，然而那醇和的情致却仿佛黯淡了、朦胧了。随后纳尔开口了，他在桌端站起身来，打破了那沉闷的静默。「难道我们，」他说，「许久以前那样苦苦谋划了又谋划，又将信心托付于魔法，如今反倒要弃绝那些属魔法之物，去诅咒我们的邻人——那些住在我们所知原野之外、于人无害的族类——去诅咒空中那些美丽之物，以及那些住在海底的、已故水手们的情人吗？」

===Original===
“No, no,” said some. And they quaffed their mead again.

And then one rose with his horn of mead held high, then another and then another, till all were standing upright all round the light of the candles. “Magic!” one cried. And the rest with one accord took up his cry till all were shouting “Magic.”

The Freer on his homeward way heard that cry of Magic, he gathered his sacred robe more closely around him and clutched his holy things, and said a spell that kept him from sudden demons and the doubtful things of the mist.

===Chinese===
「不，不。」有人说道。他们于是又痛饮起蜂蜜酒来。

随后一人高举着他的角杯站了起来，接着又一个，又一个，直到所有人都围着烛光挺身而立。「魔法！」一人喊道。其余的人齐声附和，直到所有人都在高喊「魔法」。

教士在归家途中听见了那一声声「魔法」的呼喊，他把圣袍更紧地裹在身上，紧紧攥着他的圣物，念了一道咒，好使自己免遭突如其来的魔鬼与雾中可疑之物的侵害。

## IV Alveric Comes Back to Earth After Many Years / 四 阿尔韦里克多年后重归人世

===Original===
Back through the guarding wood went Alveric and Lirazel, she only looking once more at those flowers and lawns, seen only by the furthest-travelling fancies of poets in deepest sleep, then urging Alveric on; he choosing the way past trees he had disenchanted.

And she would not let him delay even to choose his path, but kept urging him away from the palace that is only told of in song. And the other trees began to come lumbering towards them, from beyond the lustreless unromantic line that Alveric’s sword had smitten, looking queerly as they came at their stricken comrades, whose listless branches drooped without magic or mystery. And as the moving trees came nearer Lirazel would hold up her hand, and they all halted and came on no more; and still she urged upon Alveric to hasten.

She knew her father would climb the brazen stairs of one of those silver spires, she knew he would soon come out on to a high balcony, she knew what rune he would chant. She heard the sound of his footsteps ascending, ringing now through the wood. They fled over the plain beyond the wood, all through the blue everlasting elfin day, and again and again she looked over her shoulder and urged Alveric on. The Elf King’s feet boomed slow on the thousand brazen steps, and she hoped to reach the barrier of twilight, which on that side was smoky and dull; when suddenly, as she looked for the hundredth time at the distant balconies of the glittering spires, she saw a door begin to open high up, above the palace only told of in song. She cried “Alas!” to Alveric, but at that moment the scent of briar roses came drifting to them from the fields we know.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克与莉拉泽尔穿过护卫之林往回奔去；她只回望了那片花木与草坪一眼——那是唯有诗人在最深沉的睡梦中、在想象飞至最远之处方能见到的景致——便催着阿尔韦里克向前；他则沿着那一条路走，绕过那些已被他破除魔法的树木。

她不肯容他稍有耽搁，连择路都等不及，只不住催他远离那座唯歌谣中方有传唱的宫殿。其余的树木也开始笨重地朝他们移动过来，越过阿尔韦里克的剑所劈出的那条黯淡无奇、了无神秘之气的界线；它们走近那些受了重创的同伴时，神色古怪地望着它们——那些同伴无精打采的枝条垂落着，再无魔法，再无奥秘。而当移动的树木愈逼愈近，莉拉泽尔便抬起手来，它们便一齐止步，不再上前；她仍不住地催促阿尔韦里克快走。

她知道父亲会登上某一银色尖塔的铜阶，她知道他很快便会步上一处高高的露台，她知道他将念诵哪一道符文。她听见他登楼的脚步声响起，此刻正铮铮地回荡在林间。他们在林外的原野上奔逃，整整一日都是精灵国那永恒不暮的湛蓝天光，她一次又一次回过头去，催促阿尔韦里克快行。精灵国王的脚步在那千级铜阶上缓缓踏响，轰然如雷；她满心盼着能赶到那暮色的屏障跟前——在这一侧，那屏障是烟雾沉沉、黯然无光的。蓦地，在她第一百次回望那闪耀尖塔上远远的露台时，她看见高高的空中，在那座唯歌谣中方有传唱的宫殿之上，一扇门正缓缓开启。她对阿尔韦里克惊呼「不好了！」——可就在那一刻，野蔷薇的芬芳，从我们所知的原野上飘送而至。

===Original===
Alveric knew not fatigue for he was young, nor she for she was ageless. They rushed forward, he taking her hand; the Elf King lifted his beard, and just as he began to intone a rune that only once may be uttered, against which nothing from our fields can avail, they were through the frontier of twilight, and the rune shook and troubled those lands in which Lirazel walked no longer.

When Lirazel looked upon the fields we know, as strange to her as once they have been to us, their beauty delighted her. She laughed to see the haystacks and loved their quaintness. A lark was singing and Lirazel spoke to it, and the lark seemed not to understand, but she turned to other glories of our fields, for all were new to her, and forgot the lark. It was curiously no longer the season of bluebells, for all the foxgloves were blooming and the may was gone and the wild roses were there. Alveric never understood this.

It was early morning and the sun was shining, giving soft colours to our fields, and Lirazel rejoiced in those fields of ours at more common things than one might believe there were amongst the familiar sights of Earth’s every day. So glad was she, so gay, with her cries of surprise and her laughter, that there seemed thenceforth to Alveric a beauty that he had never dreamed of in buttercups, and a humour in carts that he never had thought of before. Each moment she found with a cry of joyous discovery some treasure of Earth’s that he had not known to be fair. And then, as he watched her bringing a beauty to our fields more delicate even than that the wild roses brought, he saw that her crown of ice had melted away.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克不知疲倦，因他正值年少；她亦不知疲倦，因她本无年岁。他们向前飞奔，他握住她的手；精灵国王扬起了胡须，正当他开始吟诵一道唯可诵念一次的符文——此符文一出，我们原野上的一切都无从招架——他们已穿过了暮色的边境；那符文震动了、搅扰了那些莉拉泽尔再不涉足的国土。

当莉拉泽尔凝望我们所知的原野——那原野之于她，便如当初之于我们一般陌生——其间的美令她满心欢喜。她看见干草堆便笑出声来，爱极了它们那古拙的模样。一只云雀正在歌唱，莉拉泽尔对它说话，云雀却似听不懂；她便转向我们原野上别的可爱之物，因为这一切于她都是崭新的，便把那云雀忘了。说来也奇，此时已不是风铃草的花期，因毛地黄尽数盛放，山楂花已经谢了，野玫瑰却正开着。阿尔韦里克始终不明白这是怎么回事。

正是清晨，阳光照耀，将柔柔的色彩铺在我们原野之上；莉拉泽尔在这些原野间欢欣不已，那些寻常之物之能令她快乐，实多于人们所能相信人间日常景象中所含的。她是那样欣喜，那样欢畅，一声声惊呼与欢笑，以至于自此以后，在阿尔韦里克看来，金凤花里竟有他从未梦见的美丽，而大车之上也有他从未想到的意趣。每一刻，她都带着一声快乐的惊呼，发现一样大地的珍宝——那是他从未晓得竟如此美好的。随后，他望着她将一种比野玫瑰所带来的还要纤柔的美带到我们原野之间，这才看见：她那顶冰之王冠，已悄然融化不见了。

===Original===
And thus she came from the palace that may only be told of in song, over the fields of which I need not tell, for they were the familiar fields of Earth, that the ages change but little and only for a while, and came at evening with Alveric to his home.

All was changed in the Castle of Erl. In the gateway they met a guardian whom Alveric knew: the man wondered to see them. In the hall and upon the stairway they met some that tended the castle, who turned their heads in surprise. Alveric knew them also, but all were older; and he saw that quite ten years must have passed away during that one blue day he had spent in Elfland.

Who does not know that this is the way of Elfland? And yet who would not be surprised if they saw it happen as Alveric saw it now? He turned to Lirazel and told her how ten or twelve years were gone. But it was as though a humble man who had wed an earthly princess should tell her he had lost sixpence; time had had no value or meaning to Lirazel, and she was untroubled to hear of the ten lost years. She did not dream what time means to us here.

===Chinese===
她便这样离开了那座唯歌谣中方有传唱的宫殿，越过那些我无需赘述的原野——那原野不过是大地所熟稔的田野，岁月对它们只略加更易，且不过一时——终于在黄昏时分随阿尔韦里克到了他的家。

厄尔城堡里一切都已不同。他们在城门处遇见一位阿尔韦里克认识的守卫：那人见他们出现，满脸惊异。在大厅里、在楼梯上，他们又遇见几个照管城堡的人，都惊讶地回过头来。阿尔韦里克也认得他们，只是人人都老了许多；他这才看出，他在精灵国度过的那一个湛蓝之日，此地必已逝去了整整十年。

精灵国向来如此行事，谁人不知？可倘若亲眼看见这事如阿尔韦里克此刻所目睹的那般发生，又有谁会不惊诧呢？他转身告诉莉拉泽尔，十年、十二年的光阴已经逝去。然而这情形，好比一个卑微之人娶了一位人间的公主，却对她说自己丢了六便士；时间于莉拉泽尔素无价值、亦无意义，她听说那十个失落的年头，心中并不烦恼。她做梦也想不到，时间在这里于我们意味着什么。

===Original===
They told Alveric that his father was long since dead. And one told him how he died happy, without impatience, trusting to Alveric to accomplish his bidding; for he had known somewhat of the ways of Elfland, and knew that those that traffic twixt here and there must have something of that calm in which Elfland forever dreams.

Up the valley, ringing late, they heard the blacksmith’s work. This blacksmith was he who had been the spokesman of those who went once to the long red room to the Lord of Erl. And all these men yet lived; for time though it moved over the Vale of Erl, as over all fields we know, moved gently, not as in our cities.

Thence Alveric and Lirazel went to the holy place of the Freer. And when they found him Alveric asked the Freer to wed them with Christom rites. And when the Freer saw the beauty of Lirazel flash mid the common things in his little holy place, for he had ornamented the walls of his house with knickknacks that he sometimes bought at the fairs, he feared at once she was of no mortal line. And, when he asked her whence she came and she happily answered “Elfland,” the good man clasped his hands and told her earnestly how all in that land dwelt beyond salvation. But she smiled, for while in Elfland she had always been idly happy, and now she only cared for Alveric. The Freer went then to his books to see what should be done.

===Chinese===
人们告诉阿尔韦里克，他的父亲早已不在人世。又有一人告诉他，老领主是安然离世的，并无焦躁，深信阿尔韦里克必能完成他所托之事；因为他多少晓得精灵国的脾气，知道在此地与彼地之间往来奔走的人，身上须有几分精灵国永远梦游其间的那份从容。

沿山谷上传来铁匠打铁的声音，回响得迟了些。这位铁匠，正是当年那群人前往那间深红长厅觐见厄尔领主时的代言人。这些人这时都还在世；因为岁月纵然流过厄尔之谷，一如流过我们所知的一切原野，却是缓缓流过，不像在我们那些城市里一般。

阿尔韦里克和莉拉泽尔由此前往教士（the Freer）的圣所。寻见教士之后，阿尔韦里克请他以基督圣礼（Christom rites）为他们行婚礼。教士一见莉拉泽尔的美在他那小小的圣所中、于诸般寻常之物间熠熠闪耀——他原曾用偶或在集市上买来的种种小玩意装饰他小屋的墙壁——便立刻害怕她并非凡人之族。待他问她从何处来、她欣然答以「精灵国」时，这位好心人便双手交握，恳恳切切地告诉她，那片国土上的一切生灵都居于拯救之外。可她只是微笑，因为她在精灵国时向来是闲闲的快乐，而此刻她心中只挂着阿尔韦里克一人。教士于是转身去翻他的书，看看该如何是好。

===Original===
For a long while he read in silence but for his breathing, while Alveric and Lirazel stood before him. And at last he found in his book a form of service for the wedding of a mermaid that had forsaken the sea, though the good book told not of Elfland. And this he said would suffice, for that the mermaids dwelt equally with the elf-folk beyond thought of salvation. So he sent for his bell and such tapers as are necessary. Then, turning to Lirazel, he bade her forsake and forswear and solemnly to renounce all things pertaining to Elfland, reading slowly out of a book the words to be used on this wholesome occasion.

“Good Freer,” Lirazel answered, “nought said in these fields can cross the barrier of Elfland. And well that this is so, for my father has three runes that could blast this book when he answered one of its spells, were any word able to pass through the frontier of twilight. I will spell no spells with my father.”

“But I cannot wed Christom man,” the Freer replied, “with one of the stubborn who dwell beyond salvation.”

Then Alveric implored her and she said the say in the book, “though my father could blast this spell,” she added, “if it ever crossed one of his runes.” And, the bell being now brought and the tapers, the good man wedded them in his little house with the rites that are proper for the wedding of a mermaid that hath forsaken the sea.

===Chinese===
良久，他默默诵读，唯闻呼吸之声，阿尔韦里克与莉拉泽尔侍立其前。终于，他在书中寻得一种仪轨，是为那弃海的美人鱼行婚礼而设——虽然那部善书并未言及精灵国。他说这便够了，因为美人鱼与精灵一族同住在拯救之外。于是他命人去取他的铃，以及所需的一切蜡烛。然后他转向莉拉泽尔，命她弃绝、誓绝、并郑重放弃一切与精灵国相关之物，缓缓地从一本经书中念出那种合乎此番正道场合所当用的字句。

「好教士，」莉拉泽尔回答道，「这些原野上所说的一切话，都越不过精灵国的屏障。这事本当如此才好，因为我父亲手中有三道符文，倘若这里有任何一句话能穿过暮色的边境，他便能用其中一道反制这书的咒语，将这本书轰得粉碎。我不要与我父亲斗法。」

「但我不能以基督圣礼，」教士答道，「将一个基督徒男子与一个执拗不化、居于拯救之外的人结合为婚。」

于是阿尔韦里克苦苦恳求她，她便依书中所言说了一遍——「虽然我父亲本可击碎这道咒语，」她补上一句，「倘若它当真碰上了他的某一道符文。」待铃与蜡烛俱已取来，这位好心人便在他那间小屋里，以合乎一个弃海美人鱼所当用的礼法，为他们两个行了婚礼。

## II Alveric Comes in Sight of the Elfin Mountains / 二 阿尔韦里克望见精灵山脉

===Original===
To the long chamber, sparsely furnished, high in a tower, in which Alveric slept, there came a ray direct from the rising sun. He awoke, and remembered at once the magical sword, which made all his awaking joyous. It is natural to feel glad at the thought of a recent gift, but there was also a certain joy in the sword itself, which perhaps could communicate with Alveric’s thoughts all the more easily just as they came from dreamland, which was preeminently the sword’s own country; but, however it be, all those that have come by a magical sword, have always felt that joy while it still was new, clearly and unmistakably.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克（Alveric）所睡的那间长形卧房陈设寥寥，高踞于一塔楼之上。初升的太阳直射进一道光线。他一觉醒来，立时想起了那柄魔法之剑，使他的整个清晨都充满欢欣。因念及新得的礼物而喜，本是人之常情，然而那剑本身亦自有其一番欢愉，或许正因他的心思刚从梦乡而来，那剑便愈发容易与之相通——梦乡本就是那剑自家的乡土；但无论如何，凡得一柄魔法之剑的人，当那剑尚新之际，无不确凿无疑地感到那种欢欣。

===Original===
He had no farewells to make, but thought it better instantly to obey his father’s command than to stay to explain why he took upon his adventure a sword that he deemed to be better than the one his father loved. So he stayed not even to eat, but put food in a wallet and slung over him by a strap a bottle of good new leather, not waiting to fill it for he knew he should meet with streams; and, wearing his father’s sword as swords are commonly worn, he slung the other over his back with its rough hilt tied near his shoulder, and strode away from the Castle and Vale of Erl. Of money he took but little, half a handful of copper only, for use in the fields we know; for he knew not what coin or what means of exchange were used on the other side of the frontier of twilight.

===Chinese===
他无须向谁辞行，心想与其留下来解释为何此番冒险要带一柄他自认为胜过父亲所钟爱之剑的剑，不如立即遵从父亲之命更为妥当。于是他连饭也不曾留下吃，只把干粮装入一只背袋（wallet），又用一条带子把一只崭新的好皮制水壶斜挎在身上——并不等着灌水，因为他知道自己一路上总会遇见溪流；他将父亲的剑照寻常佩剑之法佩在腰间，把另一柄剑用带子系了背在背上，那粗砺的剑柄便拢在肩头，随即大步走出厄尔城堡（the Castle of Erl）与厄尔之谷（the Vale of Erl）。钱财他只带了少许，不过半把铜币，以备在我们所知的原野上使用；因为暮色的边境（the frontier of twilight）那一边通行的钱币是何物、用什么交换之法，他一概不知。

===Original===
Now the Vale of Erl is very near to the border beyond which there is none of the fields we know. He climbed the hill and strode over the fields and passed through woods of hazel; and the blue sky shone on him merrily as he went by the way of the fields, and the blue was as bright by his feet when he came to the woods, for it was the time of the bluebells. He ate, and filled his water-bottle, and travelled all day eastwards, and at evening the mountains of Faery came floating into view, the colour of pale forget-me-nots.

===Chinese===
须知厄尔之谷离那道边界甚近——过了那边界便不再有我们所知的原野。他登上山冈，大步越过田野，穿过榛树林；当他沿着田间的小路走去，蔚蓝的天空欢快地照在他身上；待他走到林边，脚下那片蔚蓝也同样明亮，因为此时正是蓝风信花（bluebells）盛开之时。他一路上吃些干粮，灌满水壶（water-bottle），整日向东而行，到了傍晚，精灵国（Faery）的群山如浮影般映入眼帘，颜色淡如浅浅的勿忘草（forget-me-nots）。

===Original===
As the sun set behind Alveric he looked at those pale-blue mountains to see with what colour their peaks would astonish the evening; but never a tint they took from the setting sun, whose splendour was gilding all the fields we know, never a wrinkle faded upon their precipices, never a shadow deepened, and Alveric learned that for nothing that happens here is any change in the enchanted lands.

===Chinese===
当夕阳在阿尔韦里克身后落下，他望着那淡蓝色的群山，想看看它们的峰巅会以何等色彩使那傍晚为之惊艳；然而它们不曾从落日那里染上哪怕一丝色调——那落日的辉煌正将我们所知的原野一一镀金；它们的峭壁之上不曾有一道皱褶褪去，不曾有一道阴影加深，阿尔韦里克由此明白：此间发生的任何事，皆不能使那施过魔法的土地有丝毫改变。

===Original===
He turned his eyes from their serene pale beauty back to the fields we know. And there, with their gables lifting into the sunlight above deep hedgerows beautiful with spring, he saw the cottages of earthly men. Past them he walked while the beauty of evening grew, with songs of birds, and scents wandering from flowers, and odours that deepened and deepened, and evening decked herself to receive the Evening Star. But before that star appeared the young adventurer found the cottage he sought; for, flapping above its doorway, he saw the sign of huge brown hide with outlandish letters in gilt which proclaimed the dweller below to be a worker in leather.

===Chinese===
他将目光从它们那宁静淡雅之美收回，重又投向我们所知的原野。只见在那一片春色秀美的深树篱之上，一座座人世农家的小茅屋将其山墙探入阳光之中。他从这些茅屋旁走过，傍晚的美渐次浓盛，有鸟雀之歌，有花朵间飘来的幽香，有愈发愈发深沉的气味——傍晚正盛装打扮，以迎候那颗昏星（Evening Star）。然而在那颗星升起之前，这位年轻的冒险者已寻到了他要找的那间茅屋；因为他看见门前飘挂着一块硕大的棕色兽皮招牌，上面用镀金的怪异字母标明，住在下面的是一位皮匠（worker in leather）。

===Original===
An old man came to the door when Alveric knocked, little and bent with age, and he bent more when Alveric named himself. And the young man asked for a scabbard for his sword, yet said not what sword it was. And they both went into the cottage where the old wife was, by her big fire, and the couple did honour to Alveric. The old man then sat down near his thick table, whose surface shone with smoothness wherever it was not pitted by little tools that had drilled through pieces of leather all that man’s lifetime and in the times of his fathers. And then he laid the sword upon his knees and wondered at the roughness of hilt and guard, for they were raw unworked metal, and at the huge width of the sword; and then he screwed up his eyes and began to think of his trade. And in a while he thought out what must be done; and his wife brought him a fine hide; and he marked out on it two pieces as wide as the sword, and a bit wider than that.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克叩门时，一个年老背驼的小老头出来应门，待阿尔韦里克报上姓名，他腰背弯得更深了。这年轻人要为他的剑求一个剑鞘（scabbard），却不说那是一柄什么剑。两人一同进了茅屋，屋里老妻正守着一炉旺火，老两口对阿尔韦里克礼敬有加。那老人随即在厚实的桌旁坐下，桌面被打磨得光滑发亮——凡是用那些小工具一辈辈在他父祖之时、在他这一辈子钻穿皮件所留下的坑洼之外，无一不磨得锃亮。然后他把那剑横在膝上，惊诧于那剑柄与护手的粗砺，因为那是未经锻冶的生铁；又惊诧于那剑身之宽阔；他眯起眼睛，开始琢磨自己的手艺。过了一会儿，他便想出了该怎么下手；他的妻子给他拿来一张好皮子；他在皮子上划出两片与剑身同宽、比剑身略宽一点的形状。

===Original===
And any questions he asked concerning that wide bright sword Alveric somewhat parried, for he wished not to perplex his mind by telling him all that it was: he perplexed that old couple enough a little later when he asked them for lodging for the night. And this they gave him with as many apologies as if it were they that had asked a favour, and gave him a great supper out of their cauldron, in which boiled everything that the old man snared; but nothing that Alveric was able to say prevented them giving up their bed to him and preparing a heap of skins for their own night’s rest by the fire.

===Chinese===
至于老人就那又宽又亮的剑所问的种种，阿尔韦里克皆支吾以对，因为他不愿将此剑的来历尽数相告，徒然搅扰老人的心思：其实不久之后他便已让这对老夫妇大惑不解——因为他开口向他们借宿一夜。他们应承下来，连声致歉，仿佛求人的反倒是他；又从他们的大锅里给他盛了一顿丰盛的晚饭，那锅里煮着老汉所猎获的一切；然而无论阿尔韦里克如何推辞，都拦不住他们把床让给他，又为自己在炉火边铺下一堆皮子，好歇息过夜。

===Original===
And after their supper the old man cut out the two wide pieces of leather with a point at the end of each and began to stitch them together on each side. And then Alveric began to ask him of the way, and the old leather-worker spoke of north and south and west and even of northeast, but of east or southeast he spoke never a word. He dwelt near the very edge of the fields we know, yet of any hint of anything lying beyond them he or his wife said nothing. Where Alveric’s journey lay upon the morrow they seemed to think the world ended.

===Chinese===
晚饭过后，老人将那两片宽阔的皮料各在一端剪出尖角，开始沿两侧把它们缝合起来。这时阿尔韦里克便向他打听前路，老皮匠说北说南说西，甚至说到东北，却对东或东南只字不提。他所居之处已近我们所知原野的真正边缘，然而对于那之外存在着什么的任何暗示，他与妻子都三缄其口。在阿尔韦里克明日所要前往的方向上，他们似乎认为那里便是世界的尽头。

===Original===
And pondering afterwards, in the bed they gave him, all that the old man had said, Alveric sometimes marvelled at his ignorance, and yet sometimes wondered if it might have been skill by which those two had avoided all the evening any word of anything lying to the east or southeast of their home. He wondered if in his early days the old man might have gone there, but he was unable even to wonder what he had found there if he had gone. Then Alveric fell asleep, and dreams gave him hints and guesses of the old man’s wanderings in Fairyland, but gave him no better guides than he had already, and these were the pale-blue peaks of the Elfin Mountains.

===Chinese===
后来阿尔韦里克躺在他们让给他的床上，细细回味老人方才所说的一切，有时惊异于他的懵懂无知，有时又怀疑：这对老夫妇一整个晚上绝口不提所居之地以东或东南的任何事物，莫非正是出于一种心机？他暗想老人年轻时会否也曾去过那里，但倘若他真的去过，他在那里究竟见到了什么——这件事阿尔韦里克甚至连揣想都无从揣想。随后阿尔韦里克沉沉睡去，梦境给了他些许暗示与猜测，关于那老人在精灵国（Fairyland）的游历，但梦也并未给他比他已有的更好的向导，而那向导便是精灵山脉（the Elfin Mountains）淡蓝色的诸峰。

===Original===
The old man woke him after he had slept long. When he came to the day-room a bright fire was burning there, his breakfast was ready for him and the scabbard made, which fitted the sword exactly. The old people waited on him silently and took payment for the scabbard, but would not take aught for their hospitality. Silently they watched him rise to go, and followed him without a word to the door, and outside it watched him still, clearly hoping that he would turn to the north or west; but when he turned and strode for the Elfin Mountains, they watched him no more, for their faces never were turned that way. And though they watched him no longer yet he waved his hand in farewell; for he had a feeling for the cottages and fields of these simple folk, such as they had not for the enchanted lands. He walked in the sparkling morning through scenes familiar from infancy; he saw the ruddy orchis flowering early, reminding the bluebells they were just past their prime; the small young leaves of the oak were yet a brownish yellow; the new beech-leaves shone like brass, where the cuckoo was calling clearly; and a birch tree looked like a wild woodland creature that had draped herself in green gauze; on favoured bushes there were buds of may. Alveric said over and over to himself farewell to all these things: the cuckoo went on calling, and not for him. And then, as he pushed through a hedge into a field untended, there suddenly close before him in the field was, as his father had told, the frontier of twilight. It stretched across the fields in front of him, blue and dense like water; and things seen through it seemed misshapen and shining. He looked back once over the fields we know; the cuckoo went on calling unconcernedly; a small bird sang about its own affairs; and, nothing seeming to answer or heed his farewells, Alveric strode on boldly into those long masses of twilight.

===Chinese===
待他酣睡一场之后，老人将他唤醒。他走进外间屋，那里已燃起明亮的炉火，早饭为他备好了，剑鞘也已做成，恰好与那剑严丝合缝。两位老人默然侍奉他用饭，收下了剑鞘的酬资，对他的一饭一宿却分文不取。他们默默看他起身欲行，一言不发地送到门口，门外仍目送着他，分明巴望着他折向北方或西方；可当他转身朝着精灵山脉大步而去，他们便不再看了，因为他们的脸从不朝那个方向。虽然他们已不再看他，他仍挥了挥手作别；因为他心中对这对朴实村人的茅屋与田地自有情愫，正如他们对于那施过魔法的土地所无的那种情愫。他在那熠熠生辉的清晨走着，一路景物自幼便熟识；他看见红艳的兰花（orchis）早早开了，像是在提醒蓝风信花它们的好时节已过；橡树初生的小叶还是带褐的黄色；新发的山毛榉叶子亮如黄铜，杜鹃（cuckoo）正在那里清清亮亮地啼叫；一棵白桦看上去像一头林间的野物，给自己披上了一袭绿纱；得地气的灌木丛上已结了五月花（may）的蕾。阿尔韦里克一遍又一遍地在心里向这一切道别：杜鹃依旧啼着，却不是为他。然后，当他拨开一道树篱走进一片荒芜的田地时，正如他父亲所言，那暮色的边境（the frontier of twilight）忽然就在他面前近在咫尺。它横亘在他前面的田野上，蓝色而浓稠，如水一般；透过它所见之物，皆似扭曲而闪光。他回首向我们所知的原野望了一眼；杜鹃依旧若无其事地啼着；一只小鸟正唱着自己的一桩琐事；而既然一切都仿佛并不回应、也不留心他的道别，阿尔韦里克便大步踏入了那一片绵长的暮霭之中。

===Original===
A man in a field not far was calling to horses, there were folk talking in a neighbouring lane, as Alveric stepped into the rampart of twilight; at once all these sounds grew dim, humming faintly, as from great distances: in a few strides he was through, and not a murmur at all came then from the fields we know. The fields through which he had come had suddenly ended; there was no trace of its hedges bright with new green; he looked back, and the frontier seemed lowering, cloudy and smoky; he looked all round and saw no familiar thing; in the place of the beauty of May were the wonders and splendours of Elfland.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克踏入那暮色的壁垒（rampart of twilight）之时，不远处一块田里有人正在吆喝马匹，邻近的小径上有人正闲谈；这些声响顷刻间都黯淡下去，嗡嗡地微响，仿佛来自极远之地：再走几步他便穿了过去，那时我们所知的原野上便连一丝低语也传不过来了。他方才所经的田野忽然到了尽头；那些亮着新绿的新篱已无踪影；他回首望去，只见那边境阴沉低压，云雾蒸腾；他环顾四周，不见一物是熟识的；五月的美所在之处，如今是精灵国（Elfland）的奇景与辉煌。

===Original===
The pale-blue mountains stood august in their glory, shimmering and rippling in a golden light that seemed as though it rhythmically poured from the peaks and flooded all those slopes with breezes of gold. And below them, far off as yet, he saw going up all silver into the air the spires of the palace only told of in song. He was on a plain on which the flowers were queer and the shape of the trees monstrous. He started at once toward the silver spires.

===Chinese===
那淡蓝色的群山以它们的辉煌巍然而立，在一片金色的光辉中摇漾荡漾——那光辉仿佛有节律地从诸峰倾泻而下，以一阵阵金色的微风漫过所有的山坡。在群山之下，尚隔很远，他看见那仅在歌谣中被传唱过的宫殿的尖塔，通体银白地升入空中。他所立之处是一片原野，其上花朵奇诡，树木形状怪异。他当下便朝着那些银色的尖塔进发。

===Original===
To those who may have wisely kept their fancies within the boundary of the fields we know it is difficult for me to tell of the land to which Alveric had come, so that in their minds they can see that plain with its scattered trees and far off the dark wood out of which the palace of Elfland lifted those glittering spires, and above them and beyond them that serene range of mountains whose pinnacles took no colour from any light we see. Yet it is for this very purpose that our fancies travel far, and if my reader through fault of mine fail to picture the peaks of Elfland my fancy had better have stayed in the fields we know. Know then that in Elfland are colours more deep than are in our fields, and the very air there glows with so deep a lucency that all things seen there have something of the look of our trees and flowers in June reflected in water. And the colour of Elfland, of which I despaired to tell, may yet be told, for we have hints of it here; the deep blue of the night in summer just as the gloaming has gone, the pale blue of Venus flooding the evening with light, the deeps of lakes in the twilight, all these are hints of that colour. And while our sunflowers carefully turned to the sun, some forefather of the rhododendrons must have turned a little towards Elfland, so that some of that glory dwells with them to this day. And, above all, our painters have had many a glimpse of that country, so that sometimes in pictures we see a glamour too wonderful for our fields; it is a memory of theirs that intruded from some old glimpse of the pale-blue mountains while they sat at easels painting the fields we know.

===Chinese===
对那些明智地将自己的奇思妙想约束在我们所知原野之界限内的人而言，我实在难以向他们形容阿尔韦里克所来到的这片国土，好让他们在心中看见那片疏落着树木的原野，看见远处那片幽暗的森林——精灵国的宫殿正是从其中擎起那些闪烁的尖塔——以及那在尖塔之上、尖塔之外的那一脉宁静的群山，其峰巅不为我们所见的任何光芒着色。然而我们的奇思之所以远游，正为的便是此目的；倘若我的读者因我之过而不能在心中描摹出精灵国之峰，那么我的奇思倒不如老老实实留在我们所知的原野里为好。那么须知，精灵国里的颜色比我们原野上的颜色更深；那里的空气本身就辉耀着一种极深的清光，使其中所见的一切，都带了几分我们六月里的树与花倒映在水中的模样。而精灵国的颜色——我本已绝望于言说——却仍是可以言说的，因为我们在此间也能寻见它的几分暗示：夏夜薄暮方逝之时那种深蓝，金星（Venus）那淡蓝的光漫满傍晚，暮色里湖水的深蓝，这一切都是那颜色的暗示。而我们的向日葵固然一丝不苟地朝着太阳，可杜鹃花（rhododendrons）的某位先祖，想必曾微微侧首朝着精灵国，所以那一份光辉至今仍栖息在它们身上。更有甚者，我们的画家们也曾屡屡瞥见那片国土，所以有时在画作中我们可见一种过于瑰丽、不属我们原野的神魅（glamour）；那是他们的一段记忆，是从某次对那淡蓝色群山的旧时一瞥中闯入的——彼时他们正坐在画架前，画着我们所知的原野。

===Original===
So Alveric strode on through the luminous air of that land whose glimpses dimly remembered are inspirations here. And at once he felt less lonely. For there is a barrier in the fields we know, drawn sharply between men and all other life, so that if we be but a day away from our kind we are lonely; but once across the boundary of twilight and Alveric saw this barrier was down. Crows walking on the moor looked whimsically at him, all manner of little creatures peered curiously to see who was come from a quarter whence so few ever came; to see who went on a journey whence so few ever returned; for the King of Elfland guarded his daughter well, as Alveric knew although he knew not how. There was a merry sparkle of interest in all those little eyes, and a look that might mean warning.

===Chinese===
于是阿尔韦里克在那片国土熠熠的空气中大步前行——那片国土朦胧地为人所记的一二瞥，在此间便是灵感的源头。他当下便觉得不再那么孤单了。因为在我们所知的原野上有一道藩篱，鲜明地将人与其余一切生灵隔开，是以我们只要离开同类一日，便觉孤寂；然而一过暮色的边境，阿尔韦里克便见这道藩篱已轰然倒塌。荒原上踱步的乌鸦古怪地瞧着他，各种各样的小生灵都好奇地窥探，要看一看是谁从一个极少有人来的地方来到了这里；要看一看是谁踏上了一条极少有人归来的旅程；因为精灵国王（the King of Elfland）将他的女儿看守得很严，阿尔韦里克虽不知其法，却知其事。所有那些小眼睛里都闪烁着一种兴味盎然的欢快，还有一种或许意味警告的神情。

===Original===
There was perhaps less mystery here than on our side of the boundary of twilight; for nothing lurked or seemed to lurk behind great boles of oak, as in certain lights and seasons things may lurk in the fields we know; no strangeness hid on the far side of ridges; nothing haunted deep woods; whatever might possibly lurk was clearly there to be seen, whatever strangeness might be was spread in full sight of the traveller, whatever might haunt deep woods lived there in the open day.

===Chinese===
此处的神秘或许反倒不如暮色边境我们这一边为多；因为橡树粗大的树干之后，并无一物潜伏，或似有潜伏——在我们所知的原野上，在某些光线、某些时节，是可能有物潜伏于树后的；山脊的那一边也不曾藏着什么怪异；深林之中亦无物作祟；凡可能潜伏之物皆显然在那里一览无遗，凡可能有的怪异皆在旅人眼前铺陈无遗，凡可能出没于深林之物皆在那里生活在光天化日之下。

===Original===
And, so strong lay the enchantment deep over all that land, that not only did beasts and men guess each other’s meanings well, but there seemed to be an understanding even, that reached from men to trees and from trees to men. Lonely pine trees that Alveric passed now and then on the moor, their trunks glowing always with the ruddy light that they had got by magic from some old sunset, seemed to stand with their branches akimbo and lean over a little to look at him. It seemed almost as though they had not always been trees, before enchantment had overtaken them there; it seemed they would tell him something.

===Chinese===
而那魔法深深地笼罩着整片土地，其力如此之强，以至于不仅兽与人能彼此会意，甚至似乎还有一种默契，从人达于树，又从树达于人。阿尔韦里克在荒原上时或经过的孤松，它们的树干始终辉耀着一抹红光——那是它们以魔法从某一道古老的夕阳那里得来的——它们似乎叉着枝站立着，微微倾身看着他。几乎像是在被魔法追上此地之前，它们并不一直是树似的；似乎它们想告诉他些什么。

===Original===
But Alveric heeded no warnings either from beasts or trees, and strode away toward the enchanted wood.

===Chinese===
然而阿尔韦里克不顾兽的、也不顾树的任何警告，大步朝那片施过魔法的森林（the enchanted wood）走去。

## Dedication To Lady Dunsany / 题献 献给邓萨尼夫人

## IX Lirazel Blows Away / 九 莉拉泽尔乘风而去

===Original===
And the days went by, the summer passed over Erl, the sun that had travelled northward fared south again, it was near to the time when the swallows left those eaves, and Lirazel had not learnt anything. She had not prayed to the stars again, or supplicated their images, but she had learned no human customs, and could not see why her love and gratitude must remain unexpressed to the stars. And Alveric did not know that the time must come when some simple trivial thing would divide them utterly.

===Chinese===
时日推移，夏日从厄尔的上空过去，那曾北行的太阳复又南归，距燕子离开那些屋檐的时辰已近，而莉拉泽尔什么也未曾学得。她未再向群星祈祷，也未再向星象恳求，可她也未学得任何人间的习俗，仍不明白为何她对群星的爱与感激必须深藏不宣。而阿尔韦里克尚不知晓，那一日终将到来——某件简简单单、微不足道的小事，便会将他们彻底拆散。

===Original===
And then one day, hoping still, he took her with him to the house of the Freer to teach her how to worship his holy things. And gladly the good man brought his candle and bell, and the eagle of brass that held up his book when he read, and a little symbolic bowl that had scented water, and the silver snuffers that put his candle out. And he told her clearly and simply, as he had told her before, the origin, meaning and mystery of all these things, and why the bowl was of brass and the snuffer of silver, and what the symbols were that were carved on the bowl. With fitting courtesy he told her these things, even with kindness; and yet there was something in his voice as he told, a little distant from her; and she knew that he spoke as one that walked safe on shore calling far to a mermaid amid dangerous seas.

===Chinese===
后来有一日，他心存指望，便带她一同去教士的居所，好教她如何敬奉他的圣物。那善良之人欣然取来他的蜡烛与铜铃，取来那只他诵经时为他承托经书的铜鹰，取来那只盛着芳香水液的小小象征之碗，以及那剪灭烛火的银质烛剪。他如先前那般清清楚楚、明明白白地向她讲述这一切事物的本源、含义与奥秘，讲述那碗何以是铜的、那烛剪何以是银的，又讲述碗上所刻的种种符号究竟何所指。他以相当的礼数讲述这些，甚至带着和善；然而他讲述之时的声口里仍有某样东西，与她略有些疏远；她明白，他说话的样子，恰如一个安然行于岸上的人，远远地向身处危海之中的人鱼高声呼唤。

===Original===
As they came back to the castle the swallows were grouped to go, sitting in lines along the battlements. And Lirazel had promised to worship the holy things of the Freer, like the simple bell-fearing folk of the valley of Erl: and a late hope was shining in Alveric’s mind that even yet all was well. And for many days she remembered all that the Freer had told her.

===Chinese===
他们回到城堡之时，燕群已三五成群地聚拢，预备启程，一排排地栖在雉堞之上。莉拉泽尔已应允要敬奉教士的圣物，一如厄尔之谷里那淳朴的畏钟之民：于是一线迟来的希望在阿尔韦里克心中闪亮起来，以为到头来一切都好。其后许多日子，她都记得教士对她讲过的那一番话。

===Original===
And one day walking late from the nursery, past tall windows to her tower, and looking out on the evening, remembering that she must not worship the stars, she called to mind the holy things of the Freer, and tried to remember all she was told of them. It seemed so hard to worship them just as she should. She knew that before many hours the swallows would all be gone; and often when they left her her mood would change; and she feared that she might forget, and never remember more, how she ought to worship the holy things of the Freer.

===Chinese===
一日，她从育婴室晚归，走过那一扇扇高窗，朝她的塔楼走去，望着窗外的暮色，想起自己不可再去敬奉群星，便在心中默念教士的圣物，竭力回想人家所告诉她的关于那些圣物的一切。要恰如其分地敬奉它们，竟显得如此之难。她知道用不了几个时辰，燕群便将尽去；而每逢它们离她而去，她的心绪便要变上一变；她唯恐自己会忘却，再也记不起当如何敬奉教士的圣物。

===Original===
So she went out into the night again over the grasses to where a thin brook ran, and drew out some great flat pebbles that she knew where to find, turning her face away from the images of the stars. By day the stones shone beautifully in the water, all ruddy and mauve; now they were all dark. She drew them out and laid them in the meadow: she loved these smooth flat stones, for somehow they made her remember the rocks of Elfland.

===Chinese===
于是她又在夜色里走出去，踏过青草，来到一道细细的溪流之畔，从水中摸出几枚她知其所在的大而扁平的卵石，背过脸去，不望那群星的影像。白日里，这些石头在水中美丽地闪烁，红的红，紫的紫；此刻却尽是黝暗。她将它们一一取出，铺在草地上：她爱这些光滑的扁石，因为它们不知怎的总使她想起精灵国的岩石。

===Original===
She laid them all in a row, this for the candlestick, this for the bell, that for the holy bowl. “If I can worship these lovely stones as things ought to be worshipped,” she said, “I can worship the things of the Freer.”

Then she kneeled down before the big flat stones and prayed to them as though they were Christom things.

And Alveric seeking her in the wide night, wondering what wild fancy had carried her whither, heard her voice in the meadow, crooning such prayers as are offered to holy things.

When he saw the four flat stones to which she prayed, bowed down before them in the grass, he said that no worse than this were the darkest ways of the heathen. And she said “I am learning to worship the holy things of the Freer.”

“It is the art of the heathen,” he said.

===Chinese===
她把它们一字排开，这一枚代烛台，这一枚代铜铃，那一枚代圣碗。「我若能如敬奉万物所当受的那样来敬奉这些可爱的石头，」她说，「便能敬奉教士的圣物了。」

于是她跪在那几枚大扁石之前，向它们祈祷，仿佛它们便是基督圣物。

阿尔韦里克在这苍茫夜色中寻她，不知是哪一桩狂放的奇思将她带往了那里，却听见她的声音从草地上传来，正哼唱着那般献给圣物的祷词。

他看见她匍匐在草中，向着她所祈祷的那四枚扁石叩拜，便说异教徒那些最幽暗的作为，也不过恶于此了。而她说：「我正在学习敬奉教士的圣物。」

「那是异教之术。」他说。

===Original===
Now of all things that men feared in the valley of Erl they feared most the arts of the heathen, of whom they knew nothing but that their ways were dark. And he spoke with the anger which men always used when they spoke there of the heathen. And his anger went to her heart, for she was but learning to worship his holy things to please him, and yet he had spoken like this.

And Alveric would not speak the words that should have been said, to turn aside anger and soothe her; for no man, he foolishly thought, should compromise in matters touching on heathenesse. So Lirazel went alone all sadly back to her tower. And Alveric stayed to cast the four flat stones afar.

===Chinese===
原来厄尔之谷里人们所畏惧的种种事物之中，他们最畏的是异教之术，对于异教徒他们一无所知，只知其道幽暗。他便带着此地人们谈及异教徒时向来所带的那股怒气说话。而他的怒气直刺她的心，因为她原不过是为了取悦于他，才学习敬奉他的圣物，他却如此相待。

阿尔韦里克也不肯说出那本该说出口的话，以化解怒气、抚慰于她；因为他愚拙地以为，凡涉及异教之乡（heathenesse）之事，凡男人皆不可退让。于是莉拉泽尔独自一人，满怀哀伤，回到她的塔楼。阿尔韦里克则留下来，将那四枚扁石远远掷开。

===Original===
And the swallows left, and unhappy days went by. And one day Alveric bade her worship the holy things of the Freer, and she had quite forgotten how. And he spoke again of the arts of heathenesse. The day was shining and the poplars golden and all the aspens red.

Then Lirazel went to her tower and opened the casket, that shone in the morning with the clear autumnal light, and took in her hand the rune of the King of Elfland, and carried it with her across the high vaulted hall, and came to another tower and climbed its steps to the nursery.

===Chinese===
于是燕群去了，愁苦的日子一日日过去。一日阿尔韦里克命她敬奉教士的圣物，而她竟已将如何敬奉忘得一干二净。他便又说起那异教之乡之术。那一日天光明朗，白杨金黄，山楮尽赤。

于是莉拉泽尔回到她的塔楼，打开那只匣子——那匣子在清晨澄澈的秋光之中熠熠生辉——将精灵国王的符文握在手中，携着它穿过那高高的拱顶大厅，来到另一座塔楼，攀其阶而上，到了育婴室。

===Original===
And there all day she stayed and played with her child, with the scroll still tight in her hand: and, merrily though she played at whiles, yet there were strange calms in her eyes, which Ziroonderel watched while she wondered. And when the sun was low and she had put the child to bed she sat beside him all solemn as she told him childish tales. And Ziroonderel, the wise witch, watched; and for all her wisdom only guessed how it would be, and knew not how to make it otherwise.

===Chinese===
她整日待在那里与她的孩子嬉戏，那一卷羊皮纸仍紧紧握在手中：她虽不时嬉笑欢闹，眼中却有一种奇异的沉静——泽龙德尔（Ziroonderel）在一旁看着，心中暗暗称奇。日落时分，她将孩子安顿睡下，便坐在他身旁，一脸肃穆地给他讲些稚气的旧事。那聪慧的女巫泽龙德尔看着；凭她一切智慧，也只能猜到事情将如何，却不知如何使之另作他样。

===Original===
And before sunset Lirazel kissed the boy and unrolled the Elf King’s scroll. It was but a petulance that had made her take it from the coffer in which it lay, and the petulance might have passed and she might not have unrolled the scroll, only that it was there in her hand. Partly petulance, partly wonder, partly whims too idle to name, drew her eyes to the Elf King’s words in their coal-black curious characters.

===Chinese===
日落之前，莉拉泽尔吻了吻那孩子，展开了精灵国王的那卷羊皮。当初使她从匣中取出这卷轴的，不过是一桩任性的小情绪，那任性本可消散，她也本可不展开这卷轴，只是它此刻正在她手中。一半是任性，一半是好奇，再加上些懒得去命名的闲愁闲绪，吸引她的目光投向那精灵国王的字句——它们以一种煤玉般乌黑的古怪字符写成。

===Original===
And whatever magic there was in the rune of which I cannot tell (and dreadful magic there was), the rune was written with love that was stronger than magic, till those mystical characters glowed with the love that the Elf King had for his daughter, and there were blended in that mighty rune two powers, magic and love, the greatest power there is beyond the boundary of twilight with the greatest power there is in the fields we know. And if Alveric’s love could have held her he should have trusted alone to that love, for the Elf King’s rune was mightier than the holy things of the Freer.

===Chinese===
无论这道符文之中含着我所不能言说的何种魔法（而其中确有可怖的魔法），这道符文都是以比魔法更为强烈的爱心写就的，以至那些神秘的字符都因精灵国王对女儿的爱而灼灼生辉；在这道宏大的符文里，融合着两种力量——魔法与爱——一种是暮色之境外最伟大的力量，一种是我们所知的原野上最伟大的力量。倘若阿尔韦里克的爱本可留住她，他便该独自信托那份爱，因为精灵国王的符文，比教士的圣物更为强大。

===Original===
No sooner had Lirazel read the rune on the scroll than fancies from Elfland began to pour over the border. Some came that would make a clerk in the city today leave his desk at once to dance on the seashore; and some would have driven all the men in a bank to leave doors and coffers open and wander away till they came to green open land and the heathery hills; and some would have made a poet of a man, all of a sudden as he sat at his business. They were mighty fancies that the Elf King summoned by the force of his magical rune. And Lirazel sat there with the rune in her hand, helpless amongst this mass of tumultuous fancies from Elfland. And as the fancies raged and sang and called, more and more over the border, all crowding on one poor mind, her body grew lighter and lighter. Her feet half rested half floated, upon the floor; Earth scarcely held her down, so fast was she becoming a thing of dreams. No love of hers for Earth, or of the children of Earth for her, had any longer power to hold her there.

===Chinese===
莉拉泽尔一读卷上之符文，来自精灵国的种种奇念便开始越境倾注而来。其中有那等奇念，足以使今之城里一介书吏当下便弃其案牍，奔赴海岸翩然起舞；亦有那等奇念，足以驱使一家银行中所有的人敞开门户与金库，信步远去，直至走到绿色的旷野和石楠花盛开的群山之间；又有那等奇念，足以使一个坐着办公的人须臾之间化作一位诗人。这些奇念皆是精灵国王以其魔法符文之力所召来的强大之物。莉拉泽尔手持符文坐在那里，在这堆自精灵国奔涌而至的纷乱奇念之间，已是无可奈何。随着那些奇念愈益汹涌、歌吟、呼唤，越境而来者愈益其多，尽数挤在这一颗可怜的心智之上，她的身躯便愈来愈轻。她的双足半踏半浮于地板之上；人间几乎已留她不住，她正飞速地化作一桩梦中之物。她对人间之爱，或人世之子对她的爱，皆已再无力将她留于此间。

===Original===
And now came memories of her ageless childhood beside the tarns of Elfland, by the deep forest’s border, by those delirious lawns, or in the palace that may not be told of except only in song. She saw those things as clearly as we see small shells in water, looking through clear ice down to the floor of some sleeping lake, a little dimmed in that other region across the barrier of ice; so too her memories shone a little dimly from across the frontier of Elfland. Little queer sounds of elfin creatures came to her, scents swam from those miraculous flowers that glowed by the lawns she knew, faint sounds of enchanted songs blew over the border and reached her seated there, voices and melodies and memories came floating through the twilight, all Elfland was calling. Then measured and resonant, and strangely near, she heard her father’s voice.

===Chinese===
此时她那无年无岁的童年记忆也涌了上来——那是在精灵国的山潭之畔，在幽深森林的边缘，在那些令人心醉神驰的草坪之上，或在那座除歌谣之外不许言说的宫殿之中。她把这些看得清清楚楚，正如我们透过清冰望向某座沉睡湖泊的湖底、能看见水中的小小贝壳一般——那冰之屏障另一侧的事物略略有些朦胧；同样，她的记忆从精灵国边境的另一侧辉耀过来，也略略有些昏暗。精灵之物的细小古怪的声息传到她耳中，奇异的香气从她所熟悉的草坪之畔那些灼灼生辉的奇迹之花间游来，附魔之歌的隐约声响越境而至，飘到端坐着的她身边——嗓音、旋律与记忆穿过暮霭飘来，整片精灵国都在呼唤。于是她听见了父亲的声音，沉稳、洪亮，又奇异地近。

===Original===
She rose at once, and now Earth had lost on her the grip that it only has on material things, and a thing of dreams and fancy and fable and fantasy she drifted from the room; and Ziroonderel had no power to hold her with any spell, nor had she herself the power even to turn, even to look at her boy as she drifted away.

===Chinese===
她应声而起，此刻人间已失去了对她那唯对物质之物才有的握持之力，她作为一个由梦、幻想、传说与奇思所成之物，飘然离开了房间；泽龙德尔并无任何咒术可以留住她，而她自己亦无力回首，无力在飘然而去之际望一眼她的孩子。

===Original===
And at that moment a wind came out of the northwest, and entered the woods and bared the golden branches, and danced on over the downs, and led a company of scarlet and golden leaves, that had dreaded this day but danced now it had come; and away with a riot of dancing and glory of colour, high in the light of the sun that had set from the sight of the fields, went wind and leaves together. With them went Lirazel.

===Chinese===
就在那一刻，一阵风自西北而起，闯入林中，剥落了金黄的枝柯，又舞着越过丘陵，引领起一队赤红与金黄的落叶——这些叶子本畏惧这一日的到来，而今既已到来，便翩然起舞；于是乎，在已从这片原野的视野中沉落的太阳之余光里，带着一场舞蹈的狂欢与色彩的辉煌，风与叶一同远去。与它们同去的，还有莉拉泽尔。

## XXV Lirazel Remembers the Fields We Know / 二十五 莉拉泽尔想起我们所知的原野

===Original===
As the trolls scurried earthwards to laugh at the ways of man, Lirazel stirred where she sat on her father’s knee, who grave and calm on his throne of mist and ice had hardly moved for twelve of our earthly years. She sighed and the sigh rippled over the fells of dream and lightly troubled Elfland. And the dawns and the sunsets and twilight and the pale blue glow of stars, that are blended together forever to be the light of Elfland, felt a faint touch of sorrow and all their radiance shook. For the magic that caught these lights and the spells that bound them together, to illumine forever the land that owes no allegiance to Time, were not so strong as a sorrow rising dark from a royal mood of a princess of the elvish line. She sighed, for through her long content and across the calm of Elfland there had floated a thought of Earth; so that in the midmost splendours of Elfland, of which song can barely tell, she called to mind common cowslips, and many a trivial weed of the fields we know. And walking in those fields she saw in fancy Orion, upon the other side of the boundary of twilight, remote from her by she knew not what waste of years. And the magical glories of Elfland and its beauty beyond our dreaming, and the deep deep calm in which ages slept, unhurt unhurried by time, and the art of her father that guarded the least of the lilies from fading, and the spells by which he made daydreams and yearnings true, held her fancy no longer from roving nor contented her any more. And so her sigh blew over the magical land and slightly troubled the flowers.

===Chinese===
正当那些巨怪急急奔向人间去哂笑世人行事之际，莉拉泽尔在父亲膝上挪动了一下身子；她父亲庄重而安详地坐在薄雾与冰之宝座上，在我们人间的十二个年头里几乎未曾动过分毫。她叹了一口气，那声叹息荡过梦之群山，轻轻地搅扰了精灵国。于是那黎明、日落、暮色与群星淡蓝色的幽光——它们永远交融在一起，化作精灵国不灭的光——也感到了一丝淡淡的哀伤，它们所有的辉光都为之颤动。因为那些捕捉这些光华的魔法与将它们系结于一处的咒语，本是为了永远照亮那不向时间称臣的国度，却敌不过一道从精灵皇室公主的尊贵心绪中幽幽升起的悲伤。她叹息，是因为穿过她久长的满足与精灵国的宁静，飘来了一缕关于人间的思念；以至于在这连歌谣都难以尽述的、精灵国最中心的辉煌之中，她想起了寻常的黄花九轮草，想起了我们所知原野上许多微不足道的野草。当她在那些原野上漫步时，她在遐想中看见了奥利安，他在暮色边境的另一侧，与她相隔着不知多少荒芜的岁月。而精灵国那魔法的荣耀、那超越我们梦境的美、那众时代在其中沉睡的极深极深的静谧——不为时间所伤、不被时间所催——以及她父亲护住最小一朵百合不使凋谢的法术、他使白日梦与渴念成真的咒语，再也不能拴住她的遐想不让它远游，再也不能使她满足。于是她的叹息吹过那片魔法之国，轻轻地惊动了那些花朵。

===Original===
And her father felt her sorrow and knew that it troubled the flowers and knew that it shook the calm that lay upon Elfland, though no more than a bird would shake a regal curtain, fluttering against its folds, when wandering lost upon a summer’s night. And though he knew too it was but for Earth that she sorrowed, preferring some mundane way to the midmost glories of Elfland, as she sat with him on the throne that may only be told of in song, yet even this moved nothing in his magical heart but compassion; as we might pity a child who in fanes that to us seemed sacred might be found to be sighing for some trivial thing. And the more that Earth seemed to him unworthy of sorrow, being soon come soon gone, the helpless prey of time, an evanescent appearance seen off the coasts of Elfland, too brief for the graver care of a mind weighted with magic, the more he pitied his child for her errant whim that had rashly wandered here, and become entangled—alas—with the things that pass away. Ah, well! she was not content. He felt no wrath against Earth that had lured her fancies away: she was not content with the innermost splendours of Elfland, but she sighed for something more: his tremendous art should give it. So he raised his right arm up from the thing whereon it rested, a part of his mystical throne that was made of music and mirage; he raised his right arm up and a hush fell over Elfland.

===Chinese===
她的父亲感到了她的悲伤，知道它惊动了花朵，知道它震动了笼罩在精灵国之上的静谧——虽然那震动也不过像一只夏夜迷途的鸟儿扑打着翅翼撞在王帐的褶皱上所引起的那般轻微。他虽也知道她正是为了人间而悲伤，与他一同坐在那只能以歌谣传颂的宝座上，竟宁愿取那尘俗之道而不取精灵国最中心的辉煌，然而即便如此，在他那魔法之心里所撼动的，也只有怜悯而已；正如我们怜悯一个孩子，在我们看来神圣的殿宇之中，竟被发现正为某件琐事叹息。人间越是在他看来不值得悲伤——来去匆匆，是时间无助的猎物，不过是精灵国海滨之外一抹转瞬即逝的幻影，对于一个负着魔法之重、心思庄重的心灵而言过于短促，不值深忧——他便越发怜悯他的孩子，怜悯她那任性胡为、轻率地漂泊到此处、竟与那些终将消逝之物（唉）纠缠在一起的痴念。罢了，罢了！她并不满足。他对那引走她遐思的人间并不动怒：她既不甘于精灵国最深处的辉煌，便仍为别物叹息：他那无上的法术该当予她以满足。于是他将右臂从所凭倚之物——他那神秘宝座的一部分，由乐音与蜃景所造——上抬起；他抬起右臂，一片寂静便笼罩了精灵国。

===Original===
The great leaves ceased from their murmur through the green deeps of the forest; silent as carven marble were fabulous bird and monster; and the brown trolls scampering earthwards all halted suddenly hushed. Then out of the hush rose little murmurs of yearning, little sounds as of longing for things that no songs can say, sounds like the voices of tears if each little salt drop could live, and be given a voice to tell of the ways of grief. Then all these little rumours danced gravely into a melody that the master of Elfland called up with his magical hand. And the melody told of dawn coming up over infinite marshes, far away upon Earth or some planet that Elfland did not know; growing slowly out of deep darkness and starlight and bitter cold; powerless, chilly and cheerless, scarce overcoming the stars; obscured by shadows of thunder and hated by all things dark; enduring, growing and glowing; until through the gloom of the marshes and across the chill of the air came all in a glorious moment the splendour of colour; and dawn went onward with this triumphant thing, and the blackest clouds turned slowly rose and rode in a sea of lilac, and the darkest rocks that had guarded night shone now with a golden glow. And when his melody could say no more of this wonder, that had forever been foreign to all the elvish dominions, then the King moved his hand where he held it high, as one might beckon to birds, and called up a dawn over Elfland, luring it from some planet of those that are nearest the sun. And fresh and fair though it came from beyond the bourn of geography, and out of an age long lost and beyond history’s ken, a dawn glowed upon Elfland that had known no dawn before. And the dewdrops of Elfland slung from the bended tips of the grasses gathered in that dawn to their tiny spheres and held there shining and wonderful that glory of skies such as ours, the first they had ever seen.

===Chinese===
巨大的叶片不再在森林翠绿的深处作簌簌的低语；传说中的飞禽与怪兽静默得如同雕成的大理石；棕色皮肤的巨怪们正向着人间一路奔去，此刻也都骤然止步，悄然无声。继而，从那寂静里升起了一缕缕渴慕的低吟，一声声盼念的细响，盼念着那些没有歌谣能诉说之物，声音仿佛是泪珠的语声——倘若每一颗小小的咸泪都能活过来，都被赋予一个嗓音去讲述悲伤的种种路径。随后，所有这些细碎的声息庄重地旋舞成一段旋律，那是精灵国之主以他魔法之手召唤出来的。这段旋律讲述的是黎明在无垠的沼泽上升起，远远地在人间，或在精灵国所不知的某个星球之上；从深深的黑暗、星光与彻骨的严寒中缓缓地生发；孱弱、凄寒、黯淡，几乎压不过群星；为雷影所遮蔽，为一切黑暗之物所憎恶；却又坚忍、生长、发光；直到穿透沼泽的阴霾、越过空气的寒冽，在一个辉煌的瞬间，色彩的壮丽一并降临；黎明便带着这胜利之物向前推进，最黑的云缓缓转作玫瑰之色，泛游于一片丁香色的海中，而那些曾守护黑夜的最暗的岩石，此刻也闪耀出金色的光华。当他的旋律再无可言以形容这等从未属于过任何精灵辖地的奇迹时，国王便移动了他高擎的手，如同有人向群鸟招手一般，在精灵国之上召唤出一道黎明，将它从离太阳最近的某个星球上引了过来。尽管它来自地理之界以外，来自一个早已失落、超出历史所能察知的年代，它却清新而美好：一道精灵国此前从未见识过的黎明，此刻辉耀于精灵国之上。精灵国的露珠从低垂的草尖上凝聚于那道黎明之中，结成它们微小的珠体，在那里晶莹而奇妙地承载起如我们这般天空的辉光——那是它们所曾见过的第一道。

===Original===
And the dawn grew strangely and slowly over those unwonted lands, pouring upon them the colours that day after day our daffodils, and day after day our wild roses, through all the weeks of their season, drink deep with voluptuous assemblies in utterly silent riot. And a gleam that was new to the forest appeared on the long strange leaves, and shadows unknown to Elfland slipped out from the monstrous tree-boles, and stole over grasses that had not dreamed of their advent; and the spires of that palace perceiving a wonder, less lovely indeed than they, yet knew that the stranger was magic, and uttered an answering gleam from their sacred windows, that flashed over elvish fells like an inspiration and mingled a flush of rose with the blue of the Elfin Mountains. And watchers on wonderful peaks that gazed from their crags for ages, lest from Earth or from any star should come a stranger to Elfland, saw the first blush of the sky as it felt the coming of dawn, and raised their horns and blew that call that warned Elfland against a stranger. And the guardians of savage valleys lifted horns of fabulous bulls and blew the call again in the dark of their awful precipices, and echo carried it on from the monstrous marble faces of rocks that repeated the call to all their barbarous company; so Elfland rang with the warning that a strange thing troubled her coasts. And to the land thus expectant, thus watchful, with magical sabres elate along lonely crags, summoned from blackened scabbards by those horns to repel an enemy, dawn came now wide now golden, the old old wonder we know. And the palace with every marvel and with all its charms and enchantments flashed out of its ice-blue radiance a glory of welcome or rivalry, adding to Elfland a splendour of which only song may say.

===Chinese===
那黎明在那些陌生的土地上奇异而缓慢地生长，将色彩倾注于其上——那些色彩，是我们的水仙花日复一日、是我们的野玫瑰在其花季的每一周里，以纵情的相聚在彻底无声的喧腾之中深深痛饮的色彩。一缕林中前所未见的光芒出现在那长长的怪异叶片上；精灵国素所未知的阴影从巨硕的树身里悄悄溜出，潜过那些从未梦见过它们降临的青草；而那宫殿的尖塔感知到一桩奇迹——虽实不如它自身那般美丽——却知道这位来客乃是魔法，便从它神圣的窗中吐出一缕应答的微光，如灵光一闪掠过精灵的群山，将一抹玫瑰色的红晕融进了精灵山脉的蓝。那些在奇妙峰巅之上、从峭壁间凝望了世世代代的守望者——为防人间或任何星辰有异客闯入精灵国——望见天空因感到黎明将至而初泛的红晕，便举起号角，吹响了那警告精灵国防备异客的号声。荒莽山谷的守卫者们举起传说中神牛之角，在他们可怖的悬崖的幽暗之中再次吹响那号声，回声从巨硕的大理石岩面间接力传递，将那号声复述给他们所有粗犷的同伴；于是精灵国到处回荡着那道警报：有一件异物搅扰了她的海滨。于是这片既有所待、又有所备的土地上——魔法之剑在孤峭的巉岩之上一柄柄意气昂扬，被那些号角从熏黑的剑鞘中召出以御外敌——黎明此刻时而辽阔、时而金黄地降临了，那正是我们所知的那古老的、古老的奇迹。而那座宫殿携其一切奇观、一切法术与魅惑，从它冰蓝色的光辉中迸发出一缕欢迎、抑或争胜的壮丽，为精灵国添了一种唯有歌谣方能道尽的辉煌。

===Original===
It was then that the elfin King moved his hand again, where he held it high by the crystal spires of his crown, and waved a way through the walls of his magical palace, and showed to Lirazel the unmeasured leagues of his kingdom. And she saw by magic, for so long as his fingers made that spell; the dark green forests and all the fells of Elfland, and the solemn pale-blue mountains and the valleys that weird folk guarded, and all the creatures of fable that crept in the dark of huge leaves, and the riotous trolls as they scampered away towards Earth: she saw the watchers lift their horns to their lips, while there flashed a light on the horns that was the proudest triumph of the hidden art of her father, the light of a dawn lured over unthinkable spaces to appease his daughter and comfort her whims and recall her fancies from Earth. She saw the lawns whereon Time had idled for centuries, withering not one bloom of all the boundary of flowers; and the new light coming upon the lawns she loved, through the heavy colour of Elfland, gave them a beauty that they had never known until dawn made this boundless journey to meet the enchanted twilight; and all the while there glowed and flashed and glittered those palace spires of which only song may tell. From that bewildering beauty he turned his eyes away, and looked in his daughter’s face to see the wonder with which she would welcome her glorious home as her fancies came back from the fields of age and death, whither—alas—they had wandered. And though her eyes were turned to the Elfin Mountains, whose mystery and whose blue they strangely matched, yet as the Elf King looked in those eyes for which alone he had lured the dawn so far from its natural courses, he saw in their magical deeps a thought of Earth! A thought of Earth, though he had lifted his arm and made a mystical sign with all his might to bring a wonder to Elfland that should content her with home. And all his dominions had exulted in this, and the watchers on awful crags had blown strange calls, and monster and insect and bird and flower had rejoiced with a new joy, and there in the centre of Elfland his daughter thought of Earth.

===Chinese===
正是在那一刻，精灵国王再次移动了他那只手——他高擎于王冠水晶尖塔之旁的手——在他魔法宫殿的墙壁之间挥出一条通路，将他那无边无际的国土展现在莉拉泽尔眼前。趁着他手指结出那道咒术，她借魔法看见了：墨绿的森林与精灵国所有的群山，庄重的淡蓝色山脉与怪异之族所守护的谷地，一切在巨大叶片的幽暗中潜行的传说之兽，以及那些喧腾的巨怪正向着人间一路奔去；她看见守望者们把号角举到唇边，此刻号角上闪烁的那道光，乃是她父亲那隐秘之术最足以自傲的胜利——那是引诱一道黎明横越不可思量之空阔，只为抚慰他的女儿、宽慰她的痴性、将她的人间遐思唤回的光。她看见了那些草坪——时间在其上虚度了几个世纪，却未使一圈花界之中有任何一朵凋萎；而她所爱的那些草坪，被这道新光透过精灵国沉重的色调照亮，显出一种它们此前从未识得的美，直到黎明完成这无边无际的旅程，来与那被施过魔法的暮色相会；而那宫殿的尖塔始终在熠熠生辉、灼灼闪烁、灼灼放光——关于它们，唯歌谣能道。他从那令人迷乱的壮美之中收回目光，望进女儿的脸，想看她将以怎样的惊叹来迎接她那辉煌的家园——此时她的遐思已从衰老与死亡的原野上归来，唉，它们曾游荡到那里。虽然她的双眸转向了精灵山脉，奇怪地与那山脉的神秘、那山脉的蓝色相契合，然而当精灵国王望进那一双眼睛——他正是只为这一双眼睛，才把黎明从它天然的轨迹上引得如此之远——他却在它们魔法的深处看见了一缕关于人间的思念！一缕对人间的思念，尽管他曾举起手臂，拼尽全力结出一个神秘的手印，要为精灵国招来一桩足以令她满足于家园的奇迹。而他所有的辖地都为此欢腾，可怖巉岩上的守望者们吹响了陌生的号声，怪兽、昆虫、飞禽与花朵皆以一种崭新的喜悦为之欢欣，可就在精灵国的正中心，他的女儿心里想的却是人间。

===Original===
Had he shown her any wonder but dawn he might have lured home that fancy, but in bringing this exotic beauty to Elfland to blend with its ancient wonders, he awoke memories of morning coming over fields that he knew not, and Lirazel played in fancy in fields once more with Orion, where grew the unenchanted earthly flowers amongst the English grasses.

“Is it not enough?” he said in his strange rich magical voice, and pointed across his wide lands with the fingers that summoned wonder.

She sighed: it was not enough.

===Chinese===
倘若他给她看的是黎明以外的任何奇迹，他也许本可将那缕遐思引回家中；可是，把这种异域之美带进精灵国、要它与那些古老的奇观相融，他却唤醒了清晨降临在一片他所不知的原野之上的记忆；于是莉拉泽尔又在遐想之中于原野上与奥利安嬉戏起来，在英伦的青草之间，开着那些不曾受过魔法的尘世之花。

「这还不够吗？」他用他那奇异的、丰沛的、魔法般的嗓音说道，以那召唤奇迹的手指，向他广大的国土一指。

她叹息：这还不够。

===Original===
And sorrow came upon that enchanted King: he had only his daughter, and she sighed for Earth. There had been once a queen that had reigned with him over Elfland; but she was mortal, and being mortal died. For she would often stray to the hills of Earth to see the may again, or to see the beechwoods in autumn; and though she stayed but a day when she came to the fields we know, and was back in the palace beyond the twilight before our sun had set, yet Time found her whenever she came; and so she wore away, and soon she died in Elfland; for she was only a mortal. And wondering elves had buried her, as one buries the daughters of men. And now the King was all alone with his daughter, and she had just sighed for Earth. Sorrow was on him, but out of the dark of that sorrow arose, as often with men, and went up singing out of his mourning mind, an inspiration gleaming with laughter and joy. He stood up then and raised up both his arms and his inspiration broke over Elfland in music. And with the tide of that music there went like the strength of the sea an impulse to rise and dance which none in Elfland resisted. Gravely he waved his arms and the music floated from them; and all that stalked through the forest and all that crept upon leaves, all that leaped among craggy heights or browsed upon acres of lilies, all things in all manner of places, yea the sentinel guarding his presence, the lonely mountain-watchers and the trolls as they scampered towards Earth, all danced to a tune that was made of the spirit of spring, arrived on an earthly morning amongst happy herds of goats.

===Chinese===
悲伤于是降临在那位魔法之王的身上：他唯有这一个女儿，而她竟为人间而叹息。曾经有一位王后与他一同统治精灵国；可她是凡人，既为凡人，便终有一死。因为她常常溜到人间的山丘上去再看一看五月花，或去看一看秋日的山毛榉林；虽然她每次到我们所知的原野上来只停留一日，又总在我们的太阳落山之前便回到了暮色那一边的宫殿，可时间却每逢她来便找上她；于是她一点点地消损，不久便死于精灵国之中；因为她只不过是个凡人。惊异的众精灵将她埋葬了，如同人埋葬自己的女儿那样。如今国王孤身一人与他的女儿为伴，而她方才竟为人间而叹息。悲伤笼罩着他，但从那悲伤的幽暗之中，正如人之常情，升起了一个闪烁着欢笑与喜悦的灵感，一路歌唱着从他哀愁的心里飞出。于是他站起身来，高举双臂，他的灵感化作音乐倾泻于精灵国之上。随着那音乐的潮汐，涌起了一股如同大海之力一般、令人要起身舞蹈的冲动，精灵国之中无人能拒。他庄重地挥动双臂，音乐便从其上飘扬而出；一切潜行于森林之中的、一切匍匐于叶片之上的，一切跳跃于嶙峋高处或啮食于成亩百合之间的，一切在一切处所的一切生灵——是的，守卫在他驾前的哨兵，孤独的山巅守望者，以及那向着人间一路奔去的巨怪们——全都随着一支曲调起舞，那曲调乃由春天之神魂所成，是降临在一个尘世的清晨、在欢快的羊群之间的。

===Original===
And the trolls were very near to the frontier now, their faces already puckered to laugh at the ways of men; they were hurrying with all the eagerness of small vain things to be over the twilight that lies between Elfland and Earth: now they went forward no longer, but only glided in circles and intricate spirals, dancing some such dance as the gnats in summer evenings dance over the fields we know. And grave monsters of fable in deeps of the ferny forest danced minuets that witches had made of their whims and their laughter, long ago long ago in their youth before cities had come to the world.
