旅人那庄重的衣裳与高耸的礼帽其色甚黑，衬着原野上朦胧的绿意，远远便可望见他穿过灰蒙蒙的傍晚，走向沼泽的边缘。然而在这样的时辰、在那荒凉之地旁，并无一人可来观看，因为黑暗的威胁已弥漫于田野之间，所有的牛都已归栏，农人也都在自己屋里取暖；于是旅人独行。不久他踏着不稳的小径，来到了芦苇与纤细的灯心草之间，一阵风正向它们诉说那些于人毫无意义的故事——关于荒凉的漫长史话，关于雨的古老传说；而身后远远的高地上，天色正暗下去，他看见有灯火在房屋所在之处开始闪烁。他行走间带着一种与人有要务在身之人才有的庄重与严肃气度；然而他却背向那些房屋，前往无人涉足之处，不往任何村落或人间的孤舍前行，因为沼泽直通精灵国。在他与那隔开人世与精灵国的朦胧边境之间，再无一人，然而旅人仍一路前行，宛如身负要务。他每迈出庄重的一步，明亮的苔藓便摇晃起来，沼泽似欲将他吞没，而他那根体面的手杖深陷泥淖，全然不能给他支撑；然而旅人似乎只在意自己踱步的庄严。就这样他越过那致命的沼泽，其仪态堪比节庆之日长者们主持开市时的缓步游行——最年长者为之祈福，众农人齐聚棚摊，往来交易。

===Original===
And up and down, up and down, songbirds went wavering home, skirting the marsh's edge on their way to their native hedges; pigeons passed landward to roost in high dark trees; the last of a multitude of rooks was gone; and all the air was empty.

===Chinese===
上上下下、上上下下，鸣禽摇摆着归巢，沿着沼泽的边缘飞向它们出生的树篱；鸽子朝陆地方向飞过，去高大的暗树间栖宿；成群的秃鼻鸦中最后一只也已远去；于是整片天空空无一物。

===Original===
And now the great marsh thrilled to the news of the coming of a stranger; for, no sooner had the traveller gravely set a foot on one of those brilliant mosses that bloom in the pools, than a thrill shot under their roots and below the stems of the bulrushes, and ran like a light beneath the surface of the water, or like the sound of a song, and passed far over the marshes, and came quivering to the border of magical twilight that divides Elfland from Earth; and stayed not there, but troubled the very border and passed beyond it and was felt in Elfland: for where the great marshes run down to the border of Earth the frontier is thinner and more uncertain than elsewhere.

===Chinese===
此时大沼泽因一个陌生人将至的消息而战栗；因为旅人庄重地将一只脚踏上池中所盛开的一块璀璨苔藓之上，一缕颤动便从其根下、从蒲草的茎下窜过，如一道光在水皮之下奔流，又如一首歌的声响，掠过辽阔的沼泽，颤动着来到那隔开精灵国与人世的魔法暮色之边境；且不止于此，它扰动了那道边境本身，越过了它，直传至精灵国而被察觉：因为在大沼泽直抵人世边境之处，那边界比别处更薄、更不稳。

===Original===
And as soon as they felt that thrill in the deep of the marshes the will-o'-the-wisps soared up from their fathomless homes, and waved their lights to beckon the traveller on, over the quaking mosses at the hour when the duck were flighting. And under that whirr and rush and rejoicing of wings that the ducks make in that hour the traveller followed after the waving lights, further and further into the marshes. Yet sometimes he turned from them, so that for a while they followed him, instead of leading as they were accustomed to do, till they could get round in front of him and lead him once more. A watcher, if there had been one in such bad light and in such a perilous place, had noticed after a while in the venerable traveller's movements a queer resemblance to those of the hen green plover when she lures the stranger after her in spring, away from the mossy bank where her eggs lie bare. Or perhaps such a resemblance is merely fanciful, and a watcher might have noticed no such thing. At any rate on that night in that desolate place there was no watcher whatever.

===Chinese===
当它们在沼泽深处感到那一缕颤动，鬼火们便从其深不可测的家中腾起，挥动着它们的光引诱旅人向前，越过那摇颤的苔藓——正是群鸭振翅飞掠的时辰。在群鸭于那个时辰所掀起的那阵扑打、冲涌与欢腾的羽翼之声下，旅人追随那摇曳的光，愈行愈远，深入沼泽。然而他有时又转过身去背离它们，以至于有一阵子它们反要追随他，不再如它们所习惯的那样在前引路，直到它们能绕到他前面，再度将他引上。一个旁观者——倘若在那样的暗光下、在那样的险地竟有一个旁观者的话——或可于不久之后察觉到，这位庄重旅人的举止有一种古怪的相类之处：仿佛春日里雌的绿鸻为引开陌生人而将其诱至自己身后、远远离开那裸露着其卵的苔岸时的作派。又或许这种相似纯属臆想，旁观者未必能注意到这等事。无论如何，那一夜、在那荒凉之地，根本没有任何旁观者。

===Original===
And the traveller followed his curious course, sometimes towards the dangerous mosses, sometimes towards the safe green land, always with grave demeanour and reverent gait; and the will-o'-the-wisps in multitudes gathered about him. And still that deep thrill that warned the marsh of a stranger throbbed on through the ooze below the roots of the rushes; and did not cease, as it should as soon as the stranger was dead, but haunted the marsh like some echo of music that magic has made everlasting, and troubled the will-o'-the-wisps even over the border in Elfland.

===Chinese===
旅人便循着他那古怪的路线前行，时而朝危险的苔藓走去，时而朝安全的青绿陆地，始终神态庄重、步态肃穆；而成群的鬼火聚拢在他四周。那警示沼泽有陌生人到来的深沉颤动，仍透过灯心草根下的淤泥一下下悸动着，不曾止息——本应在陌生人一死之时便立即停止的——却如某段被魔法化为永恒的乐声之回响一般，萦绕于沼泽之上，甚至惊扰了那边境之外、精灵国之内的鬼火。

===Original===
Now it is far from my intention to write anything detrimental to will-o'-the-wisps, or anything that may be construed as being a slight upon them: no such construction should be put upon my writings. But it is well known that the people of the marshes lure travellers to their doom, and have delighted to follow that avocation for centuries, and I may be permitted to mention this in no spirit of disapproval.

===Chinese===
我绝无意写下任何有损于鬼火的话，亦无意写下任何可被视为对它们的轻慢之言：切莫对我的文字作此等解读。然而众所熟知，沼泽之民惯于诱引旅人趋于其死地，且以此为乐已数百年之久，我或可获准提及此事，而毫无非难之意。

===Original===
The will-o'-the-wisps then that were about this traveller redoubled their efforts with fury; and when still he eluded their last enticements only on the very edge of the deadliest pools, and still lived and still travelled, and the whole marsh knew of it, then the greater will-o'-the-wisps that dwell in Elfland rose up from their magical mire and rushed over the border. And the whole marsh was troubled.

===Chinese===
于是围在这旅人四周的鬼火们发狂般地加倍施为；当他在那些最致命的水潭边上竟又躲过了它们最后的诱引，仍然活着、仍然前行，而整个沼泽都已知晓此事，于是那些栖居精灵国之中的更强大的鬼火从它们的魔法泥淖中升起，越过边境蜂拥而来。整个沼泽为之骚动。

===Original===
Almost like little moons grown nimbly impudent the people of the marshes glowed before that solemn traveller, leading his reverend steps to the edge of death only to retrace their steps again to beckon him back once more. And then in spite of the great height of his hat and the dark length of his coat that frivolous people began to perceive that mosses were bearing his weight which never before had supported any traveller. At this their fury increased and they all leaped nearer to him; and nearer and nearer they flocked wherever he went; and in their fury their enticements were losing their craftiness.

===Chinese===
几乎如一群变得轻捷放肆的小月亮，沼泽之民在那位庄重的旅人之前熠熠发光，引他那可敬的脚步走到死亡的边缘，却又转身折返，再度招引他一回。随后，尽管他那高耸的礼帽、那又长又黑的衣袍，这轻浮之族开始察觉到：竟有苔藓承受住了他的体重，而那些苔藓从不曾承托过任何旅人。于是它们益发狂怒，全都朝他跳得更近；无论他走到何处，它们都愈聚愈近；在它们的狂怒之中，它们的诱引渐渐失去了原本的狡黠。

===Original===
And now a watcher in the marshes, if such there had been, had seen something more than a traveller surrounded by will-o'-the-wisps; for he might have noticed that the traveller was almost leading them, instead of the will-o'-the-wisps leading the traveller. And in their impatience to have him dead the people of the marshes had never thought that they were all coming nearer and nearer to the dry land.

===Chinese===
此时沼泽中若有一个旁观者——倘若真有的话——他所见的便不止是一个被鬼火环绕的旅人；因为他或可察觉到，几乎已是那旅人在引领着它们，而非鬼火在引着旅人。而沼泽之民一心急不可耐地要他死去，竟从未想到它们自己已愈来愈接近干燥的陆地。

===Original===
And when all was dark but the water they suddenly found themselves in a field of grass with their feet rasping against the rough pasture, while the traveller was seated with his knees gathered up to his chin and was eyeing them from under the brim of his high black hat. Never before had any of them been lured to dry land by traveller, and there were amongst them that night those eldest and greatest among them who had come with their moon-like lights right over the border from Elfland. They looked at each other in uneasy astonishment as they dropped limply onto the grass, for the roughness and heaviness of the solid land oppressed them after the marshes. And then they began to perceive that that venerable traveller whose bright eyes watched them so keenly out of that black mass of clothes was little larger than they were themselves, in spite of his reverend airs. Indeed, though stouter and rounder he was not quite so tall. Who was this, they began to mutter, who had lured will-o'-the-wisps? And some of those elders from Elfland went up to him that they might ask him with what audacity he had dared to lure such as them. And then the traveller spoke. Without rising or turning his head he spoke where he sat.

===Chinese===
当万物俱暗、唯有水面尚泛微光之时，它们忽然发现自己已置身于一片草地之上，双足擦着粗砺的牧草，而那旅人则坐着，双膝拢至下颌，从他那顶黑色高帽的帽檐下打量着它们。它们之中此前从未有谁被一个旅人诱到了干燥的陆地上，而且那一夜它们当中还有那些最为年长、最为强大的——它们带着月亮般的光，径直越过边境从精灵国而来。它们彼此以不安而惊诧的目光相顾，软软地瘫落到草地上，因为在沼泽之后，坚硬陆地的粗粝与沉重压得它们喘不过气来。于是它们开始察觉到，那位庄重的旅人——他明亮的眼睛正从那一团黑色的衣物之中锐利地盯着它们——尽管气度可敬，身量却并不比它们自己大多少。诚然，他虽较为壮硕圆滚，个头却还略矮一筹。它们开始咕哝：此人究竟是谁，竟敢诱引鬼火？于是精灵国来的几位长者走到他跟前，要质问他以何等的胆大妄为竟敢诱引它们这等生灵。这时旅人开口了。他并未起身，亦未回头，就那样坐在原地说话。

===Original===
"People of the marshes," he said, "do you love unicorns?"

===Chinese===
「沼泽之民啊，」他说，「你们可喜爱独角兽？」

===Original===
And at the word unicorns scorn and laughter filled every tiny heart in all that frivolous multitude, excluding all other emotions, so that they forgot their petulance at having been lured; although to lure will-o'-the-wisps is held by them to be the gravest of insults, and never would they have forgiven it if they had had longer memories. At the word unicorns they all giggled in silence. And this they did by flickering up and down like the light of a little mirror flashed by an impudent hand. Unicorns! Little love had they for the haughty creatures. Let them learn to speak to the people of the marshes when they came to drink at their pools. Let them learn to give their due to the great lights of Elfland, and the lesser lights that illumined the marshes of Earth!

===Chinese===
一听独角兽三字，嘲讽与大笑便盈满了那一整群轻浮之辈每一颗细小的心，将其他一切情绪悉数排斥在外，于是它们便忘了自己被诱引一场所惹出的愠怒——尽管在它们看来，诱引鬼火乃是最不可饶恕的侮辱，倘若它们的记性再长久些，它们是断然不会宽恕的。一听独角兽三字，它们全都无声地咯咯笑了起来。它们如此作笑，是凭着一上一下地闪烁，如同一只放肆的手所晃动的一面小镜之光。独角兽！它们对那些高傲的生灵毫无好感。让它们学着点，到沼泽之民的水潭饮水时，该如何与沼泽之民说话才是！让它们学着点，该如何礼敬精灵国的伟大之光，以及照亮人世沼泽的那些次等之光！

===Original===
"No," said an elder of the will-o'-the-wisps, "none loves the proud unicorns."

===Chinese===
「不，」一位鬼火长者说，「没有人喜爱那些高傲的独角兽。」

===Original===
"Come then," said the traveller, "and we will hunt them. And you shall light us in the night with your lights, when we hunt them with dogs over the fields of men."

===Chinese===
「那就来吧，」旅人说，「我们一同去猎它们。当我们在夜里用犬于人类的田野之上追逐它们时，你们便用你们的光为我们照亮。」

===Original===
"Venerable traveller," said that elder will-o'-the-wisp: but at those words the traveller flung up his hat and leaped from his long black coat, and stood before the will-o'-the-wisps stark naked. And the people of the marshes saw that it was a troll that had tricked them.

===Chinese===
「可敬的旅人，」那位鬼火长者说：可话音未落，旅人已抛起了他的帽子，从他长长的黑外衣中一跃而出，赤条条地站在众鬼火之前。沼泽之民这才看清，戏弄它们的原来是一只巨怪。

===Original===
Their anger at this was slight; for the people of the marshes have tricked the trolls, and the trolls have tricked the people of the marshes, each of them so many times for ages and ages, that only the wisest among them can say which has tricked the other most and is how many tricks ahead. They consoled themselves now by thinking of times when trolls had been made to look ludicrous, and consented to come with their lights to help to hunt unicorns, for their wills were weak when they stood on the dry land and they easily acquiesced in any suggestion or followed anyone's whim.

===Chinese===
它们对此并不十分恼怒；因为沼泽之民戏弄过巨怪，巨怪也戏弄过沼泽之民，世世代代以来彼此不知互弄了多少回，唯有它们之中最聪明的才能说出究竟谁戏弄对方更多、领先了多少个诡计。它们便以回忆那些巨怪被弄得狼狈不堪的旧事来自我宽慰，继而答应随它们的光前去助猎独角兽——因为当它们立于干燥的陆地之上时，意志便变得薄弱，对任何提议都易于首肯，或追随任何人的一个念头。

===Original===
It was Lurulu who had thus tricked the will-o'-the-wisps, knowing well how they love to lure travellers; and, having obtained the highest hat and gravest coat he could steal, he had set out with a bait that he knew would bring them from great distances. Now that he had gathered them all on the solid land and had their promise of light and help against unicorns, which such creatures will give easily on account of the unicorns' pride, he began to lead them away to the village of Erl, slowly at first while their feet grew accustomed to the hard land; and over the fields he brought them limping to Erl.

===Chinese===
戏弄这些鬼火的正是卢鲁鲁（Lurulu），他深知鬼火们喜爱诱引旅人；于是弄到了他能偷到的最高的帽子与最庄重的外衣，便带着一样他明知能把它们从远处引来作饵的东西出发了。如今他既已将它们尽数引到了坚实的陆地上，又得了它们以光与助猎独角兽的许诺——这类生灵因独角兽的高傲而极易许下此诺——他便开始引它们前往厄尔的村庄，起初行得缓慢，好让它们的脚渐渐习惯坚硬的陆地；越过田野，他将它们一瘸一拐地带到了厄尔。

===Original===
And now there was nothing in all the marshes that at all resembled man, and the geese came down on a huge tumult of wings. The little swift teal shot home; and all the dark air twanged with the flight of the duck.

===Chinese===
此时整片沼泽之中再无一物略似人形，群鹅鼓着一片巨大的羽翼之喧而降。小巧迅捷的水凫如箭般射回巢穴；而整片幽暗的空气，都因群鸭的飞翔而嗡嗡作响。

## III The Magical Sword Meets Some of the Swords of Elfland / 三 魔法之剑会精灵国之诸剑

===Original===
When Alveric came to the enchanted wood the light in which Elfland glowed had neither grown nor dwindled, and he saw that it came from no radiance that shines on the fields we know, unless the wandering lights of wonderful moments that sometimes astonish our fields, and are gone the instant they come, are strayed over the border of Elfland by some momentary disorder of magic. Neither sun nor moon made the light of that enchanted day.
A line of pine trees up which ivy climbed, as high as their lowering black foliage, stood like sentinels at the edge of the wood. The silver spires were shining as though it were they that made all this azure glow in which Elfland swam. And Alveric having by now come far into Elfland, and being now before its capital palace, and knowing that Elfland guarded its mysteries well, drew his father’s sword before he entered the wood. The other still hung on his back, slung in its new scabbard over his left shoulder.

===Chinese===
待阿尔韦里克（Alveric）来到那片施过魔法的森林，精灵国所辉映的那片光既未增长也未衰减；他看出那光并非源于照亮我们所知之原野的任何辉芒——除非那些美妙时刻中游移的光，那些偶尔使我们原野为之惊异、一来即逝的光，是由于某种魔法上一时的失序，才迷走入精灵国边境之内的。那施过魔法的白昼之光，既非日所为，也非月所为。
一排松树挺立在林缘，有如哨兵一般；常春藤攀缘其上，直攀到它们那低垂的黑色枝叶那么高。银色的尖塔熠熠生辉，仿佛正是它们成就了精灵国沐浴其中的那全部蔚蓝的辉光。阿尔韦里克此时已深入精灵国，立于其主宫殿之前，深知精灵国对其奥秘守护甚严，故在入林之前便拔出了他父亲的那柄剑。另一柄剑仍挂在他背上，装在新制的剑鞘里，斜悬于他左肩之上。

===Original===
And the moment he passed by one of those guardian pine trees, the ivy that lived on it unfastened its tendrils and, rapidly letting itself down, came straight for Alveric and clutched at his throat.
The long thin sword of his father was just in time; had it not been drawn he would have scarcely got it out, so swift was the rush of the ivy. He cut tendril after tendril that grasped his limbs as ivy grasps old towers, and still more tendrils came for him, until he severed the main stem between him and the tree. And as he was doing this he heard a hissing rush behind him, and another had come down from another tree and was rushing at him with all its leaves spread out. The green thing looked wild and angry as it gripped his left shoulder as though it would hold it forever. But Alveric severed those tendrils with a blow of his sword and then fought with the rest, while the first one was still alive but now too short to reach him, and was lashing its branches angrily on the ground. And soon, as the surprise of the attack was over and he had freed himself of the tendrils that had gripped him, Alveric stepped back till the ivy could not reach him and he could still fight it with his long sword. The ivy crawled back then to lure Alveric on, and sprang at him when he followed it. But, terrible though the grip of ivy is, that was a good sharp sword; and very soon Alveric, all bruised though he was, had so lopped his assailant that it fled back up its tree. Then he stepped back and looked at the wood in the light of his new experience, choosing a way through. He saw at once that in the barrier of pine trees the two in front of him had had their ivy so shortened in the fight that if he went midway between the two the ivy of neither would be able to reach him. He then stepped forward, but the moment he did so he noticed one of the pine trees move closer to the other. He knew then that the time was come to draw his magical sword.

===Chinese===
就在他经过其中一株守卫松树的那一瞬，寄生于其上的常春藤便解开了它的卷须，迅速垂身而下，直扑阿尔韦里克，去扼他的咽喉。
他父亲那柄细长的剑来得正是时候；倘若未曾拔出，他几乎再无机会将它抽出，常春藤扑来之势便是如此之迅疾。他将那一根又一根卷须斩断——那些卷须攀缠他的四肢，一如常春藤攀缠古旧的塔楼——而更多的卷须仍向他袭来，直到他将他与树之间的那段主茎一刀割断。正当他做这些之时，他听见身后一阵嘶嘶的扑动之声，又一株树上又一条常春藤已垂降下来，舒展着它全部的叶子向他猛扑过来。那碧绿之物一把握住他的左肩，神情狂怒，仿佛要将其永世攥住。但阿尔韦里克一剑斩断了那些卷须，又与余下的卷须厮杀起来；此时那第一条藤虽仍活着，却已短得够不着他，只能在地面愤怒地挥抽着枝条。不久，随着那场猝不及防的袭击已过，他也摆脱了攥住他的卷须，阿尔韦里克便向后退去，退到常春藤够不着他、而他仍能以长剑与之相斗的地方。于是那常春藤便爬了回去，意在引诱阿尔韦里克上前，等他跟随时再向他猛扑。然而常春藤的攥握虽可怖，那却是一柄又快又利的好剑；不多时，阿尔韦里克虽周身是伤，却已将来犯之藤斫得七零八落，使它逃回它的树上去了。他便退后一步，凭他新得的经验在那片光里审视那片森林，拣选一条通路。他一眼便看出：在那道松树屏障中，他身前的那两株，它们的常春藤在方才那一战里已被斫得甚短，只要他从两株正中穿过，任何一株的藤都够不着他。于是他举步向前，可他刚一动，便察觉其中一株松树向另一株挪近了些。他便知道，拔出他那柄魔法之剑的时辰已到了。

===Original===
So he returned his father’s sword to the scabbard by his side and drew out the other over his shoulder and, going straight up to the tree that had moved, swept at the ivy as it sprang at him: and the ivy fell all at once to the ground, not lifeless but a heap of common ivy. And then he gave one blow to the trunk of the tree, and a chip flew out not larger than a common sword would have made, but the whole tree shuddered; and with that shudder disappeared at once a certain ominous look that the pine had had, and it stood there an ordinary unenchanted tree. Then he stepped on through the wood with his sword drawn.
He had not gone many paces when he heard behind him a sound like a faint breeze in the treetops, yet no wind was blowing in that wood at all. He looked round therefore, and saw that the pine trees were following him. They were coming slowly after him, keeping well out of the way of his sword, but to left and right they were gaining on him, so that he saw he was being gradually shut in by a crescent that grew thicker and thicker as it crowded amongst the trees that it met on the way, and would soon crush him to death. Alveric saw at once that to turn back would be fatal, and decided to push right on, relying chiefly on speed; for his quick perception had already noticed something slow about the magic that swayed the wood; as though whoever controlled it were old or weary of magic, or interrupted by other things. So he went straight ahead, hitting every tree in his way, whether enchanted or not, a blow with his magical sword; and the runes that ran in that metal from the other side of the sun were stronger than any spells that there were in the wood. Great oak trees with sinister boles drooped and lost all their enchantment as Alveric flashed past them with a flick of that magical sword. He was marching faster than the clumsy pines. And soon he left in that weird and eerie wood a wake of trees that were wholly unenchanted, that stood there now without hint of romance or mystery even.

===Chinese===
于是他将父亲的剑还入身侧的鞘中，越过肩头抽出另一柄，径直走向那株挪动过的树，趁常春藤向他扑来时一挥：那常春藤便一下子全部跌落地上，并非失了性命，却成了一堆寻常的常春藤。接着他朝那株树干砍了一记，飞出一片木屑，并不比寻常刀剑所能削下的更大，然而整株树都战栗了一下；随着那一阵战栗，那株松树素来带着的一股凶险之气顿时消失，它便立在那里，成了一株寻常的、被剥夺了魔法的树。于是他提剑前行，穿过森林。
他没走几步，便听见身后一阵声响，犹如树梢上一缕微弱的轻风，然而那林中根本没有一丝风。他便回头一望，看见那些松树正尾随而来。它们缓缓地追着他，与他的剑远远地保持着距离，但从左右两翼却向他逼近，使他看出自己正渐渐被一弯新月亮所合围，那新月愈挤愈厚，一路挤入它所遇见的那些树丛之中，不久便要将他碾死。阿尔韦里克立刻看出：回头是必死之途，便决意径直前行，主要倚仗脚力；因为他那敏锐的感知已察觉到，操纵这片森林的那魔法有些迟缓之处，仿佛那施法之人已经年迈，或已厌倦了魔法，或正被别的事所打断。于是他径直向前，路上一株树也不放过，无论是施过魔法的还是未施过魔法的，都给以他的魔法之剑一击；而那金属中从太阳的另一面流淌而来的符文，比森林里所有的咒法都更为强盛。那些树干带着阴森之气的巨大橡树，在阿尔韦里克挥动那柄魔法之剑一闪而过之时，都颓然垂头，尽失其魔法。他的脚步快过那些笨拙的松树。不久他便在那片诡谲可怖的森林里留下了一条全然失去魔法的树的尾迹——那些树立在那里，如今连一丝浪漫或神秘的影子都不剩了。

===Original===
And all of a sudden he came from the gloom of the wood to the emerald glory of the Elf King’s lawns. Again, we have hints of such things here. Imagine lawns of ours just emerging from night, flashing early lights from their dewdrops when all the stars have gone; bordered with flowers that just begin to appear, their gentle colours all coming back after night; untrodden by any feet except the tiniest and wildest; shut off from the wind and the world by trees in whose fronds is still darkness: picture these waiting for the birds to sing; there is almost a hint there sometimes of the glow of the lawns of Elfland; but then it passes so quickly that we can never be sure. More beautiful than aught our wonder guesses, more than our hearts have hoped, were the dewdrop lights and twilights in which these lawns glowed and shone. And we have another thing by which to hint of them, those seaweeds or sea-mosses that drape Mediterranean rocks and shine out of blue-green water for gazers from dizzy cliffs: more like sea-floors were these lawns than like any land of ours, for the air of Elfland is thus deep and blue.
At the beauty of these lawns Alveric stood gazing as they shone through twilight and dew, surrounded by the mauve and ruddy glory of the massed flowers of Elfland, beside which our sunsets pale and our orchids droop; and beyond them lay like night the magical wood. And jutting from that wood, with glittering portals all open wide to the lawns, with windows more blue than our sky on summer’s nights; as though built of starlight; shone that palace that may be only told of in song.

===Chinese===
蓦地，他从那森林的幽暗之中走了出来，来到精灵国王那些草坪的翡翠般的辉煌之中。再说一遍，我们在此世也有这类事物的暗示。试想我们人间的一片草坪刚刚从夜色中显现，群星俱已隐去，露珠上闪着清晨的光；边上正当初绽的花朵，它们柔和的颜色在夜后一点点地归来；除了最微小最野的那些足印之外，未被任何脚步踏过；被一些树隔绝于风与世界之外，那些树的枝叶之间尚存着黑暗：请想象这般景象正等着鸟儿来啼唱；那里面有时几乎有一点精灵国草坪那种辉光的暗示；但随即逝去得那样快，使我们永难确信。比我们所能猜想的一切更美，比我们心头所盼望的一切更多，那些露珠之光与暮色之光，正是这片草坪在其中熠熠生辉的光。我们还有另一桩可以借以暗示它们的事物：那些披挂在地中海礁石上的海草或海苔，从蓝绿色的水里向悬崖上头晕眩的凝望者闪出光来：这片草坪与其说像我们人世的任何一片陆地，不如说更像海底，因为精灵国的空气便是这样深邃，这样蓝。
阿尔韦里克为这片草坪之美所摄，伫立凝望，看它们在暮色与露水之间熠熠生辉；四周是精灵国成簇花朵那锦葵色与火红色相间的辉煌，与之相比，我们人世的落日黯然失色，我们的兰花也低垂了头；而在它们的那一边，那片施过魔法的森林如夜色般横陈。从那森林中突兀而出，闪闪发光的大门一齐向草坪敞开，窗户比我们夏夜的星空更蓝；仿佛以星光筑就；那座只能在歌谣中被传唱的宫殿，便如此熠熠生辉。

===Original===
As Alveric stood there with his sword in his hand, at the wood’s edge, scarcely breathing, with his eyes looking over the lawns at the chiefest glory of Elfland; through one of the portals alone came the King of Elfland’s daughter. She walked dazzling to the lawns without seeing Alveric. Her feet brushed through the dew and the heavy air and gently pressed for an instant the emerald grass, which bent and rose, as our harebells when blue butterflies light and leave them, roaming carefree along the hills of chalk.
And as she passed he neither breathed nor moved, nor could have moved if those pines had still pursued him, but they stayed in the forest not daring to touch those lawns.
She wore a crown that seemed to be carved of great pale sapphires; she shone on those lawns and gardens like a dawn coming unaware, out of long night, on some planet nearer than us to the sun. And as she passed near Alveric she suddenly turned her head; and her eyes opened in a little wonder. She had never before seen a man from the fields we know.
And Alveric gazed in her eyes all speechless and powerless still: it was indeed the Princess Lirazel in her beauty. And then he saw that her crown was not of sapphires but ice.

===Chinese===
正当阿尔韦里克手提其剑立于林缘，几乎屏住了呼吸，双目越过草坪凝望着精灵国最盛的辉煌之际；只从其中一道门里，单独走出了精灵国王的女儿。她耀眼地走到草坪之上，未曾看见阿尔韦里克。她的双足拂过露水与沉甸甸的空气，又轻轻地在翡翠般的草上按了一瞬，那草便弯下又挺起，一如我们的蓝铃花，当蓝色的蝴蝶落在上面又飞离之时，沿着白垩的丘陵无拘无束地游荡。
她走过之际，他不曾呼吸，也不曾动弹；纵然那些松树仍在追他，他也不能动弹了——然而那些松树已留在森林之中，不敢触碰那些草坪。
她戴着一顶王冠，仿佛是以巨大的淡色蓝宝石雕琢而成；她在那些草坪与花园之上闪耀，犹如一道黎明出乎意料地从漫长的夜里，来到某颗比我们更近太阳的行星之上。当她从阿尔韦里克身畔经过时，忽然回过头来；她的双目微微一奇地睁大了。她从未见过来自我们所知之原野的男人。
而阿尔韦里克凝视着她的双眼，仍然一言不能发，一动不能动：那果然是莉拉泽尔（Lirazel）公主，带着她那一份美丽。继而他又看出，她的王冠并非蓝宝石所制，而是冰。

===Original===
“Who are you?” she said. And her voice had the music that, of earthly things, was most like ice in thousands of broken pieces rocked by a wind of spring upon lakes in some northern country.
And he said: “I come from the fields that are mapped and known.”
And then she sighed for a moment for those fields, for she had heard how life beautifully passes there, and how there are always in those fields young generations, and she thought of the changing seasons and children and age, of which elfin minstrels had sung when they told of Earth.
And when he saw her sigh for the fields we know he told her somewhat of that land whence he had come. And she questioned him further, and soon he was telling her tales of his home and the Vale of Erl. And she wondered to hear of it and asked him many questions more; and then he told her all that he knew of Earth, not presuming to tell Earth’s story from what his own eyes had seen in his bare score of years, but telling those tales and fables of the ways of beasts and men, that the folk of Erl had drawn out of the ages, and which their elders told by the fire at evening when children asked of what happened long ago. Thus on the edge of those lawns whose miraculous glory was framed by flowers we have never known, with the magical wood behind them, and that palace shining near which may only be told of in song, they spoke of the simple wisdom of old men and old women, telling of harvests and the blossoming of roses and may, of when to plant in gardens, of what wild animals knew; how to heal, how to sow, how to thatch, and of which of the winds in what seasons blow over the fields we know.

===Chinese===
「你是谁？」她说。她的声音里有一种乐音，在人世万物之中，最像北方某国湖泊之上、被一阵春风摇动的成千块碎冰所发之声。
他便说：「我从那片被绘入地图、为人所知的原野上来。」
于是她为那片原野叹息了片刻，因为她曾听说，那里的生命如何美丽地逝去，那里的原野上又如何总有年轻的一代代相继而生；她便想起了更迭的四季、孩童与年岁——这些都是精灵国的吟游诗人们在讲述人世时所曾歌咏过的。
当他看见她为我们所知的原野而叹息，他便对她略略讲了一些他所从来的那片土地。她又进一步相问，不久他便给她讲起了他的家园与厄尔之谷（the Vale of Erl）的故事。她听得惊奇，又问了他许多；于是他便把他人世所知的一切都告诉了她，并不敢妄凭自己区区二十年里亲眼所见，便来讲述人世的故事，而是讲述那些关于兽与人之道的传说与寓言——那些是厄尔之民从漫长的岁月里抽引出来的，也是他们的老人在傍晚的炉火旁、当孩子们问起久远以前发生过什么之时所讲的。就这样，在那片草坪的边上——草坪那不可思议的辉煌由我们所从未见过的花朵镶成框，他们身后便是那片施过魔法的森林，那座只能在歌谣中传唱的宫殿在近处熠熠生辉——他们谈论起老翁老妪那朴素的智慧，讲起收获与玫瑰、五月花的绽放，讲起园中何时下种，野生的兽类又知道些什么；如何医病，如何播种，如何苫屋顶，以及那些风，在怎样的季节里，吹过我们所知的原野。

===Original===
And then there appeared those knights who guard that palace lest any should come through the enchanted wood. Four of them they came shining over the lawns in armour, their faces not to be seen. In all the enchanted centuries of their lives they had not dared to dream of the princess: they had never bared their faces when they knelt armed before her. Yet they had sworn an oath of dreadful words that no man else should ever speak with her, if one should come through the enchanted wood. With this oath now on their lips they marched towards Alveric.
Lirazel looked at them sorrowfully yet could not halt them, for they came by command of her father which she could not avert; and well she knew that her father might not recall his command, for he had uttered it ages ago at the bidding of Fate. Alveric looked at their armour, which seemed to be brighter than any metal of ours, as though it came from one of those buttresses near, which are only told of in song; then he went towards them drawing his father’s sword, for he thought to drive its slender point through some joint of the armour. The other he put into his left hand.
As the first knight struck, Alveric parried, and stopped the blow, but there came a shock like lightning into his arm and the sword flew from his hand, and he knew that no earthly sword could meet the weapons of Elfland, and took the magical sword in his right hand. With this he parried the strokes of the Princess Lirazel’s guard, for such these four knights were, having waited for this occasion through all the ages of Elfland. And no more shock came to him from any of those swords, but only a vibration in his own sword’s metal that passed through it like a song, and a kind of a glow that arose in it, reaching to Alveric’s heart and cheering it.

===Chinese===
就在此时，那些为守护那座宫殿、以防有人穿过施过魔法的森林而来的骑士们现身了。他们一共四人，披甲在草坪上熠熠行来，面容不可得见。在他们那施过魔法的几个世纪的生命里，他们从未敢妄想这位公主：他们披甲跪在她面前时，也从未敢袒露过脸庞。然而他们曾以可怖的言辞发下一誓：若有人穿过那片施过魔法的森林而来，再无别的人可以与她交谈。如今他们口衔此誓，向阿尔韦里克逼来。
莉拉泽尔忧伤地望着他们，却无法阻止他们，因为他们是奉她父亲之命而来，这她无从回避；她也深知她父亲无法收回他的命令，因为那是他在亘古之前、应命运之命而发的。阿尔韦里克看着他们的盔甲，那盔甲似乎比我们人世任何金属都更为明亮，仿佛来自附近某座只在歌谣中被传唱的堡垒；他便迎上前去，抽出父亲的剑，心想将那纤细的剑尖刺入盔甲的某道接缝之中。另一柄剑他换到了左手里。
当头一位骑士一剑劈下，阿尔韦里克架住一格，止住了那一击，但一道宛如闪电的冲击钻入他的手臂，那剑便脱手飞出；他便知道，任何人间的剑都不能与精灵国之兵刃相抗，于是将那柄魔法之剑换入右手。他便以此剑格挡莉拉泽尔公主卫队的劈砍——这四位骑士正是公主的卫队，他们为这一刻已等候了精灵国的全部世代。那些剑不再给他带来任何冲击，只有他自己剑身之中的一种震动，如同一支歌一般穿过剑身，又有一种微微的辉光从剑中升起，直抵阿尔韦里克的心，使之振奋。

===Original===
But as Alveric continued to parry the swift blows of the guard, that sword that was kin to the lightning grew weary of these defences, for it had in its essence speed and desperate journeys; and, lifting Alveric’s hand along with it, it swept blows at the elvish knights, and the armour of Elfland could not hold it out. Thick and curious blood began to pour through rifts in the armour, and soon of that glittering company two were fallen; and Alveric, encouraged by the zeal of his sword fought cheerily and soon overthrew another, so that only he and one of the guard remained, who seemed to have some stronger magic about him than had been given to his fallen comrades. And so it was, for when the Elf King had first enchanted the guard he had charmed this elvish soldier first of all, while all the wonder of his runes were new; and the soldier and his armour and his sword had something still of this early magic about them, more potent than any inspirations of wizardry that had come later from his master’s mind. Yet this knight, as Alveric soon was able to feel along his arm and his sword, had none of those three master runes of which the old witch had spoken when she made the sword on her hill; for these were preserved unuttered by the King of Elfland himself, with which to hedge his own presence. To have known of their existence she must have flown by broom to Elfland and spoken secretly alone with the King.

===Chinese===
然而随着阿尔韦里克继续招架卫队那疾风般的劈砍，那柄与闪电同宗的剑渐渐厌倦了这些防御，因为它的本性里便是速度与孤注一掷的远行；于是它带动阿尔韦里克的手，一并向那些精灵骑士劈去，精灵国的盔甲竟抵挡不住。又浓又奇的血开始顺着盔甲的裂缝涌出，不久那一队闪闪发光的人中便倒下了两个；阿尔韦里克受他剑之锐气所鼓舞，斗得兴起，不多时又打翻了一个，于是场上只剩他与卫队中的一人相对，而此人似乎周身带着一股比他那几位倒下的同袍更强的魔法。事实也确乎如此，因为精灵国王当初为卫队施法之时，最先施法的便是这位精灵兵士，那时他全部符文的奇能尚是新的；所以这兵士、他的盔甲与他的剑，都还带着些许这种早期魔法的余韵，比日后从他主人心智中所生出的任何巫术灵感都更为强盛。然而这一位骑士——阿尔韦里克不久便顺着臂与剑感觉出来——并不具备那三道主符文（master-runes），就是那位老女巫在山上铸剑之时所说的那三道；因为这三道为精灵国王亲自秘而不宣，留作拱卫他自身圣容之用。她若要知晓这三道的存在，非得乘扫帚飞到精灵国，与国王单独密谈不可。

===Original===
And the sword that had visited Earth from so far away smote like the falling of thunderbolts; and green sparks rose from the armour, and crimson as sword met sword; and thick elvish blood moved slowly, from wide slits, down the cuirass; and Lirazel gazed in awe and wonder and love; and the combatants edged away fighting into the forest; and branches fell on them hacked off by their fight; and the runes in Alveric’s far-travelled sword exulted, and roared at the elf-knight; until in the dark of the wood, amongst branches severed from disenchanted trees, with a blow like that of a thunderbolt riving an oak tree, Alveric slew him.
At that crash, and at that silence, Lirazel ran to his side.
“Quick!” she said. “For my father has three runes…” She durst not speak of them.
“Whither?” said Alveric.
And she said: “To the fields you know.”

===Chinese===
那柄自极远之地降临过人世的剑，劈下时如同雷石（thunderbolt）之落；绿色的火星从盔甲上腾起，剑与剑相交之时又有绯红之火；又浓的精灵之血从宽宽的裂缝里缓缓流下胸甲；莉拉泽尔怀着敬畏、惊奇与爱意凝望着；两个交锋之人一边厮杀一边挪进了森林；被他们的争斗斫断的树枝纷纷落在他们身上；阿尔韦里克那柄远行之剑中的符文欢腾起来，朝着那精灵骑士怒吼；直到森林的幽暗之中，在被剥夺了魔法的树所斫断的枝丫之间，阿尔韦里克以一记如雷石劈开橡树般的猛击，将他诛杀。
听见那一声巨响，又随之而来的那一片寂静，莉拉泽尔奔到了他的身侧。
「快！」她说，「因为我父亲有三道符文……」她不敢把它们说出来。
「往何处去？」阿尔韦里克说。
她便说：「到你所知的那片原野上去。」

## I The Plan of the Parliament of Erl / 一 厄尔议会的图谋

===Original===
In their ruddy jackets of leather that reached to their knees the men of Erl appeared before their lord, the stately white-haired man in his long red room. He leaned in his carven chair and heard their spokesman.

And thus their spokesman said.

“For seven hundred years the chiefs of your race have ruled us well; and their deeds are remembered by the minor minstrels, living on yet in their little tinkling songs. And yet the generations stream away, and there is no new thing.”

“What would you?” said the lord.

“We would be ruled by a magic lord,” they said.

===Chinese===
厄尔的人们身披及膝的赭红皮衣，前来觐见他们的领主——那位仪态庄重、白发苍苍的老者，端坐于深红长厅之中。他倚在那张雕花座椅里，听取代言人的陈词。

那代言人便如是说道。

「七百年来，贵族的历代首领治理有方，恩泽不衰；其功业为那些二三流的游吟诗人所铭记，至今仍在那叮当作响的浅歌小曲中流传不息。然而世代如流水般逝去，再无新鲜之事。」

「尔等想要什么？」领主问。

他们说：「我们愿由一位通晓魔法的领主来治理。」

===Original===
“So be it,” said the lord. “It is five hundred years since my people have spoken thus in parliament, and it shall always be as your parliament saith. You have spoken. So be it.”

And he raised his hand and blessed them and they went.

They went back to their ancient crafts, to the fitting of iron to the hooves of horses, to working upon leather, to tending flowers, to ministering to the rugged needs of Earth; they followed the ancient ways, and looked for a new thing. But the old lord sent a word to his eldest son, bidding him come before him.

And very soon the young man stood before him, in that same carven chair from which he had not moved, where light, growing late, from high windows, showed the aged eyes looking far into the future beyond that old lord’s time. And seated there he gave his son his commandment.

===Chinese===
领主说：「那就如此吧。我的子民已有五百年未曾在议会中如此进言，而议会所言，今后亦当永远遵行。你们既已开口，便如此定下。」

于是他抬起手，为他们祝福，众人便退下了。

他们回到那些古老的营生里去——为马蹄钉上铁掌，缝制皮革，莳弄花草，照应大地上粗粝的所需；他们沿袭着古老的方式，盼望着一件新事。而那位老领主却遣人传话给长子，召他前来觐见。

不多时，那年轻人便立于他面前；老领主仍倚在那张未曾挪动过的雕花座椅里。高窗之外天色渐晚，余晖映出那双苍老的眼睛——正凝望着远过他自己岁月的未来。他便在那里坐着，向儿子颁下训令。

===Original===
“Go forth,” he said, “before these days of mine are over, and therefore go in haste, and go from here eastwards and pass the fields we know, till you see the lands that clearly pertain to Faery; and cross their boundary, which is made of twilight, and come to that palace that is only told of in song.”

“It is far from here,” said the young man Alveric.

“Yes,” answered he, “it is far.”

“And further still,” the young man said, “to return. For distances in those fields are not as here.”

===Chinese===
他说：「去罢——趁我这残年未尽，须得即刻动身。自此向东，越过我们所知的原野，直抵那分明属于精灵国的疆土；再越过那条以暮色铸成的边界，去到那座唯歌谣中方有传唱的宫殿。」

年轻人阿尔韦里克（Alveric）说：「那地方离此甚远。」

「不错，」他答道，「是很远。」

「而归途更远，」年轻人说，「因那原野上的距离，不似我们这里。」

===Original===
“Even so,” said his father.

“What do you bid me do,” said the son, “when I come to that palace?”

And his father said: “To wed the King of Elfland’s daughter.”

===Chinese===
他父亲说：「纵然如此。」

儿子说：「您要我到了那宫殿，做些什么？」

他父亲说：「娶精灵国王的女儿为妻。」

===Original===
The young man thought of her beauty and crown of ice, and the sweetness that fabulous runes had told was hers. Songs were sung of her on wild hills where tiny strawberries grew, at dusk and by early starlight, and if one sought the singer no man was there. Sometimes only her name was sung softly over and over. Her name was Lirazel.

She was a princess of the magic line. The gods had sent their shadows to her christening, and the fairies too would have gone, but that they were frightened to see on their dewy fields the long dark moving shadows of the gods, so they stayed hidden in crowds of pale pink anemones, and thence blessed Lirazel.

“My people demand a magic lord to rule over them. They have chosen foolishly,” the old lord said, “and only the Dark Ones that show not their faces know all that this will bring: but we, who see not, follow the ancient custom and do what our people in their parliament say. It may be some spirit of wisdom they have not known may save them even yet. Go then with your face turned towards that light that beats from fairyland, and that faintly illumines the dusk between sunset and early stars, and this shall guide you till you come to the frontier and have passed the fields we know.”

===Chinese===
年轻人想起了她的美貌，与那顶冰之王冠，想起了古传符文中所说她身上那股至甘至美。野山上长着细小的草莓，黄昏时分、初星之下，便有人歌咏她；倘若循声去寻那歌者，却空无一人。有时，只是将她的名字轻而又轻地一遍遍低唱。她的名字叫莉拉泽尔（Lirazel）。

她是魔法一族的公主。诸神曾遣他们的阴影去赴她的洗礼；精灵们本也想去，却在他们那沾满露珠的田野上，望见诸神投下的一道道幽长、黝暗、移动的阴影，心生畏惧，于是便藏身于一簇簇淡粉色的银莲花间，从那里为莉拉泽尔祈福。

「我的子民要求一位通晓魔法的领主来治理他们。他们选得愚蠢，」老领主说，「此事将招致何等后果，唯有那些不示真容的幽冥之神尽知；而我们这些看不清前路的人，唯有遵从古老的风俗，照议会所言去做。或许会有某种他们尚未识得的智慧之灵，终能拯救他们。那么你去罢——面朝那自精灵之境拍涌而来的光，它微微照亮日落与初星之间那片暮色，必能指引你，直到你抵达边境、越过我们所知的原野。」

===Original===
Then he unbuckled a strap and a girdle of leather and gave his huge sword to his son, saying: “This that has brought our family down the ages unto this day shall surely guard you always upon your journey, even though you fare beyond the fields we know.”

And the young man took it though he knew that no such sword could avail him.

Near the Castle of Erl there lived a lonely witch, on high land near the thunder, which used to roll in summer along the hills. There she dwelt by herself in a narrow cottage of thatch and roamed the high fields alone to gather the thunderbolts. Of these thunderbolts, that had no earthly forging, were made, with suitable runes, such weapons as had to parry unearthly dangers.

===Chinese===
随后他解开一根皮带与一条皮腰带，将自己的大剑交给儿子，说道：「这把剑护着我们一族穿越漫长的岁月，直到今日；它必能在你旅途上始终庇佑于你，纵使你此去远过我们所知的原野。」

年轻人接了过来，虽然他心里明白，这样的剑并不能助他成事。

厄尔城堡（the Castle of Erl）附近住着一位孤身的女巫，居于高处、临近雷霆之地——夏日里，雷声常沿着群山隆隆滚过。她独自栖身在一间狭小的茅屋里，独自在高原的田野上徘徊，以拾取雷石。这些雷石未经人世任何炉火锻冶；以它们为材，配以相宜的符文，便可打造出那种用以抵御非人间凶险的兵器。

===Original===
And alone would roam this witch at certain tides of spring, taking the form of a young girl in her beauty, singing among tall flowers in gardens of Erl. She would go at the hour when hawk-moths first pass from bell to bell. And of those few that had seen her was this son of the Lord of Erl. And though it was calamity to love her, though it rapt men’s thoughts away from all things true, yet the beauty of the form that was not hers had lured him to gaze at her with deep young eyes, till﻿—whether flattery or pity moved her, who knows that is mortal?﻿—she spared him whom her arts might well have destroyed and, changing instantly in that garden there, showed him the rightful form of a deadly witch. And even then his eyes did not at once forsake her, and in the moments that his glance still lingered upon that withered shape that haunted the hollyhocks he had her gratitude that may not be bought, nor won by any charms that Christians know. And she had beckoned to him and he had followed, and learned from her on her thunder-haunted hill that on the day of need a sword might be made of metals not sprung from Earth, with runes along it that would waft away, certainly any thrust of earthly sword, and except for three master-runes could thwart the weapons of Elfland.

As he took his father’s sword the young man thought of the witch.

It was scarcely dark in the valley when he left the Castle of Erl, and went so swiftly up the witch’s hill that a dim light lingered yet on its highest heaths when he came near the cottage of the one that he sought, and found her burning bones at a fire in the open. To her he said that the day of his need was come. And she bade him gather thunderbolts in her garden, in the soft earth under her cabbages.

===Chinese===
每当春之潮汛至，这女巫又会独自游荡，化作一个美艳的少女，在厄尔花园高高的花丛间歌唱。她总在那种时分前去——正是天蛾初次从一朵钟形花飞向另一朵的时刻。曾见过她的人寥寥无几，而这位厄尔领主之子便是其中之一。爱她本是灾祸，她会使人的心思尽离一切真实之物；然而那并不属于她的、借来的美貌，仍引得他用少年那深沉的目光凝望她，直到——是奉承还是怜悯打动了她？凡人谁能知晓？——她放过了一个本可被她的法术轻易毁灭的人，就在那花园里刹那间变了形相，让他看见一个致命女巫的真身。即便在那一刻，他的目光也并未立刻舍她而去；在那短短几瞬里，他的视线仍停留于那出没在蜀葵花间的枯槁身形之上，于是赢得了她的感激——那种感激非金钱可买，亦非凡间基督徒所知的任何法术所能求得。她向他招了招手，他便随她而去；在那雷声出没的山丘上，他从她口中得知：到了急需之日，可以不产于大地的金属铸成一柄剑，剑身遍刻符文，能拂开世间任何一柄凡剑的刺击；除却三道主符文之外，连精灵国的兵刃也能抵挡。

年轻人接过父亲的剑时，心中想起了那位女巫。

他离开厄尔城堡时，谷中天色尚未全暗；他疾步攀上女巫所居的山丘，赶到那要寻的人的茅屋近前时，最高的荒原上仍余着一抹朦胧的天光。他见她在露天的火堆旁焚烧骨头。他对她说，他急需之日已至。她便命他去她的园子里，在那甘蓝菜下松软的泥土中，拾取雷石。

===Original===
And there with eyes that saw every minute more dimly, and fingers that grew accustomed to the thunderbolts’ curious surfaces, he found before darkness came down on him seventeen: and these he heaped into a silken kerchief and carried back to the witch.

On the grass beside her he laid those strangers to Earth. From wonderful spaces they came to her magical garden, shaken by thunder from paths that we cannot tread; and though not in themselves containing magic were well adapted to carry what magic her runes could give. She laid the thighbone of a materialist down, and turned to those stormy wanderers. She arranged them in one straight row by the side of her fire. And over them then she toppled the burning logs and the embers, prodding them down with the ebon stick that is the sceptre of witches, until she had deeply covered those seventeen cousins of Earth that had visited us from their etherial home. She stepped back then from her fire and stretched out her hands, and suddenly blasted it with a frightful rune. The flames leaped up in amazement. And what had been but a lonely fire in the night, with no more mystery than pertains to all such fires, flared suddenly into a thing that wanderers feared.

As the green flames, stung by her runes, leaped up, and the heat of the fire grew intenser, she stepped backwards further and further, and merely uttered her runes a little louder the further she got from the fire. She bade Alveric pile on logs, dark logs of oak that lay there cumbering the heath; and at once, as he dropped them on, the heat licked them up; and the witch went on pronouncing her louder runes, and the flames danced wild and green; and down in the embers the seventeen, whose paths had once crossed Earth’s when they wandered free, knew heat again as great as they had known, even on that desperate ride that had brought them here. And when Alveric could no longer come near the fire, and the witch was some yards from it shouting her runes, the magical flames burned all the ashes away and that portent that flared on the hill as suddenly ceased, leaving only a circle that sullenly glowed on the ground, like the evil pool that glares where thermite has burst. And flat in the glow, all liquid still, lay the sword.

===Chinese===
在那里，他的双眼一刻比一刻看得更模糊，手指却渐渐习惯了雷石那奇异的表面；不等夜色完全压下来，他已拾得十七枚：他将这些堆进一方丝帕，带回给女巫。

他将这些大地的异客放在她身旁的草地上。它们来自奇异的虚空，被雷霆从不为我们所能涉足的路径上震落，方才来到她这魔法的园中；它们本身虽不含魔法，却极宜于承载她符文所能赋予的法力。她放下一根唯物论者的大腿骨，转向这些风雨中的浪客。她把它们沿火堆边排成笔直的一行。随即她将燃烧的柴木与余烬倾覆其上，以那根乌木杖——亦即女巫的权杖——将其一一捣压下去，直到那十七枚从以太之乡造访我们的大地之表亲被深深掩埋。她自火边退后一步，伸出双手，猛然以一道可怖的符文轰击火堆。火焰惊骇地高高跃起。原本不过是夜里一炉孤寂的火，并无多于寻常篝火之秘，此刻却骤然化作一桩令夜行者心生畏惧之物。

被她符文所激的绿色火焰高高窜起，火堆的热力愈来愈烈，她便一步一步向后退去——离火愈远，她念诵符文的声音反倒愈加高亢。她命阿尔韦里克往火里添柴——用那些横七竖八堆在荒原上的乌黑橡木；他刚一把柴丢进去，热力便将其舔噬殆尽；女巫仍不停念诵着愈发响亮的符文，火焰狂野地舞作一片碧绿；而在余烬深处，那十七枚曾自由遨游、轨道曾与大地相交的浪客，又一次感到了炽热——一如它们当初被那趟绝望的飞坠送到此地时所感受过的那般炽热。待到阿尔韦里克再也无法靠近火堆、女巫也退到数步开外高声诵咒之时，那魔法之火已将所有灰烬烧尽，山丘上熊熊燃烧的奇观骤然止息，只留下地上一圈闷燃的红光，犹如铝热剂爆开处那汪灼灼发光的凶潭。而就在那红光之中，平平地、尚是一汪流液般地，横卧着那柄剑。

===Original===
The witch approached it and pared its edges with a sword that she drew from her thigh. Then she sat down beside it on the earth and sang to it while it cooled. Not like the runes that enraged the flames was the song she sang to the sword: she whose curses had blasted the fire till it shrivelled big logs of oak crooned now a melody like a wind in summer blowing from wild wood gardens that no man tended, down valleys loved once by children, now lost to them but for dreams, a song of such memories as lurk and hide along the edges of oblivion, now flashing from beautiful years a glimpse of some golden moment, now passing swiftly out of remembrance again, to go back to the shades of oblivion, and leaving on the mind those faintest traces of little shining feet which when dimly perceived by us are called regrets. She sang of old summer noons in the time of harebells: she sang on that high dark heath a song that seemed so full of mornings and evenings preserved with all their dews by her magical craft from days that had else been lost, that Alveric wondered of each small wandering wing, that her fire had lured from the dusk, if this were the ghost of some day lost to man, called up by the force of her song from times that were fairer. And all the while the unearthly metal grew harder. The white liquid stiffened and turned red. The glow of the red dwindled. And as it cooled it narrowed: little particles came together, little crevices closed: and as they closed they seized the air about them, and with the air they caught the witch’s rune, and gripped it and held it forever. And so it was it became a magical sword. And little magic there is in English woods, from the time of anemones to the falling of leaves, that was not in the sword. And little magic there is in southern downs, that only sheep roam over and quiet shepherds, that the sword had not too. And there was scent of thyme in it and sight of lilac, and the chorus of birds that sings before dawn in April, and the deep proud splendour of rhododendrons, and the litheness and laughter of streams, and miles and miles of may. And by the time the sword was black it was all enchanted with magic.

Nobody can tell you about that sword all that there is to be told of it; for those that know of those paths of Space on which its metals once floated, till Earth caught them one by one as she sailed past on her orbit, have little time to waste on such things as magic, and so cannot tell you how the sword was made, and those who know whence poetry is, and the need that man has for song, or know any one of the fifty branches of magic, have little time to waste on such things as science, and so cannot tell you whence its ingredients came. Enough that it was once beyond our Earth and was now here amongst our mundane stones; that it was once but as those stones, and now had something in it such as soft music has; let those that can define it.

And now the witch drew the black blade forth by the hilt, which was thick and on one side rounded, for she had cut a small groove in the soil below the hilt for this purpose, and began to sharpen both sides of the sword by rubbing them with a curious greenish stone, still singing over the sword an eerie song.

===Chinese===
女巫走上前去，用一柄从腿侧拔出的剑削整剑缘。随后她在剑旁席地而坐，一面唱着歌，一面等它冷却。不似那激怒火焰的符文，她唱给剑听的是另一支歌：她那曾将火轰击至使粗大的橡木干瘪卷缩的咒语，此刻却化作低吟的旋律，有如夏日里一阵风，自无人照料的野林花园吹来，掠过那些孩子们曾经钟爱、如今唯有在梦中方能寻回的溪谷——一支关于种种记忆的歌，这些记忆潜藏、隐匿于遗忘的边缘：时而自美好的年岁里闪出一抹金色时光的吉光片羽，时而又匆匆退出忆念，归于遗忘的幽翳，只在心底留下那最最隐约的、小小一双闪亮足印的痕迹——这等痕迹当我们依稀察觉时，便唤作追悔。她歌咏着风铃草时节那些古老的夏日正午：她在这高耸幽暗的荒原上唱着，那歌里仿佛盛满了无数个清晨与黄昏，连同它们所有的露水，都被她的法术从本将失落的岁月里一一挽留了下来——以至于阿尔韦里克不禁揣想，她那火从暮色里诱来的每一片小小的游翅，会不会都是人类某个失落之日的幽魂，被她歌声的力量从更美的年代里唤回。这一切之间，那非人间的金属愈来愈硬。白色的流液凝定，转为红色。红色的辉光渐渐敛去。随着它冷却，它也收窄：细小的微粒彼此靠拢，细微的裂缝合拢：而合拢之际，它们攫住了周围的空气，连空气一并捉住的，还有女巫的符文；它紧紧攥住，永世不放。这柄剑便如此成了一柄魔法之剑。自银莲花初开直至落叶纷飞，英格兰林间所有的些许魔法，无一不在剑中。在南方的丘陵草原上——那里只有羊群与沉默的牧人出没——所有的些许魔法，这柄剑也无不具备。剑中有百里香的气息，有紫丁香的倩影，有四月破晓前众鸟的合唱，有杜鹃花那深沉而骄矜的华彩，有溪流的轻盈与欢笑，还有绵延无尽的五月山楂花。待到剑身转黑，它已浑身皆是魔法。

关于这柄剑，没有人能将其中应有尽有之事向你和盘托出；因为那些通晓它的金属曾在其上漂浮的太空轨道的人——直到大地在其轨道上航行而过，将它们一一截获——他们无暇在魔法这类事情上虚掷光阴，故而无法告诉你这剑是如何铸成的；而那些知晓诗从何来、知晓人对歌曲有何需要的人，或通晓魔法五十分支中任何一支的人，又无暇在科学这类事情上虚掷光阴，故而无法告诉你它的成分从何而来。只需知道：它曾一度在我们的大地之外，如今却在此处，与我们这些尘世的顽石为伍；它曾一度不过如那些顽石一般，而如今内里已有了某种宛若轻柔乐音的东西——会定义的人，尽去定义罢。

此时女巫握住剑柄，将黑色的剑身拔起——那剑柄粗厚，一侧浑圆；她为便于此举，早在剑柄下方的泥土里掘出一道小槽。她开始用一块奇异的青绿石头，磨擦剑身两侧以开其锋，口中仍对剑唱着一支可怖的歌。

===Original===
Alveric watched her in silence, wondering, not counting time; it may have been for moments, it may have been while the stars went far on their courses. Suddenly she was finished. She stood up with the sword lying on both her hands. She stretched it out curtly to Alveric; he took it, she turned away; and there was a look in her eyes as though she would have kept that sword, or kept Alveric. He turned to pour out his thanks, but she was gone.

He rapped on the door of the dark house; he called “Witch, Witch” along the lonely heath, till children heard on far farms and were terrified. Then he turned home, and that was best for him.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克默然望着她，心中暗暗称奇，不计光阴；也许不过片刻，也许众星已在它们的轨道上行过了老远。她猝然完工。她双手平托着剑站起身来，简慢地将剑朝阿尔韦里克一伸；他接了过来，她便转身而去——她眼中有一抹神情，仿佛她本想留下那柄剑，又或者，留下阿尔韦里克。他转过身想倾吐一番谢意，而她已不见踪影。

他叩响那黑屋的门；他在孤寂的荒原上高声呼唤「女巫，女巫」，直喊得远处农庄的孩子听见、心生惊恐。于是他踏上归途——那于他最为相宜。

## XIV The Quest for the Elfin Mountains / 十四 探寻精灵山脉

===Original===
Winter descended on Erl and gripped the forest, holding the small twigs stiff and still: in the valley it silenced the stream; and in the fields of the oxen the grass was brittle as earthenware, and the breath of the beasts went up like the smoke of encampments. And Orion still went to the woods whenever Oth would take him, and sometimes he went with Threl. When he went with Oth the wood was full of the glamour of the beasts that Oth hunted, and the splendour of the great stags seemed to haunt the gloom of far hollows; but when he went with Threl a mystery haunted the wood, so that one could not say what creature might not appear, nor what haunted and hid by every enormous bole. What beasts there were in the wood even Threl did not know: many kinds fell to his subtlety, but who knew if these were all?

===Chinese===
冬日降临厄尔，攫住了森林，使细枝僵直不动：在山谷里它噤住了溪流；在牡牛的田野间，青草脆如陶片，牲口的呼吸升起，一如营帐的炊烟。奥利安仍时常随奥斯进林子去，只要奥斯肯带他去，有时也随斯瑞尔同去。随奥斯去时，林间满是奥斯所猎之兽的魔力，巨鹿的华美似常盘桓于远处幽谷的阴翳之中；而随斯瑞尔去时，一种神秘盘桓于林间，使人无从断言会有什么生灵不将现身，也无从断言每一根巨大的树干旁有何物潜藏匿伏。林中究竟有何等走兽，便连斯瑞尔也不尽知：许多种类在他的精明之下应手而毙，但谁又知道这些是否便是全部？

===Original===
And when the boy was late in the wood, on happy evenings, he would always hear as the sun went blazing down, rank on rank of the elfin horns blowing far away eastwards in the chill of the coming dusk, very far and faint, like reveillé heard in dreams. From beyond the woods they sounded, all those ringing horns, from beyond the downs, far over the furthest curve of them; and he knew them for the silver horns of Elfland. In all other ways he was human, and but for his power to hear those horns of Elfland, whose music rings but a yard beyond human hearing, and his knowledge of what they were; but for these two things he was as yet not more than a human child.

And how the horns of Elfland blew over the barrier of twilight, to be heard by any ear in the fields we know, I cannot understand; yet Tennyson speaks of them as heard “faintly blowing” even in these fields of ours, and I believe that by accepting all that the poets say while duly inspired our errors will be fewest. So, though science may deny or confirm it, Tennyson’s line shall guide me here.

===Chinese===
到了愉快的傍晚，当那孩子耽留在林中迟迟未归时，每逢落日熊熊西沉，他总会听见一排又一排的精灵号角，在将临之暮的寒意里远远地向东方吹响，极其遥远而微弱，恍如梦中所闻的起床号。那些嘹亮的号角从林子那一边传来，从丘陵那一边传来，远远越过丘陵最远的弧线；他知道那是精灵国的银号。在其余一切方面他都是个人间的孩子，若不是他能听见那些精灵国的号角——其乐音只在人力听觉一码开外之处回响——又知道那是什么；除却这两样，他至今也不过是一个人间孩童而已。

至于精灵国的号角如何能吹越那暮色的屏障，竟让我们所知原野上的任何耳朵听闻，我实难索解；然而丁尼生（Tennyson）曾言，即便在我们这些原野之上，亦能听见它们「隐隐吹响」，而我深信：凡诗人正当灵感沛然之际所言，我们尽皆信受，则过失最少。故而，尽管科学或可否认或证实，丁尼生的这一行诗将在此指引我。

===Original===
Alveric in those days went through the village of Erl, with his thoughts far from there, moodily; and he stopped at many doors, and spoke and planned, with his eyes always fixed as it seemed on things no one else could see. He was brooding on far horizons, and the last, over which was Elfland. And from house to house he gathered a little band of men.

It was Alveric’s dream to find the frontier further north, to travel on over the fields we know, always searching new horizons, till he came to some place from which Elfland had not ebbed; to this he determined to dedicate his days.

===Chinese===
那段时日，阿尔韦里克穿行于厄尔的村中，心绪郁郁，心思却远在彼方；他在许多人家门前驻足，与人言谈、与人筹谋，而双目总似凝视着旁人所不能见之物。他久久凝思着远方的地平线，以及那最后一道地平线——精灵国就在那一边。于是他从一家到一家，渐渐聚拢了一小队人马。

阿尔韦里克的梦想，是到更北的地方去寻找那道边境，一路行过我们所知的原野，不断搜寻新的地平线，直到他抵达某处精灵国尚未退却之地；他决意将毕生岁月奉献于此。

===Original===
When Lirazel was with him amongst the fields we know, his thoughts had ever been to make her more earthly; but now that she was gone the thoughts of his own mind were becoming daily more elvish, and folk began to look sideways at his fantastic mien. Dreaming always of Elfland and of elvish things he gathered horses and provender and made for his little band so huge a store of provisions that those who saw it wondered. Many men he asked to be of that curious band, and few would go with him to haunt horizons, when they heard whither he went. And the first that he found to be of that band was a lad that was crossed in love; and then a young shepherd, well used to lonely spaces; then one that had heard a curious song that someone sang one evening: it had set his thoughts roving away to impossible lands, and so he was well content to follow his fancies. One huge full moon one summer had shone all a warm night long on a lad as he lay in the hay, and after that he had guessed or seen things that he said the moon showed him: whatever they were none else saw any such things in Erl: he also joined Alveric’s band as soon as he asked him. It was many days before Alveric found these four; and more he could not find but a lad that was quite witless, and he took him to tend the horses, for he understood horses well, and they understood him, though no human man or woman could make him out at all, except his mother, who wept when Alveric had his promise to go; for she said that he was the prop and support of her age, and knew what storms would come and when the swallows would fly, and what colours the flowers would come up from seeds she sowed in her garden, and where the spiders would build their webs, and the ancient fables of flies: she wept and said that there would be more things lost by his going than ever folk guessed in Erl. But Alveric took him away: many go thus.

===Chinese===
从前莉拉泽尔还在、与他同处我们所知的原野之间时，他的心思总在于使她更近人间；可如今她既已离去，他自己的心念却一日比一日愈发精灵化，乡民们也开始侧目而视他那古怪的形容。他日夜梦想着精灵国与精灵之事，备下马匹与草料，为他那一小队人马攒下了那般庞大的粮秣储备，见者无不称奇。他邀了许多人加入那支奇异的队伍，可人们一旦听说他要往何处去，肯随他去追逐地平线的便寥寥无几。他所寻得的头一个，是一个失恋的少年；其次是一个年轻的牧羊人，惯于独处空旷之地；再是一个某日傍晚听人唱过一首奇异的歌的人：那歌使他心神飘荡，飞往不可能的国土，于是他乐于追随自己的遐想。还有一个少年，某个夏天一轮巨大圆满的月亮曾整夜温煦地照着他卧在干草之间，自那以后他便猜度或望见了一些他说是月亮展示给他看的事物：那些事物究竟为何，厄尔再无第二人见过这般东西：阿尔韦里克一开口相邀，他便也加入了。阿尔韦里克费了许多时日才寻得这四人；此外他再也寻不到别人，只有一个全然痴傻的小伙子，他便收下他来照管马匹——因他虽痴傻，却深谙马性，马也懂得他，只是人间男女无一能把他揣度分明，唯有他的母亲除外；当阿尔韦里克取得他随行的诺言时，她哭了；因为她说他是她暮年的依靠与支柱，能预知风暴何时将至、燕子何时起飞，能预知她园中所播之花种将抽出何色花朵，能预知蜘蛛将于何处结网，以及苍蝇的那些古老传闻：她哭着说，他这一去所将失去的事物，远非厄尔之人所能揣度。然而阿尔韦里克还是带走了他：许多人便是这样走的。

===Original===
And one morning six horses heaped and hung with provisions all round their saddles waited at Alveric’s gateway, with the five men that were to roam with him as far as the world’s edge. He had taken long counsel with Ziroonderel, but she said that no magic of hers had power to charm Elfland or to cross the dread will of its King; he therefore commended Orion to her care, knowing well that though hers was but simple or earthly magic, yet no magic likely to cross the fields we know, nor curse nor rune directed against his boy, would be able to thwart her spell; and for himself he trusted to the fortune that waits at the end of long weary journeys. To Orion he spoke long, not knowing how long that journey might be before he again found Elfland, nor how easily he might return across the frontier of twilight. He asked the boy what he desired of life.

===Chinese===
于是在某个清晨，六匹马在阿尔韦里克的门前候着，鞍前鞍后堆满挂满了粮秣，随行的还有那五个要与他一同远游至天涯海角的人。他曾与齐龙德尔泽尔（Ziroonderel）久作计议，但她说她所有的法术皆无力蛊惑精灵国，也无力拂逆其国王那可畏的旨意；于是他将奥利安托付给她照看，因为他深知，她的法术虽只是寻常的、人间的法术，然而任何意图越过我们所知原野的法术、任何针对他孩子的诅咒或符文，都断难挫败她的咒语；至于他自己，他只信托那在漫长疲乏旅途尽头等候着的命运。他对奥利安叮嘱良久，不知此去要行多久方能重觅精灵国，亦不知自己将多么轻易——或多么艰难——地再越过那暮色的边境归来。他问孩子，他这一生有何心愿。

===Original===
“To be a hunter,” said he.

“What will you hunt while I am over the hills?” said his father.

“Stags, like Oth,” said Orion.

Alveric commended that sport, for he himself loved it.

“And some day I will go a long way over the hills and hunt stranger things,” said the boy.

“What kind of things,” asked Alveric. But the boy did not know.

His father suggested different kinds of beasts.

“No, stranger than them,” said Orion. “Stranger even than bears.”

“But what will they be?” asked his father.

“Magic things,” said the boy.

But the horses moved restlessly down below in the cold, so that there was no time for more idle talk, and Alveric said farewell to the witch and his son and strode away thinking little of the future, for all was too vague for thought.

===Chinese===
「做个猎人。」他说。

「我翻过山去的时候，你要猎些什么？」他父亲问。

「猎牡鹿，像奥斯那样。」奥利安说。

阿尔韦里克赞许这桩营生，因为他自己也喜爱它。

「有朝一日我还要走很远的路，翻过山去猎些更奇异的东西。」那孩子说。

「什么样的东西？」阿尔韦里克问。可孩子也说不上来。

父亲便举出好几种走兽来。

「不，比那些更奇异。」奥利安说，「比熊还要奇异。」

「可那会是些什么呢？」他父亲问。

「是些魔法之物。」那孩子说。

可底下的马在寒气中躁动不安，已没有工夫再作这般闲谈，阿尔韦里克便向女巫和儿子道别，大步走开，很少去想将来——因为一切都太渺茫，无从思量。

===Original===
Alveric mounted his horse over the heaps of provisions, and all the band of six men rode away. The villagers stood in the street to see them go. All knew their curious quest; and when all had saluted Alveric and all had called their farewells to the last of the riders, a hum of talk arose. And in the talk was contempt of Alveric’s quest, and pity, and ridicule; and sometimes affection spoke and sometimes scorn; yet in the hearts of all there was envy; for their reason mocked the lonely roving of that outlandish adventure, but their hearts would have gone.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克从粮垛之上翻身上马，一行六人尽皆策马而去。村民们立在街上目送他们启程。人人都知道他们那古怪的远征；待众人都向阿尔韦里克行了礼、都向那最后一个骑者喊过了道别的话，便起了一阵嘁嘁喳喳的议论。那议论里有对阿尔韦里克此行的轻蔑，有怜悯，也有讥嘲；有时是情谊在说话，有时是嘲弄；然而众人心里都怀着妒羡；因为他们的理智嗤笑那番异乡奇遇的孤身浪游，他们的心却恨不得随之同去。

===Original===
And away rode Alveric out of the village of Erl with his company of adventurers behind him; a moonstruck man, a madman, a lovesick lad, a shepherd boy and a poet. And Alveric made Vand, the young shepherd, the master of his encampment, for he deemed him to be the sanest amongst his following; but there were disputes at once as they rode, before they came to make any encampment; and Alveric, hearing or feeling the discontent of his men, learned that on such a quest as his it was not the sanest but the maddest that should be given authority. So he named Niv, the witless lad, the master of his encampment; and Niv served him well till a day that was far thence, and the moonstruck man stood by Niv, and all were content to do the bidding of Niv, and all honoured Alveric’s quest. And many men in numerous lands do saner things with less harmony.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克就这样策马出了厄尔的村庄，身后跟着他那一队冒险家：一个中了月魔的人、一个疯子、一个害相思的少年、一个牧羊童和一个诗人。阿尔韦里克任命年轻的牧羊人范德（Vand）为营地之长，因为他认其为随从中头脑最清醒的一个；可他们尚在骑行途中、还未及扎下任何营帐，便已起了争执；阿尔韦里克听见——或觉出——了手下人的不满，于是明白：像他这般的远征，当授予权柄的并非最清醒之人，而是最癫狂之人。他便命名那痴傻的少年尼夫（Niv）为营地之长；尼夫将他侍奉得很好，一直到距此甚远的某一日为止；那中了月魔的人侍立于尼夫之侧，众人都甘听尼夫的差遣，众人也都尊崇阿尔韦里克的远征。而在许多国度里，许多人做着的尽是更清醒之事，其和睦却远不及这一伙。

===Original===
They came to the uplands and rode over the fields, and rode till they came to the furthest hedges of men, and to the houses that they have built at the verge, beyond which even their thoughts refuse to fare. Through this line of houses at the edge of those fields, four or five in every mile, Alveric went with his queer company. The leather-worker’s hut was far to the south. Now he turned northward to ride past the backs of the houses, over fields through which once the barrier of twilight had run, till he should find some place where Elfland might seem not to have ebbed so far. He explained this to his men, and the leading spirits, Niv, and Zend who was moonstruck, applauded at once; and Thyl, the young dreamer of songs, said the scheme was a wise one too; and Vand was carried away by the keen zeal of these three; and it was all one to Rannok the lover. And they had not gone far along the backs of the houses when the red sun touched the horizon, and they hastened to make an encampment by what remained of the light of that short winter’s day. And Niv said they would build a palace like those of kings, and the idea fired Zend to work like three men, and Thyl helped eagerly; and they set up stakes and stretched blankets upon them and made a wall of brushwood, for they were but just outside the hedgerows, and Vand helped too with rough hurdles and Rannok toiled on wearily; and when all was finished Niv said that it was a palace. And Alveric went in and rested, while they lit a fire outside. And Vand cooked a meal for them all, which he did every day for himself upon lonely downs; and none could have cared for the horses better than Niv.

===Chinese===
他们来到高地，策马越过田野，直骑到人类最远的篱笆跟前，骑到他们建于边陲的那些屋子跟前——越过那些屋子，连他们的心思也拒不肯再往前去了。沿着田野边上这一线屋舍——每英里四五间——阿尔韦里克带着他那一队古怪的同伴穿了过去。那皮匠的茅屋远在南方。如今他折向北，从那些屋子的背后骑过，行过昔日暮色的屏障所曾流贯的田野，直到他寻得一处精灵国似乎未曾退却如此之远的地方。他把这番道理讲给手下人听，那几个领头的灵魂人物——尼夫，以及中了月魔的曾德（Zend）——立刻鼓掌喝彩；年轻的歌曲梦幻者锡尔（Thyl）也说这主意甚是高明；范德被这三人热烈的热忱所感染；至于害相思的兰诺克（Rannok），则全无所谓。他们沿着屋子的背后没走多远，红红的太阳已触及地平线，他们便趁着那短促冬日所余的天光赶紧扎营。尼夫说他们要造一座国王所居那样的宫殿，这个主意激得曾德干起活来顶得上三个人，锡尔也热切地来帮忙；他们立起木桩，在上头绷开毯子，又拢起一道柴枝编的墙——因为他们就在树篱外不远——范德也用粗陋的栅栏搭把手，兰诺克则疲惫地苦干着；待一切完工，尼夫便说这是一座宫殿。阿尔韦里克走进去歇息，他们便在外头生起一堆火。范德替众人做了一顿饭——他往常独自在孤丘之上，每日都是这般替自己操持；而要说照看马匹，再没人能比尼夫照看得更好了。

===Original===
And as the gloaming faded away the cold of winter grew; and by the time that the first star shone there seemed nothing in all the night but bitter cold, yet Alveric’s men lay down by their fire in their leathers and furs and slept, all but Rannok the lover.

To Alveric lying on furs in his shelter, watching red embers glowing beyond dark shapes of his men, the quest promised well: he would go far north watching every horizon for any sign of Elfland: he would go by the border of the fields we know, and always be near provisions: and if he got no glimpse of the pale-blue mountains he would go on till he found some field from which Elfland had not ebbed, and so come round behind them. And Niv and Zend and Thyl had all sworn to him that evening that before many days were gone they would surely all find Elfland. Upon this thought he slept.

===Chinese===
随着暮色一点点褪去，冬日的寒意愈深；待到第一颗星亮起，整夜的天地间似只剩刺骨的寒冷，然而阿尔韦里克的人裹着皮革与毛皮，卧在火堆旁睡了——只除了害相思的兰诺克。

阿尔韦里克躺在栖身之所的毛皮之上，望着手下人黝黑身影之外那红红的余烬闪烁，此番远征看来颇有指望：他要一路向北，每一道地平线都盯着有无精灵国的踪影：他要沿着我们所知原野的边境行进，时时都不远离粮秣：倘若望不见那淡蓝色的群山，他便继续前行，直到寻得某一片精灵国尚未退却的田野，从而绕到它们背后。那天傍晚，尼夫、曾德和锡尔都已向他起誓：不出几日，他们必定都能寻见精灵国。怀着这一念头，他入眠了。

## XIII The Reticence of the Leather-Worker / 十三 皮匠的缄默

===Original===
It was many days before Alveric learned from the monotony of the rocks that one day’s journey was the same as another, and that by no number of journeys would he bring any change to his rugged horizons, which were all drearily like the ones they replaced and never brought a view of the pale-blue mountains. He had gone, while his fortnight’s provisions grew lighter and lighter, for ten days over the rocks: it was now evening and Alveric understood at last that if he travelled further and failed soon to see the peaks of the Elfin Mountains he would starve. So he ate his supper sparingly in the darkness, his bundle of firewood having long since been used, and abandoned the hope that had led him. And as soon as there was any light at all to show him where the east was he ate a little of what he had saved from his supper, and started his long tramp back to the fields of men, over rocks that seemed all the harsher because his back was to Elfland. All that day he ate and drank little, and by nightfall he still had left full provisions for four more days.

He had hoped to travel faster during these last days, if he should have to turn back, because he would travel lighter: he had given no thought to the power of those monotonous rocks to weary and to depress with their desolation when the hope that had somewhat illumined their grimness was gone: he had thought little of turning back at all, till the tenth evening came and no pale-blue mountains, and he suddenly looked at his provisions. And all the monotony of his homeward journey was broken only by occasional fears that he might not be able to come to the fields we know.

===Chinese===
过了许多天，阿尔韦里克（Alveric）才从那片岩石的单调中明白：一天的旅程与另一天并无不同，无论走多少趟，也休想为他那崎岖的地平线带来任何改变——它们全都阴沉地与所替代的那些相仿，始终不曾带来那淡蓝色山脉的一瞥。他那十四日的干粮愈背愈轻，就这样在岩石上走了十天：如今已是傍晚，阿尔韦里克终于明白，若再往前走而仍不能很快望见精灵山脉（the Elfin Mountains）的群峰，他便会饿死。于是他在黑暗中省着吃过了晚饭——他那一捆柴早已用尽——并抛弃了那引他前来的希望。待到天光初现、足以向他指明东方所在，他便把晚饭里省下的一点食物吃了，开始他漫长的徒步折返，走回人类的田野；他背朝精灵国（Elfland），脚下的岩石似乎因此愈发显得严酷。那一整日他吃喝都很少，到夜幕降临时，他仍余下足足四日的口粮。

他原本打算在这些最后的日子里走得更快一些——若不得不折返的话——因为他将轻装而行：他却未曾想到，当那曾多少照亮过这些岩石之险恶的希望一旦消失，这些单调的岩石便以其荒凉之力教人疲倦、教人沮丧：他此前几乎未曾想过要回头，直到第十个傍晚来临、仍不见那淡蓝色的山脉，他才蓦然看向自己的口粮。而他归途上那一切单调，只被偶尔泛起的恐惧所打断——他怕自己再也到不了我们所知的那些原野（the fields we know）。

===Original===
The myriad rocks lay larger and thicker than tombstones and not so carefully shaped, yet the waste had the look of a graveyard stretching over the world with unrecording stones above nameless heads. Chilled by the bitter nights, guided by blazing sunsets, he went on through the morning mists and the empty noons and weary birdless evenings. More than a week went by since he had turned, and the last of his water was gone, and still he saw no sign of the fields we know, or anything more familiar than rocks that he seemed to remember and which would have misled him northward, southward, or eastward, were it not for the red November sun that he followed and sometimes some friendly star. And then at last, just as the darkness fell blackening that rocky multitude, there showed westward over the rocks, pale at first against remnants of sunset, but growing more and more orange, a window under one of the gables of man. Alveric rose and walked towards it till the rocks in the darkness and weariness overcame him and he lay down and slept; and the little yellow window shone into his dreams and made forms of hope as fair as any that came from Elfland.

The house that he saw in the morning when he woke seemed impossible to be the one whose tiny light had held out hope and help to him in the loneliness; it seemed now too plain and common. He recognized it for a house not far from the one of the leather-worker. Soon he came to a pool and drank. He came to a garden in which a woman was working early, and she asked him whence he had come. “From the east,” he said, and pointed, and she did not understand. And so he came again to the cottage from which he had started, to ask once more for hospitality from the old man who had housed him twice.

===Chinese===
无数岩石横陈着，比墓碑更大更厚，却又不曾雕琢得那么齐整，然而这片荒原看去就像一片墓地，铺展在世间，以无字的石覆于无名的坟头之上。他为苦寒之夜所侵，以熊熊燃烧的晚霞为引，穿过晨雾、空旷的正午和那疲倦而无鸟的傍晚，一路前行。自他转身以来已过了不止一周，他最后一口水也喝光了，却仍看不见我们所知的原野的一丝踪迹；眼前所见，除了那些他似乎记得、几乎要将他引人误向北、南或东的岩石外，再无更为熟悉之物——幸而有他追随的那轮红色的十一月太阳，有时还有某颗友善的星辰。终于，就在黑暗沉沉压下、将那一片嶙峋岩石尽数涂黑之际，西面岩石之上现出了一线光——那是一座人间山墙下的一扇窗户，起初在残存的晚霞映衬下淡淡的，渐渐越来越呈橘黄。阿尔韦里克起身朝它走去，直走到黑暗与疲倦借岩石之力将他压倒，他便倒下睡去；那一扇小黄窗照进了他的梦，化作与精灵国中所来的同样美好的希望之形。

清晨他醒来时所见的那座房子，竟难以令人相信就是那在孤独中曾以一点微光向他伸出希望与援手之处；如今看来它太平常、太普通了。他认出这是离皮匠（the leather-worker）家不远的一户人家。不久他来到一汪水潭边饮了个够。他来到一座园子，一位妇人正在其中趁早劳作，她问他从何而来。「从东方来。」他说着用手指了指，她却不明白。于是他又回到了他当初出发的那座茅舍，再次向那位曾两度收留他的老人求一份款待。

===Original===
He was standing in his doorway as Alveric came, walking wearily, and again he made him welcome. He gave him milk and then food. And Alveric ate, and then rested all the day: it was not till evening he spoke. But when he had eaten and rested and he was at the table again, and supper was now before him and there was light and warmth, he felt all at once the need of human speech. And then he poured out the story of that great journey over the land where the things of man ceased, and where yet no birds or little beasts had come, or even flowers, a chronicle of desolation. And the old man listened to the vivid words and said nothing, making some comments of his own only when Alveric spoke of the fields we know. He heard with politeness but said never a word of the land from which Elfland had ebbed. It was indeed as though all the land to the east were delusion, and as though Alveric had been restored from it or had awoken from dream, and were now among reasonably daily things, and there was nothing to say of the things of dream. Certainly never a word would the old man say in recognition of Elfland, or of anything eighty yards east of his cottage door. Then Alveric went to his bed and the old man sat alone till his fire was low, thinking of what he had heard and shaking his head. And all the next day Alveric rested there or walked in the old man’s autumn-smitten garden, and sometimes he tried again to speak with his host of his great journey in the desolate land, but got from him no admission that such lands were, checked always by his avoidance of the topic, as though to speak of these lands might bring them nearer.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克疲惫地走来时，老人正站在自家门廊里，便又一次将他迎入。他给他牛奶，又给他食物。阿尔韦里克吃过，便歇了一整日：直到傍晚他才开口。可待他吃饱歇足、再次坐到桌前，面前已摆上了晚饭，又有光亮与温暖，他忽然感到对人语之需。于是他倾吐出那场伟大旅程的故事——那越过人间万物终止之地、而鸟雀与走兽乃至花朵尚不曾到来的荒土之行，一部荒凉的纪事。老人听着那些生动的言辞，一言不发，唯有当阿尔韦里克说起我们所知的原野时，才偶尔自语几句。他以礼相听，但对那片精灵国已从中退去的土地，却绝口不置一词。情形确实仿佛东方所有那片土地都是一场虚妄，仿佛阿尔韦里克是从其中被救回、或从梦中醒来，如今已置身于寻常度日之物中间，至于梦中之物则无可言说。毫无疑问，老人绝不肯吐露一字以承认精灵国，或承认他那茅舍之门以东八十码外的任何事物。随后阿尔韦里克去睡了，老人独坐到炉火低暗，想着他所听闻的一切，摇着头。第二日整整一天，阿尔韦里克或在那里歇息，或在老人那被秋意所侵的园中踱步，有时他又试着与主人谈起他在那片荒土上的伟大旅程，却从他口中得不到一句承认那片土地是存在的——总是被他回避此话题所阻，仿佛一谈起那些土地便会将它们带得更近。

===Original===
And Alveric pondered on many reasons for this. Had the old man been to Elfland in his youth and seen something he greatly feared, perhaps barely escaping from death or an age-long love? Was Elfland a mystery too great to be troubled by human voices? Did these folk dwelling there at the edge of our world know well the unearthly beauty of all the glories of Elfland, and fear that even to speak of them might be a lure to draw them whither their resolution, barely perhaps, held them back? Or might a word said of the magical land bring it nearer, to make fantastic and elvish the fields we know? To all these ponderings of Alveric there was no answer.

And yet one more day Alveric rested, and after that he set out to return to Erl. He set out in the morning, and his host came with him out of his doorway, saying farewell and speaking of his journey home and of the affairs of Erl, which were food for gossip over many farmlands. And great was the contrast between the good man’s approval that he showed thus for the fields we know, over which Alveric journeyed now, and his disapproval for those other lands whither Alveric’s hopes still turned. And they parted, and the old man’s farewells dwindled, and then he turned back into his house, rubbing his hands contentedly as he slowly went, for he was glad to see one who had looked toward the fantastic lands turn now to a journey across the fields we know.

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克为此寻思了许多缘由。莫非老人年轻时曾去过精灵国，见过某件令他大为惊惧之事，或许九死一生、或逃过了一场绵延无尽的爱恋？莫非精灵国是一个过于浩大的奥秘，不堪为人声所惊扰？莫非这些居住在我们这世界边缘的人深知精灵国诸般荣耀那种超凡之美，唯恐即便提起它们，也会成为一道诱引，将他们引向那他们仅凭决心才勉强守住不去的方向？又或者，一句提及那片神奇之地的话，便会将它带得更近，使我们所知的原野变得奇幻而精灵化？阿尔韦里克这诸多思忖，皆无答案。

阿尔韦里克又歇了一日，之后便动身返回厄尔（Erl）。他清晨启程，主人随他走到门外道别，谈起他归家的路程，也谈起厄尔的种种事务——这些在许多农庄之间都是供人闲话的谈资。这位好老人对阿尔韦里克此刻所行的我们所知的原野所流露的赞许，与他对阿尔韦里克那份希望仍所向往的另一些土地所怀的非难，恰成鲜明的对照。两人就此作别，老人的道别声渐渐低去，随后他转身回屋，一边慢慢走一边满意地搓着手，因为他很高兴看到那曾向奇幻之地张望过的人，如今转身踏上穿越我们所知原野的旅程。

===Original===
In those fields the frost was master, and Alveric walked over the crisp grey grass and breathed the clear fresh air thinking little of his home or his son, but planning how even yet he might come to Elfland; for he thought that further north there might be a way, coming round perhaps behind the pale-blue mountains. That Elfland had ebbed too far for him to overtake it there he felt despairingly sure, but scarcely believed it had gone along the entire frontier of twilight, where Elfland touches Earth as far as poet has sung. Further north he might find the frontier, unmoved, lying sleepy with twilight, and come under the pale-blue mountains and see his wife again: full of these thoughts he went over the misty mellow fields.

And full of his dreams and plans about that phantasmal land he came in the afternoon to the woods that brood above Erl. He entered the wood, and deep though he was amongst thoughts that were far from there, he soon saw the smoke of a fire a little way off, rising grey among the dark oak-boles. He went towards it to see who was there, and there were his son and Ziroonderel warming their hands at the fire.

===Chinese===
在那些田野上，霜是主宰。阿尔韦里克踩着脆硬灰白的草地前行，呼吸着清冽新鲜的空气，几乎不去想他的家、也不去想他的儿子，只盘算着自己究竟还能如何再到精灵国去；因为他想更往北些或许有一条路，可以绕到那淡蓝色山脉的背后。精灵国已退得太远，他追之不及——这一点他绝望地确信；但他难以相信它会沿着整道暮色的边境（the frontier of twilight）尽数退去——在那道边境上，精灵国触及大地，远至诗人所歌咏之处。再往北他或许能找到那道尚未移动的边境，仍笼罩在暮色之中昏昏欲睡，从而来到那淡蓝色山脉之下，与妻子重逢：他满怀这些念头走过那薄雾迷蒙、柔和醇厚的田野。

满怀着关于那片幻影之地的种种梦想与计划，他在午后走到了那片笼罩于厄尔之上的林子。他走进林中，尽管深陷在远离此地的思绪之中，他很快便看见不远处一缕炊烟，在深色的橡树树干间灰灰地升起。他朝那里走去想看看是谁，却见他的儿子与齐龙德尔泽尔（Ziroonderel）正一同在火边烘手。

===Original===
“Where have you been?” called Orion as soon as he saw him.

“Upon a journey,” said Alveric.

“Oth is hunting,” Orion said, and he pointed in the direction whence the wind was fanning the smoke. And Ziroonderel said nothing, for she saw more in Alveric’s eyes than any questions of hers would have drawn from his tongue. Then Orion showed him a deerskin on which he was sitting. “Oth shot it,” he said.

There seemed to be a magic all round that fire of big logs quietly smouldering in the woods upon autumn’s discarded robe that lay brilliant there; and it was not the magic of Elfland, nor had Ziroonderel called it up with her wand: it was only a magic of the wood’s very own.

===Chinese===
「你到哪里去了？」奥利安（Orion）一看见他便喊道。

「出门远行了一趟。」阿尔韦里克说。

「奥斯（Oth）在打猎。」奥利安说着，朝风吹送炊烟而来的方向指了一指。齐龙德尔泽尔却一言不发，因为她从阿尔韦里克的眼中所见的，比她任何发问所能从他舌尖引出的都多。接着奥利安给他看自己正坐着的一张鹿皮。「奥斯射的。」他说。

那堆粗大木柴在林中静静地闷烧，置在秋所弃、光华灿烂的锦袍之上，那一圈火光四周仿佛流转着一种魔法；那不是精灵国的魔法，也不是齐龙德尔泽尔以魔杖所唤起：那不过是这片林子自家所有的一种魔法。

===Original===
And Alveric stood there for a while in silence, watching the boy and the witch by their fire in the woods, and understanding that the time was come when he must tell Orion things that were not clear to himself and that were puzzling him even now. Yet he did not speak of them then, but saying something of the affairs of Erl, turned and walked on toward his castle, while Ziroonderel and the boy came back later with Oth.

And Alveric commanded supper when he came to his gateway, and ate it alone in the great hall that there was in the Castle of Erl, and all the while he was pondering words to say. And then he went in the evening up to the nursery and told the boy how his mother was gone for a while to Elfland, to her father’s palace (which may only be told of in song). And, unheeding any words of Orion then, he held on with the brief tale that he had come to tell, and told how Elfland was gone.

“But that cannot be,” said Orion, “for I hear the horns of Elfland every day.”

“You can hear them?” Alveric said.

And the boy replied, “I hear them blowing at evening.”

===Chinese===
阿尔韦里克在那里默立了一会儿，看着那孩子与那女巫在林中他们的火边，明白那一刻已经到了：他必须告诉奥利安一些连他自己也还不甚清楚、即便此刻仍在困扰他的事情。然而他当时并未说出口，只是讲了些厄尔的琐事，便转身继续朝他的城堡走去；齐龙德尔泽尔与那孩子则是后来随奥斯一同回去的。

阿尔韦里克来到自己的门楼下时便吩咐开饭，独自在厄尔城堡（the Castle of Erl）的那座大厅里用了晚饭，整个时间都在斟酌着该说些什么。随后他在傍晚走上育婴室，告诉那孩子，他的母亲如何暂时去了精灵国，去了她父亲的宫殿（那座宫殿唯有歌诗方能传述）。他不理会奥利安当时所说的任何话，径自继续讲完他来所要讲的那段简短的故事，讲精灵国是如何离去了。

「但那不可能，」奥利安说，「因为我每天都听见精灵国的号角声。」

「你能听见？」阿尔韦里克说。

那孩子答道：「我听见它们在傍晚时分吹响。」

## XV The Retreat of the Elf King / 十五 精灵国王的退遁

===Original===
When Lirazel blew away with the splendid leaves they dropped one by one from their dance in the gleaming air, and ran on over fields for a while, and then gathered by hedgerows and rested; but Earth that pulls all things down had no hold on her, for the rune of the King of Elfland had crossed its borders, calling her home. So she rode carelessly the great northwest wind, looking down idly on the fields we know, as she swept over them homewards. No grip had Earth on her any longer at all; for with her weight (which is where Earth holds us) were gone all her earthly cares. She saw without grief old fields wherein she and Alveric walked once: they drifted by; she saw the houses of men: these also passed; and deep and dense and heavy with colour, she saw the border of Elfland.

===Chinese===
当莉拉泽尔随那些绚烂的落叶飘飞而去时，叶子们一个接一个地从它们在闪光空气中的舞蹈里落下，在田野上滚了一阵，便聚在树篱边歇息了；然而那将万物向下牵引的大地却抓不住她，因为精灵国王的符文已越过了它的疆界，唤她归家。于是她随意地驾驭着那股巨大的西北风，懒懒地俯瞰着我们所知的原野，一路飘回故乡。大地再也不能攫住她了；因为随着她的重量（大地便是凭重量留住我们）一同消散的，还有她所有尘世的牵挂。她无悲无喜地望见她与阿尔韦里克曾漫步其中的旧日田野：那些田野飘了过去；她望见人的房屋：这些也过去了；于是深浓厚重、色彩浓郁地，她望见了精灵国的边境。

===Original===
A last cry Earth called to her with many voices, a child shouting, rooks cawing, the dull lowing of cows, a slow cart heaving home; then she was into the dense barrier of twilight, and all Earth's sounds dimmed suddenly: she was through it and they ceased. Like a tired horse falling dead our northwest wind dropped at the frontier; for no winds blow in Elfland that roam over the fields we know. And Lirazel slanted slowly onward and down, till her feet were back again on the magical soil of her home. She saw full fair the peaks of the Elfin Mountains, and dark underneath them the forest that guarded the Elf King's throne. Above this forest were glimmering even now great spires in the elfin morning, which glows with more sparkling splendour than do our most dewy dawns, and never passes away.

===Chinese===
大地以种种嗓音向她发出最后一声呼唤，一个孩童的呼喊、寒鸦的哑啼、牛群沉闷的低哞、一辆慢车沉重地向家里拉去；随后她便进入了那稠密的暮色屏障，一切人世之声骤然黯淡下去：她穿了过去，那些声音便止息了。宛如一匹疲惫的马骤然倒地而亡，我们的西北风在边境上停了下来；因为在那漫游于我们所知原野之上的风，并不吹入精灵国。莉拉泽尔缓缓地斜向前行、向下落，直到她的双足又踏上了她故乡那片神奇的泥土。她清晰地望见了精灵山脉的群峰，以及峰下那片守护着精灵国王宝座的幽暗森林。在这片森林之上，即便此刻，仍有巨大的塔尖在精灵的晨光里闪烁——这晨光比我们最沾露水的黎明还要焕发出更璀璨的光华，而且永不逝去。

===Original===
Over the elfin land the elfin lady passed with her light feet, touching the grasses as thistledown touches them when it comes down to them and brushes their crests while a languid wind rolls it slowly over the fields we know. And all the elvish and fantastic things, and the curious aspect of the land, and the odd flowers and the haunted trees, and the ominous boding of magic that hung in the air, were all so full of memories of her home that she flung her arms about the first gnarled gnome-like trunk and kissed its wrinkled bark.

===Chinese===
这位精灵的贵妇以她轻盈的双足走过那片精灵的土地，轻触那些青草，一如蓟絮飘落到它们身上、拂过它们的草尖——当一阵慵懒的风缓缓地将它卷过我们所知的原野时那般。而所有那些精灵而奇异的事物、那土地上古怪的面貌、那些奇异的野花与栖有精魂的树木、那悬在空中的魔法的幽冥预感，都如此充满她故乡的回忆，以至于她一把抱住了第一棵多节而形似地精的树干，亲吻它那满是皱纹的树皮。

===Original===
And so she came to the enchanted wood; and the sinister pines that guarded it, with the watchful ivy leaning over their branches, bowed to Lirazel as she passed. Not a wonder in that wood, not a grim hint of magic, but brought back the past to her as though it had scarcely gone. It was, she felt, but yesterday morning that she had gone away; and it was yesterday morning still. As she passed through the wood the gashes of Alveric's sword were yet fresh and white on the trees.

===Chinese===
于是她来到了那片施过魔法的森林；守护着它的那些阴森的松树，连同俯在它们枝头的警觉的常春藤，在莉拉泽尔走过时都向她鞠躬。那林中每一桩奇景、每一缕魔法的森冷暗示，无不将往事唤回她心头，仿佛那些事才刚刚逝去。她觉得，她离去不过就是昨天早晨的事；而此刻仍是昨天早晨。她穿过森林时，阿尔韦里克之剑留下的刀痕在那些树上仍是崭新而发白的。

===Original===
And now a light began to glow through the wood, then flash upon flash of colours, and she knew they shone from the glory and splendour of flowers that girdled the lawns of her father. To these she came again; and her faint footprints that she had made as she left her father's palace, and wondered to see Alveric there, were not yet gone from the bended grass and the spiders' webs and the dew. There the great flowers glowed in the elfin light; while beyond them there twinkled and flashed, with the portal through which she had left it still open wide to the lawns, the palace that may not be told of but only in song. Thither Lirazel returned. And the Elf King, who heard by magic the tread of her soundless feet, was before his door to meet her.

===Chinese===
此时一道光开始透过森林发亮，继而一闪又一闪的斑斓色彩，她知道那是环绕她父亲草坪的那些花朵的荣光与华彩所发出的。她又来到了这些花前；她离开父亲宫殿时所留下的那浅浅的足印——那时她惊讶地看见阿尔韦里克在那里——还未曾从被踩弯的青草上、蜘蛛网上和露珠中消失。巨大的花朵在精灵之光里熠熠生辉；而在它们的那一边，闪烁辉耀着的，便是那座只可在歌谣中传唱、不可言说的宫殿——她当初离开它时所穿过的那道门，仍朝着草坪大开。莉拉泽尔便回到了那里。而精灵国王借魔法听见了她那无声双足的足音，已在他门前迎她。

===Original===
His great beard almost hid her as they embraced: he had sorrowed for her long through that elfin morning. He had wondered, despite his wisdom; he had feared, for all his runes; he had yearned for her as human hearts may yearn, for all that he was of magic stock dwelling beyond our fields. And now she was home again and the elfin morning brightened over leagues of Elfland with the old Elf King's joy, and even a glow was seen upon slopes of the Elfin Mountains.

===Chinese===
他们相拥时，他那把大胡须几乎将她整个人都遮住了：在那精灵的晨光中，他已为她悲伤许久。尽管他有智慧，也曾困惑；尽管他有符文，也曾忧惧；他渴慕她，一如人心之渴慕——尽管他本是居住在我们原野之外的魔法一族。如今她归家了，那精灵的晨光因老精灵国王的欢喜，在辽阔的精灵国上空更加明亮起来，连精灵山脉的坡上也可见一抹光辉。

===Original===
And through the flash and glimmer of the vast doorway they passed into the palace once more; the knight of the Elf King's guard saluted with his sword as they went, but dared not turn his head after Lirazel's beauty; they came again to the hall of the Elf King's throne, which is made of rainbows and ice; and the great King seated himself and took Lirazel on his knee; and a calm came down upon Elfland.

===Chinese===
他们穿过那道巨大门廊的流光溢彩，再次走入宫殿；精灵国王的卫队骑士在他们经过时举剑致敬，却不敢在莉拉泽尔的美貌之后回首；他们又来到了精灵国王宝座所在的殿堂，那宝座由彩虹与冰构成；伟大的国王坐了下来，将莉拉泽尔抱在膝上；于是宁静降临到了精灵国。

===Original===
And for long through the endless elfin morning nothing troubled that calm; Lirazel rested after the cares of Earth, the Elf King sat there keeping the deep content in his heart, the knight of the guard remained at the salute, his sword's point downwards still, the palace glowed and shone: it was like a scene in some deep pool beyond the sound of a city, with green reeds and gleaming fishes and myriads of tiny shells all shining in the twilight on deep water, which nothing has disturbed through all the long summer's day. And thus they rested beyond the fret of time, and the hours rested around them, as the little leaping waves of a cataract rest when the ice calms the stream: the serene blue peaks of the Elfin Mountains above them stood like unchanging dreams.

===Chinese===
在那无尽的精灵晨光中，许久都没有什么惊扰那份宁静；莉拉泽尔在尘世劳烦之后歇息着，精灵国王坐在那里将那份深深的满足护在心底，卫队的骑士仍保持着敬礼的姿态，剑尖仍朝下，宫殿辉耀闪光：那情景如同一座深潭中的景象，在城市的声响之外，有绿色的芦苇、闪烁的游鱼与无数细小的贝壳，尽在深水之上的暮色里闪闪发光，整个漫长的夏日里不曾有什么搅动它。他们便这样在时光的烦扰之外歇息着，时辰也在他们四周歇息，一如瀑布那跳跃的小小水波在冰雪使溪流平静时归于安息：他们头顶上精灵山脉那宁静的蓝色群峰，如同不变的梦境一般矗立着。

===Original===
